The expanded 170-case matrix covers direct/nested commands, aliases, root/nested defaults, empty tokens, flags before/between/after positionals, dynamic arrays/booleans, known-option interleaving, negative numbers, and separators. The handlers only record in-memory values. No destructive action or unintended remote execution is claimed.

### TC-050: advertised object defaults are not converted to MCP wire casing

- Surface: MCP input-schema discovery and default-driven invocation; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, `applySchemaCasing`.
- Reproduce in camel mode: declare optional `profile` with `S.Object({ display_name: S.String() }, { default: { display_name: "Ada" } })`. Discovery advertises `profile.properties.displayName` and requires `displayName`, but its default still contains `display_name`.
- Observed: the advertised default fails its own discovered profile schema. Calling the tool with no profile succeeds and delivers the canonical default to the handler; explicitly sending the advertised default fails with `-32602`, an unexpected canonical key, and a missing wire-cased key. A mirrored snake-mode fixture with canonical `displayName` reproduces the same mismatch.
- Expected: default metadata intended for clients to reuse should be expressed in the same key space as the surrounding wire schema. Server-side canonical default application must remain unchanged and must not mutate the author's schema/default object.
- Cause: schema casing rewrote declared properties and required names, but spread `default` metadata unchanged. Actual result/event default normalization from TC-046 did not also change discovery annotations.
- Fix: retain the matching canonical schema while converting its JSON Schema representation and reuse output conversion on cloned default metadata. Nested objects, arrays, records, unions/discriminators, optionals, and their annotations are covered. Arbitrary record keys, opaque JSON, and allowed additional properties are not indiscriminately renamed. Injected discriminator properties remain in discovered branch schemas.
- Regression evidence: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp-default-metadata.test.ts` has 62 cases. The initial valid-default matrix failed 28 cases with 20 controls; all now pass, together with 12 nested-annotation cases and two scoped-default compatibility controls. Both discovery surfaces, result/event schemas, subscription schemas, explicit-default invocation, omission, canonical handler values, and unchanged author defaults are checked.
- Compatibility refinement: the first implementation tried to throw serializer validation errors during metadata generation. A new scoped-default control demonstrated that this would turn a previously usable command into a startup failure; removed that new validation gate. Discovery converts projected metadata, while omitted invocation retains established canonical default contents. Explicitly supplied projected defaults contain only visible fields. This is not a fix for mutated-default constraints (TC-039).
- Verification/status: locally fixed for casing/normalization of valid defaults. Maintained Toolcraft checks pass 65 files / 1,520 tests and package lint/typechecks; the selected build closure completes 18 builds and consumers pass 46 files / 975 tests. Rebuilt public API round trips pass snake/camel on Node 18.18.2 and 22.23.2. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-mcp-default-metadata.png`, an actual in-process protocol capture rather than an interactive CLI or network test. No external client or network was required. JSON Schema annotations are not claimed to insert defaults automatically. A separate upstream discriminator-injection defect is TC-051.

### TC-051: discriminated branch annotations omit the generated required tag

- Surface: shared `toJsonSchema` output and MCP discovery; source: `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/index.ts`, `withInjectedDiscriminator`.
- Reproduce: build `branch = S.Object({ title: S.String() }, { default: { title: "Ada" } })`, then `S.OneOf({ discriminator: "kind", branches: { text: branch } })`. The authored branch/default validates. The generated branch schema requires `title` and `kind` but still advertises `{ title: "Ada" }` as its default.
- Observed: the generated branch default fails its own branch schema. A real initialized MCP session advertises the same incomplete annotation; sending it as the choice value returns `-32602`, `Missing discriminator "choice.kind". Expected one of: text.` Manually adding `kind: "text"` succeeds, and only that corrected call executes the handler.
- Expected: if branch-local defaults are retained on the discriminated schema, they should include the tag required by the transformed branch. Do not mutate the author's branch/default or invent a parent-level choice default.
- Cause: discriminator injection augments properties and required names but leaves the inherited branch-default annotation untouched. This occurs with single-word keys and directly in shared JSON Schema generation, independently of MCP casing (TC-050).
- Fix: inject the authoritative tag into a fresh branch-default object during shared JSON Schema generation. MCP metadata conversion uses that transformed default in the enclosing discriminator context so the tag is also correctly cased. No parent-level choice default is invented, and original branch/default objects remain unchanged. Null branch defaults are omitted from the required-object branch representation under TC-052.
- Evidence/status: locally fixed. Shared validation/compiler controls and real in-process discovery/invocation assertions first reproduced the issue without disk fixtures, external services, or handler side effects from the rejected call. The initial combined branch/default/nullability regression matrix failed 28 cases with six controls. Expanded coverage now passes 18 shared-schema and 28 MCP cases, including ordinary and stream discovery/results, authoritative tags, null/default boundaries, both casing modes, and unchanged source descriptors. Package checks pass 2,321 schema and 1,548 Toolcraft tests plus both packages' lint/typechecks; broader verification is recorded in session 25.

### TC-052: generated composition nullability does not match runtime

- Surface: shared `toJsonSchema`, MCP discovery, result validation, and event-schema conformance; sources: `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/index.ts`, composition branches/metadata, and `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, schema casing.
- Reproduce the false-positive case: place `S.Object({ title: S.String() }, { nullable: true, default: null })` inside a nonnullable `S.OneOf`. Shared runtime validation rejects `null` because a discriminated value must be an object, but generated JSON Schema retains the branch's `nullable: true` and accepts `null`. A one-branch ordinary union has the same mismatch.
- Reproduce the false-negative case: make the parent union/discriminator nullable, with two nullable object branches. Runtime validation accepts `null` at the parent, while the generated exact-one composition rejects it because both child branches match. With nonnullable branches, no child matches instead. Parent `nullable` metadata does not by itself bypass the generated composition's applicator checks.
- Expected: only the parent schema determines whether the composed value can be null. Object branches must still describe objects; branch-local null defaults must not imply an untagged or implicitly selected choice. Direct standalone nullable object schemas remain nullable.
- Fix: normalize generated object branches by removing branch-level nullability and null-default annotations. Emit one explicit `{ enum: [null] }` alternative when the parent composition is nullable. MCP casing preserves this generated alternative without treating it as an authored object branch. Retained object defaults and discriminator injection remain covered by TC-051.
- Regression evidence: one/two-branch union and discriminator matrices compare canonical and generated validation, verify parent nullability independently of branch nullability, and retain standalone nullable-object controls. Real MCP ordinary/stream calls cover omitted null defaults and explicit null, discovered/result/event schemas, and no handler execution on rejected nonnullable inputs. Extending the matrix exposed 14 RED cases after the initial tag fix; all 46 current shared/MCP metadata cases pass.
- Status/boundary: locally fixed for generated union/discriminator nullability, with package checks and broader-verification status under session 25. No general claim is made about unrelated schema keywords, arbitrary external JSON Schema implementations, or mutation of defaults after descriptor construction.

### TC-053: failed stream notifications escape their background owner

- Surface: MCP stream subscriptions with an asynchronous notification listener; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, the subscription-local notification queue, status callback, and background pump.
- Reproduce: use a real initialized `createMessageSession` whose listener rejects when receiving a selected `status`, `data`, `end`, or `error` stream notification. Subscribe to a synthetic finite stream that emits the corresponding event; for the error path, have the producer throw.
- Observed: all four subscription requests return success, followed by an unhandled rejection carrying the synthetic sink failure. Status/data failure only attempts that notification; the error-report notification is chained to the already rejected queue and never reaches the listener. Finite-stream cleanup still runs exactly once, so this is not reported as a demonstrated cleanup omission for every sink failure.
- Process-level evidence: a rebuilt public-API child without an unhandled-rejection observer exits zero with a successful sink and exits one with a rejecting data sink. The child error names the injected sink failure. No production outage, remote disconnection, or default transport behavior is assumed from this embedding-level reproduction.
- Pending-producer evidence: an abort-responsive producer emits status and waits on a deferred value. After the status listener rejects, the probe observes an unhandled rejection, an unaborted producer signal, and zero cleanup calls. Manually releasing the deferred value allows the pump to encounter its rejected queue, abort, and clean up once. A failed status sink therefore does not promptly cancel even this cooperative producer.
- Shutdown-race evidence: begin an asynchronous status delivery, close the session, and let the abort-responsive producer clean up. Reject the already-started delivery afterward. Even with cleanup complete, the status queue still reports an unhandled rejection. The regression must observe late failures from in-flight work, not only skip new sends after closure.
- Expected: the component that launches background delivery must own its rejection and terminate the subscription on delivery failure, including failures before another data event. It must not depend on a new event to discover a broken queue or retry an error notification through a permanently rejected chain. Preserve normal delivery ordering, explicit unsubscribe behavior, and observable diagnostics without manufacturing a successful delivery.
- Fix: the notification queue owns delivery failures, marks the subscription terminal, removes it from the active map, and immediately requests producer cancellation. Later queued sends are skipped. Fire-and-forget status work and cleanup promises are observed; producer errors are not retried through a broken notification sink. An active-session failure emits one generic diagnostic containing the subscription and notification kind, without copying potentially private sink-error text. A synchronously throwing diagnostic callback cannot resurrect an unhandled background rejection.
- Shutdown behavior: new queued sends are skipped after session closure, while rejection of delivery already in flight is still observed. Expected closure races do not add active-session failure diagnostics. Explicitly awaited unsubscribe cleanup failures retain their typed response instead of being swallowed by the new background ownership.
- Evidence/status: locally fixed; verification details are in session 27. The original source/API probes use bounded in-memory deferreds and synthetic failures; all sessions are closed and pending producers explicitly released. The new combined notification suite initially failed 20 cases with three controls; it now has 24 cases including the later explicit-unsubscribe control. This is distinct from TC-045's abort/startup cleanup ownership and TC-046's event serialization. The implementation was changed only after session 25's full chain became terminal.

### TC-054: blank numeric CLI input silently becomes zero

- Surface: scalar numeric CLI options; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `parseScalarValue`.
- Reproduce: declare a required `count: S.Number()` parameter, record the handler input, and invoke `check --count ""`, `check --count "   "`, or pass a newline-only value through the explicit argv API.
- Observed: each invocation exits zero and executes the handler with `{ count: 0 }`. This occurs because `Number(value)` converts blank strings to zero before numeric-schema validation. An empty shell-variable expansion can therefore become a valid but unintended numeric operation rather than an input error.
- Expected: a supplied numeric token should contain a numeric value. Reject empty/whitespace-only tokens before handler execution; retain explicitly supplied zero and supported padded numeric forms rather than forbidding all whitespace indiscriminately.
- Controls: explicit `"0"` succeeds with zero; `" 2 "` succeeds with two; `"not-a-number"` exits one without invoking the handler. Direct SDK input `{ count: "" }` rejects. CLI strings normally require numeric coercion, so the defect is specifically blank-token acceptance, not a claim that SDK and CLI accept identical raw types.
- Fix: reject empty/whitespace-only strings in the common scalar-number parser before accepting their coerced value. Keep explicit zero, padded numbers, negatives, decimals, exponent/hex forms, nullable values, and numeric constraints intact. Numeric-array flag splitting retains its existing empty-array behavior; no indiscriminate trim-to-missing rule was added.
- Evidence/status: locally fixed with TC-056's positional presence correction. `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-scalar-input.test.ts` has 118 cases; the meaningful RED run failed 45 cases with 73 controls before either code change. Coverage spans separate/inline flags, nested fields, dynamic records, required/defaulted/variadic positionals, empty-string/enum values, real omissions, and numeric compatibility controls. Source CLI/SDK probes recorded exit codes and exact handler calls without filesystem or network effects. The initial manual probe's unsupported output option and the test fixture's initially disabled `--yes` control were corrected before using their results as product evidence.
- Verification: maintained Toolcraft checks pass 68 files / 1,686 tests plus package lint/typechecks. The selected build completes 18 builds. Rebuilt CLI/SDK/MCP controls pass on Node 18.18.2 and 22.23.2. Inspected actual scalar CLI output and the maintained root-help screenshot; details are in session 28. The earlier full repository and packaging checks are not relabeled as runs of this newer patch.

### TC-055: session-scoped notification dispatch ignores closure

- Surface: Toolcraft queued stream notifications and the underlying custom-method notification context; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, subscription queue, and `packages/tiny-stdio-mcp-server/src/server.ts`, custom-method `session.notify`.
- Stream reproduction: emit a status and data value. Hold status delivery on a deferred promise so data waits behind it, then close the session and release the first delivery. The listener receives status before closure and subsequently receives data after closure. This uses a successful listener; it is distinct from TC-053's rejection ownership.
- Underlying reproduction: capture a custom method's session context, close its real message session, assert that the context signal is aborted, then call `context.notify`. The closed session's listener still receives the notification. This confirms the missing lifecycle guard below Toolcraft rather than assuming the stream queue is the only cause.
- Expected: already-started delivery may need to settle, but a new listener invocation must not start on a closed session. Preserve notifications for healthy sibling sessions and observe any eventual failure of work that was already in flight.
- Cause: the context checks initialization readiness and listener presence, but not the aborted lifecycle. Closing removes registry membership and aborts the signal without preventing the captured notification context from dispatching again. The stream queue schedules future sends without its own closure check.
- Fix: the underlying custom-method notification context checks the session's aborted lifecycle before invoking its listener. The Toolcraft queue also checks closure before starting a scheduled send. Direct caller-owned failures from already-started delivery remain observable; the Toolcraft background queue owns its own corresponding failures under TC-053.
- Evidence/status: locally fixed; verification details are in session 27. Four underlying session-context regressions cover closed/healthy contexts, active synchronous/asynchronous errors, and a pending rejection after close. A real Toolcraft queue regression covers data held behind status at closure. Original probes used bounded deferreds or direct in-process calls; sessions were closed and deferred delivery released. No remote connection, production impact, or ability to revoke a listener call already in progress is claimed.

### TC-056: explicit empty scalar positionals are mistaken for omission

- Surface: CLI positional resolution; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `resolveParams`.
- Reproduce: declare `positional: ["value"]` and `value: S.String({ default: "seed" })`. Enable the `yes` control and invoke `check --yes ""`. The original handler receives `"seed"`, not the explicitly supplied empty string. A required plain string instead reports missing input; an optional string loses the supplied value. An enum that explicitly permits `""` also rejects it as missing.
- Constraint bypass variant: a defaulted number, boolean, or minimum-length string can substitute its default for the empty token rather than validate what the caller supplied. This is separate from Number's blank-to-zero coercion (TC-054).
- Expected: presence is determined by whether a positional value exists, not whether its string length is positive. Preserve valid empty strings/enums, reject invalid empty scalar values, and apply defaults only for genuine omission under the existing CLI controls.
- Fix: process every supplied string positional, including `""`; remove the positive-length presence gate. The shared scalar parser and existing constraints decide whether that value is valid. Numeric empty values now reject through TC-054's parser guard.
- Evidence/status: locally fixed. Six empty-string/enum/default-constraint regressions and a genuine-omission control are included in the 118-case CLI matrix; required/defaulted numeric and variadic controls broaden the boundary. Rebuilt CLI, SDK, and MCP probes agree on preserving explicit empty text and rejecting blank numeric values. The rich renderer displays empty text with its existing placeholder, so handler-value assertions—not the placeholder alone—establish preservation.

### TC-057: scalar-error presentation varies by argument form

- Surface: CLI error text; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `parseOptionFieldValue`, positional/dynamic parsing, and `formatCommanderErrorMessage`.
- Reproduce with the already-invalid token `"oops"`: a normal numeric flag emits `Invalid value for "count". Expected a number, got "oops".` The equivalent positional emits the same message prefixed by `error: `. A dynamic numeric record field also gains that prefix.
- Observed/controls: all invocations exit one, execute no handler, and include the appropriate usage pointer. The difference is presentation, not erroneous acceptance or a new failure caused by rejecting blank numbers. It is also visible for the new blank-input diagnostics in the scalar CLI screenshot.
- Cause: ordinary option conversion collects `InvalidArgumentError` as a normal validation issue, while positional/dynamic failures can reach the Commander-specific formatter, which adds a prefix. Argument shape leaks into user-facing diagnostic style despite the shared underlying validation rule.
- Remediation: scalar number/boolean/enum and JSON conversion now throw Toolcraft `UserError`, matching existing string-constraint validation. This fixes the error classification at the value-conversion boundary instead of globally rewriting Commander messages. Missing operands and global-control syntax errors retain their existing Commander formatting.
- Regression: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-validation-presentation.test.ts` initially has 24 failures / 64 controls. All 88 final cases cover ordinary/nested/dynamic flags, positionals, scalar array items, explicit blank values, valid handler payloads, contextual usage pointers, and six syntax/control-error controls.
- Verification: four focused files / 557 tests, all 94 Toolcraft files / 4,760 tests, package lint/types, and the selected 18-workspace build pass. Rebuilt public CLI checks pass 88 cases on each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (264 total). Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-validation-presentation.png`, captured from five real local CLI fixtures, all exiting one without executing their handlers.
- Status: locally fixed for the stated presentation inconsistency. No parsing-policy or security change is claimed. Full snapshot 75 predates this patch.

### TC-058: dynamic options contaminate positional parameters and cross `--`

- Surface: CLI argument binding; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `createNodeCommand`, `parseDynamicValues`, and `resolveParams`.
- Reproduce: declare scalar positional `target` plus `labels: S.Record(S.String())`, then run `check --labels.env prod sample`. Before the fix, the handler receives `target: "--labels.env"` instead of `"sample"`, while also receiving the parsed labels. Inline flags produce the same error. A variadic positional includes both the dynamic flag and its consumed value.
- Separator reproduction: with optional labels, `check -- --labels.env=literal` both sets the intended literal positional and unexpectedly creates `labels.env = "literal"`. When a dynamic option precedes the separator, the parser instead treats `--` itself as an unknown dynamic parameter and fails. These are input-boundary defects, not simply excess arguments.
- Cause: Commander retains unknown dynamic-option tokens in `command.args` and its positional callback values. Toolcraft then interprets those same tokens again as dynamic options, with no consumed-token partition or end-of-options boundary. An operand-only suffix whose separator was already removed by Commander is reparsed as flags.
- Fix: retain the public `parseOptions` result's unknown-token partition per command, pass only that partition to dynamic parsing, preserve true operands separately, then bind scalar/variadic fields from the final positional tokens. Dynamic parsing returns unconsumed positionals and stops at `--`. TC-049 shares this consumption boundary, but its distinct symptom is silently ignored extras rather than corrupted accepted parameters.
- Evidence: current-source red tests explicitly capture the wrong handler payloads, extra variadic values, and separator failures. All 170 expanded cases in `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-argument-consumption.test.ts` pass after the fix. No shell execution or remote service is involved.
- Boundary: dynamic array flags intentionally consume consecutive values until another option or `--`; this fix does not invent an implicit split between an array's values and later positionals. Use the separator when that boundary would otherwise be ambiguous.

### TC-059: dynamic schemas silently accept unknown short flags as positional values

- Surface: CLI option validation; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, dynamic-command `allowUnknownOption(true)` and `parseDynamicValues`.
- Reproduce: a command with scalar positional `target: S.String()` rejects `check -x` as an unknown option, without invoking the handler. Add only optional `labels: S.Record(S.String())` to that schema and repeat the same argv: it exits zero and invokes the handler with `{ target: "-x" }`, even though no `-x` option is declared.
- Controls: the dynamic-schema command rejects unknown `--unknown`. `check -- -x` intentionally preserves the literal `"-x"`, and a numeric positional accepts `-3`. A fix must distinguish unsupported shorthand flags from legitimate negative values and end-of-options operands.
- Expanded current-build matrix: 44 cases compare plain/dynamic schemas, string/number positionals, and 11 argument forms. Dynamic string positionals also silently accept undeclared clusters and attached forms such as `-xyz` and `-x=value`. Negative integers, decimals, and exponents (`-3`, `-.5`, `-1e3`) work for dynamic numeric positionals but plain numeric positionals reject them as unknown options. Bare `-` and `--` controls retain their literal behavior; numeric schemas still reject nonnumeric literals. This exposes both excessive shorthand acceptance and schema-dependent numeric-operand classification.
- Cause: unknown options must be tolerated initially for dynamic long names. The secondary parser validates `--` names but treats every other unconsumed token as an operand, including unknown shorthand options. TC-049's excess-count check cannot catch this when a positional slot is available.
- Status/evidence: five current-source in-memory probes assert exact exit codes, handler counts, and accepted values after the maintained build stage completed. No source was changed during the active full validation chain. This behavior already followed from the pre-fix dynamic parser's short-token skip and Commander's callback values; the TC-058 partition fix exposes it more clearly but does not intentionally authorize it.
- Remediation: preserve the distinction between unknown options and literal operands through shorthand validation, with tests for `-x`, combined/attached shorthand forms, negative numeric values, a bare dash, and `--`. Do not ban every leading dash or accept arbitrary unknown flags merely because the schema includes a record.
- Local fix: after declared-array ownership normalization, move only the leading numeric/ordinary portion of the unknown partition into operands, honoring a literal separator when encountered. Dynamic fallback now rejects unsupported shorthand while preserving numeric operands and values already consumed by options. Plain and dynamic schemas no longer differ merely because an unused record exists; no blanket unknown-option relaxation was added.
- Verification: 455 new cases and four focused files / 1,275 tests pass; all 84 Toolcraft files / 3,819 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and 18 selected builds pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 130 public CLI cases, including mixed dynamic/known values and repaired array/alias boundaries. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-shorthand-operands.png` and refreshed root help after its 70 uncached builds/bundle. Initial fixture corrections and exact red/green evidence are recorded in session 51. Status: locally fixed, not committed or released; full snapshot 46 predates this patch.

### TC-060: CLI ignores parameter scope and `requiredScopes: ["cli"]`

- Surface: CLI schema interpretation; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `collectFields` and callers, compared with `packages/toolcraft/src/schema-scope.ts`, `filterSchemaForScope`.
- Reproduce scope exclusion: define required `visible: S.String()` and `apiOnly: S.String({ scope: ["mcp", "sdk"] })`. The shared CLI projection contains only `visible`. Actual `check --visible ok` fails for missing `apiOnly`; supplying `--api-only hidden` instead executes the handler with that CLI-excluded field.
- Reproduce requiredness: define `requiredCli: S.Optional(S.String({ requiredScopes: ["cli"] }))`. The shared CLI projection promotes it to required, but the actual CLI accepts no arguments and invokes the handler with `{}`.
- Cause: CLI field collection starts from the original schema and derives optionality directly from wrappers. Unlike SDK and MCP parameter paths, it does not apply the shared surface projection or required-scope promotion. The public schema metadata explicitly includes `"cli"` among supported scope names.
- Expected: CLI execution and generated parameter descriptions use one consistent CLI-scoped schema, excluding other-surface fields and enforcing CLI-specific requiredness. Keep command-level visibility and authorization separate from field presentation/validation.
- Initial evidence/status: three current-source in-memory invocations assert exact handler values/counts and exit codes and print the contrasting shared CLI-projected JSON Schemas. Confirmed and open; no source change during that full validation. No authorization bypass is asserted. Session 121 subsequently extends interactive and input-file coverage without claiming a repair.
- Remediation: apply projection consistently across CLI help/argument collection and invocation, with tests for nested containers, optional/default behavior, variants, positional declarations that reference excluded fields, and exhausted branches. Avoid fixing only help while leaving execution on the original schema.
- Expanded current-build evidence: six additional public CLI/snapshot cases confirm that excluded optional defaults still reach handlers, excluded nested objects and record values remain accepted, CLI-required nested optionals remain omitted, and excluded discriminator branches remain selectable. `createCLICommandTreeSnapshot` likewise retains excluded options and marks CLI-required optionals as nonrequired. Each contrasts its actual handler payload/snapshot with the shared CLI projection. All values are synthetic; no authorization bypass or real-secret disclosure is claimed. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session52-cli-scope.log`.
- Session-121 current-runtime extension: eighteen scenarios across flags/real JSON presets, kebab/snake, ordinary/streaming commands, and exact Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2 produce 432 invocations. They confirm 264 admitted payloads invalid against the shared CLI projection and 96 rejections of valid projected inputs, with 72 healthy controls. Cases cover whole-field exclusion/defaults, nested objects, records and array items, promoted requiredness, excluded variant branches, selected-branch requirements and nonnullable exhausted variants. Snapshot fields/requiredness are recorded for every observation. Schema descriptors and preset bytes remain unchanged; owned temporary directories are removed. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session121-cli-scope-<version>-before.log`.
- Actual interactive extension: 24 PTY flows across the same runtimes and both command kinds show twelve faulty behaviors and twelve controls. CLI asks for an explicitly non-CLI required field and passes its answer to the handler; an optional wrapper with CLI-required metadata instead completes without asking and passes `{}`. Ordinary required fields prompt correctly and ordinary absent optionals remain absent. Every process naturally exits zero after asserting the observed handler payload against the projection. This verifies the defect, not a feature pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session121-interactive-results.log` and per-case terminal histories. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-scope-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-scope-interactive-before.png`; annotations are fixture observations, not product messages.
- Repair boundary: directly passing valid shared CLI-projected schemas to existing CLI APIs exposes an additional compatibility constraint. Ninety-six projected configurations cover both variant kinds, direct/optional/array/record placement, nullable/nonnullable exhaustion, and normal/streaming commands on three runtimes. Shared validation and compiled JSON Schema both accept the intended inputs. Twenty-four direct/optional null-only configurations nevertheless fail snapshot generation, invocation, root help and even an otherwise healthy sibling with `Synthetic enum schema requires at least one value.` Array/record null-only residuals and nonnullable exclusions are healthy controls. The 288 CLI calls and 96 snapshots demonstrate why merely inserting the projection call into field collection would be incomplete and could break unrelated commands. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session121-scope-residual-<version>-corrected.log`.
- Probe correction: the first residual matrix incorrectly assumes the healthy sibling stays executable. Actual terminal output disproves that assumption; the diagnostic log and initial failed runtime logs remain. The corrected matrix records the coupled failure rather than hiding it. No production patch is made, no valid null-only residual is removed, and TC-041's shared projection repair remains intact. A CLI repair must handle these representations alongside positional declarations and ordinary-container activation, not weaken the schema helper or patch only help output. TC-060 remains open.

### TC-061: HTTP resource-limit options are accepted but not forwarded

- Surfaces: `createHTTPMCPServer` and `runHTTPMCP`; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/http.ts`, `RunHTTPMCPOptions` and `createTransportOptions`.
- The public options inherit `maxSessionsPerSubject` and `maxQueuedToolCalls` from the underlying HTTP transport/server types, but the explicit forwarding object omits both. This is a current-code omission, not a guessed dependency API.
- Per-subject reproduction: start a loopback server with an explicit UUID session generator, `maxSessionsPerSubject: 1`, and a synthetic verifier mapping the bearer token to the subject. Two independent initializations for the same subject both return HTTP 200 with session IDs; another subject also succeeds. Expected: the second same-subject initialization is rejected with 429 while a different subject remains eligible.
- Queue reproduction: set `maxQueuedToolCalls: 0`, leave the underlying active capacity at its default four, initialize a stateless server, hold four command handlers on an in-memory promise, and send a fifth request. A direct `tiny-http-mcp-server` control rejects the fifth with `Too many queued tool calls` and invokes four handlers. Toolcraft instead queues it and invokes all five after release, returning five successful results. All requests use loopback and synthetic values; the probe waits for actual handler/start events rather than guessing elapsed delays.
- Invalid-option control: direct tiny HTTP construction rejects `-1` for either limit. Toolcraft construction accepts both. The first ad hoc cleanup attempted a nonexistent server-level `close`; that cleanup error is not a product finding. No listener was started for those invalid-option constructors, and that short-lived process exited. Socket probes close their listener handles in `finally`.
- Impact: applications cannot enforce the smaller admission budgets they configure through Toolcraft. Underlying defaults still impose their own limits; this is not evidence of unlimited admission, an authentication bypass, or production denial of service.
- Fix: forward both public admission limits in `createTransportOptions`. Tests verify actual per-subject rejection and capacity recovery after deletion, actual shared-server queue admission, and invalid-option rejection through both public entrypoints, rather than merely checking an object spread. The first queue probe omitted initialization and was terminated without being counted; the corrected initialized direct/Toolcraft comparison produced the evidence above.
- Verification: the combined option/default-mode matrix fails 27 of 31 cases before the fix, with four explicit session-mode controls passing. Expanded coverage totals 32 tests; all 73 Toolcraft files / 2,080 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 18-workspace build pass. Rebuilt Node 20.20.0 and 22.23.2 loopback probes verify same-subject limits, other-subject isolation, deleted-session capacity recovery, zero queued-call capacity under four held handlers, and invalid limits. Underlying defaults remain unchanged when callers omit these limits.

### TC-062: omitted HTTP session option disables the default stateful transport

- Surfaces: HTTP session initialization, persistent notifications, and Toolcraft stream delivery; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/http.ts`, `createTransportOptions`.
- Cause: the forwarding object always includes `sessionIdGenerator: options.sessionIdGenerator`. The underlying transport distinguishes an absent property (default UUID-backed stateful sessions) from an explicitly present `undefined` (stateless). Toolcraft erases that distinction.
- Four-way loopback reproduction: direct tiny HTTP with omitted configuration issues a session ID; Toolcraft with omitted configuration does not; Toolcraft with an explicit UUID generator issues one; Toolcraft with explicit `undefined` does not. All initialize requests return 200, so basic successful tool calls alone do not establish stateful behavior.
- Stream reproduction: define an MCP stream yielding `42`, initialize, open a GET SSE channel, and subscribe through `toolcraft/streams/subscribe`. With Toolcraft's omitted option, GET returns 405 while subscribe succeeds and the producer runs, but the client receives no data/end notifications. With an explicit generator, GET returns 200 and the same client receives the actual `data` event containing `42`, followed by `end`.
- Existing tests labelled stateful/stateless only verify tool discovery; their stateful fixture omits the option but never asserts a session ID. The streaming test only checks discovery and a subscription response, not delivered events. These are concrete coverage gaps rather than failed maintained tests.
- Expected: retain property absence so the underlying stateful default applies; explicit `undefined` must still request stateless behavior, including the hosted-OAuth override. Do not substitute a new UUID generator or change the hosted configuration contract.
- Fix: preserve the own-property absence of `sessionIdGenerator` instead of materializing an undefined property. Explicit undefined and explicit custom generators retain their existing meanings; the hosted-OAuth override still explicitly selects stateless mode. No replacement generator or new configuration option is introduced.
- Verification: the 32-case combined matrix includes both entrypoints, absent/explicit mode controls, and actual default-session SSE data/end receipt. All 2,080 Toolcraft tests, package lint/typechecks, and the selected build pass. Rebuilt Node 20.20.0/22.23.2 clients receive a default session ID and the actual `42` data event followed by end; explicit stateless mode still issues no session ID. Existing hosted-OAuth/authorization integration tests pass. No event delivery is claimed for stateless mode. The initial SSE probe looked for a nonexistent `data` payload property; it was corrected to parse the actual `event` field before recording the successful stateful control. Reconnection and notification-history coverage remain pending.

### TC-063: stateless HTTP subjects can cancel each other's Toolcraft streams

- Surfaces: authenticated stateless HTTP and `toolcraft/streams/unsubscribe`; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, subscription ownership checks, and the stateless shared message-handler path in `packages/tiny-http-mcp-server/src/http-transport.ts`.
- Reproduction: run Toolcraft with `sessionIdGenerator: undefined` and a synthetic bearer verifier assigning distinct subjects A and B. A subscribes to a stream whose producer waits on its cancellation signal. B submits A's returned subscription ID to unsubscribe. The response is `{ unsubscribed: true }`, and A's producer observes its signal abort.
- Stateful control: repeat with an explicit UUID generator and separate initialized sessions for A and B. B receives `{ unsubscribed: false }`, A's producer remains active, and A can subsequently unsubscribe it. The probe waits for actual producer start and records the abort flag; it does not infer cancellation from a success response alone. Both listener handles are closed after owner cleanup.
- Cause: Toolcraft associates subscriptions with `session.signal`. Stateful HTTP has distinct message-session signals, but stateless requests share the server's default message context across authenticated subjects. Comparing the signal does not establish subject ownership in that mode.
- Impact: an authenticated caller with another subscription's ID can cancel that caller's running stream. The reproduction uses a known returned ID, synthetic subjects, and an in-memory producer; it does not demonstrate data disclosure, token forgery, unauthorized ordinary tool calls, or cancellation across properly isolated stateful sessions.
- Relationship to TC-062: fixing the omitted-option default reduces accidental stateless exposure, but does not fix explicit stateless mode or the hosted-OAuth stateless override. Do not mark this finding fixed solely by preserving the default generator.
- Transport-contract follow-up: the upstream README explicitly documents stateless GET/DELETE as 405. A rebuilt loopback probe with `enableJsonResponse: false` also proves that an SSE-formatted stateless POST is not a persistent Toolcraft notification channel: subscribe returns one SSE envelope containing the subscription result and closes the response; the finite producer yields and finishes, but no data/end notification is delivered. Merely switching POST response format therefore does not repair this composition.
- Local fix: HTTP derives an internal streaming capability from the resolved session mode, including hosted OAuth's forced stateless override. MCP checks the exposed stream set after scope/allowlist projection and rejects unsupported compositions before calling the HTTP transport factory. The error names the streams and directs users to stateful HTTP, stdio, or the existing `tools` allowlist. Ordinary stateless tools, MCP-hidden streams, and explicitly excluded streams remain supported; changing the POST response format does not bypass the guard. This rejects unsupported streaming rather than claiming to provide persistent stateless notifications or adding a new public configuration flag.
- TDD: the initial corrected matrix had ten failing rejection tests and eight passing compatibility controls; the first draft's controls omitted MCP initialization and were corrected before treating its failures as product evidence. The expanded 23-case matrix passes, including nested/multiple roots, unprefixed names, group allowlists, both POST formats, hosted override, explicit/default stateful sessions, empty/ordinary allowlists, SDK-only streams, and stdio. Rejection cases assert that the HTTP factory and producer are not invoked. Hosted configuration preparation and proxy resolution can still precede this check; no broader side-effect-free-startup claim is made.
- Verification: all 76 Toolcraft files / 2,311 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 18-workspace build closure pass. Rebuilt public entrypoints on Node 20.20.0 and 22.23.2 each reject four explicit-stateless and two hosted streaming configurations; real authenticated loopback controls preserve ordinary stateless calls, stateful SSE event delivery, cross-subject unsubscribe denial, and owner cancellation. The only started producer is the supported stateful control. `docs/toolcraft-http.md` describes the supported contract; README files remain untouched. Full snapshot 35 predates this change, and no fresh full-repository or packaging pass is inferred.
- Status: locally fixed; not committed or released.

### TC-064: variant prompts bypass the configured CLI prompt streams

- Surface: `runCLI({ promptInput, promptOutput })`; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `enforceVariantConstraints` and its `promptForField` calls.
- Reproduction: provide an in-memory TTY-like `PassThrough` input and captured writable output, then supply `sample` followed by Return. A plain required `target: S.String()` displays its prompt on the supplied output and calls the handler with `{ target: "sample" }`.
- Change the schema to `payload: S.OneOf({ discriminator: "mode", branches: { safe: S.Object({ target: S.String() }) } })`, and provide `--payload.mode safe`. The required target prompt never appears on the supplied output, the supplied answer is not consumed, and the handler instead receives `{ payload: { mode: "safe", target: "" } }` with exit zero. The process's real stdin was at EOF in this probe; the variant prompt incorrectly read that channel. A string with no length constraint legitimately accepts empty text, but that does not justify ignoring the configured input channel.
- Cause: ordinary field resolution forwards `promptStreams` to `promptForField`; variant enforcement omits it for both deferred discriminator selection and selected-branch required fields, causing fallback to process stdin/stdout.
- Impact: embedded/custom-terminal callers cannot reliably route variant prompts and may execute with an answer from the wrong channel. The reproduction proves required text-field routing and the plain-field control; other prompt kinds need targeted tests before claiming the full scope fixed.
- Fix: thread the existing `PromptStreams` through `enforceVariantConstraints` and both deferred discriminator/selected-field prompt calls. No second prompt abstraction or public option is introduced.
- TDD: 48 cases in `cli-variant-prompt-streams.test.ts` initially fail 42 with six explicit-value controls passing. They cover text, enum, boolean, discriminator/union selection, provided/prompted/deferred selectors, nested object paths, cancellation decisions, and explicitly supplied values. All cases now pass, and all 75 Toolcraft files / 2,288 tests plus package lint/typechecks pass.
- Rebuilt verification: Node 18.18.2 and 22.23.2 each pass 12 actual interactive flows using the real prompt implementation over custom in-memory TTY streams. These combine prototype-shaped required fields with text/enum/boolean answers, including false, and both provided/deferred selectors. Input keypress and output resize listeners are removed and raw mode is reset after completion. `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-variant-prompts.png` and refreshed root help were inspected. This verifies custom-terminal routing, not every physical terminal environment.
- Boundary: prompt controls and their cancellation decisions use the configured streams. The separate terminal cancellation banner still bypasses custom output sinks in rich mode; TC-066 tracks that unchanged primitive rather than claiming all cancellation output fixed here.

### TC-065: inherited names bypass CLI variant required-field checks

- Surface: selected CLI variant validation; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `getNestedValue` and `enforceVariantConstraints`.
- Reproduction: declare `payload: S.OneOf({ discriminator: "mode", branches: { safe: S.Object({ constructor: S.String() }) } })`. Invoke `check --payload.mode safe` without the required branch field. CLI exits zero and invokes the handler with an object whose only own payload key is `mode`; there is no own `constructor` value.
- Four confirmed inherited-name cases: `constructor`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty`, and an explicitly authored own schema key `__proto__`. Each omission executes the handler without the required own field. An otherwise identical `target: S.String()` control correctly exits one, reports the missing selected-branch field, and never invokes the handler.
- Cause: `getNestedValue` reads `current[segment]` without checking ownership. Selected-branch enforcement treats any non-undefined result as present, including values inherited from `Object.prototype`. The normal field-binding stage treats variant branch fields as optional until this later enforcement step, so it does not catch the omission first.
- Impact: a user-facing validator permits handler execution with missing required data and inherited values of the wrong type. The probe does not mutate any prototype or demonstrate a remote exploit; ordinary built-in inherited names are sufficient.
- Fix: `getNestedValue` checks own-property membership at every object traversal step. Explicit prototype-shaped keys remain valid; values inherited from a prototype no longer satisfy field presence.
- TDD: 160 cases in `cli-variant-own-properties.test.ts` initially fail 64 with 96 controls passing. The matrix covers the four inherited names plus an ordinary key, string/number/array/record fields, discriminator and union branches, direct/nested object paths, and omitted/explicit values. All now pass. Explicit values retain own keys and the normal object prototype.
- Rebuilt verification: Node 18.18.2 and 22.23.2 each reject eight missing-own-property cases before handler execution. The same runtimes also pass the actual prompt integration controls recorded under TC-064, including a required `constructor` field that must be supplied rather than inherited. All 2,288 Toolcraft tests and package lint/typechecks pass; selected rebuild and maintained root-help preparation pass. `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-variant-own-properties.png` was inspected. This is input presence validation, not a claim of exhaustive prototype-pollution coverage across the repository.

### TC-066: rich cancellation bypasses custom output sinks

- Surface: plain and variant CLI prompt cancellation in rich mode; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `throwPromptCancellation`, and `packages/toolcraft-design/src/prompts/primitives/cancel.ts`.
- Reproduction: provide custom `promptInput`, `promptOutput`, and `outputEmitter`; cancel an actual text prompt by sending Ctrl+C through the supplied input. The prompt renders cancellation on the custom output and the configured emitter receives the normal cancellation error, but process stdout also receives `└  Operation cancelled.` and blank lines.
- Confirmed matrix: plain and selected-variant prompts both leak that terminal banner in rich mode. Matching JSON-mode controls emit no extra stdout bytes. All four cases exit one and never invoke the command handler; cancellation itself is effective.
- Cause: `throwPromptCancellation` calls the design `cancel` primitive, which writes directly to process stdout, before throwing the error that the CLI routes through its configured emitter. It does not use either supplied output destination.
- Impact: embedded/custom-terminal callers receive an extra message outside their chosen sinks and may show duplicate cancellation text. JSON corruption, handler execution, or remote protocol impact is not asserted by this reproduction.
- Local fix: removed the two redundant direct-stdout `cancel()` calls and their import from the CLI. Actual prompts still render their cancelled state on their configured output, while the existing user-error path emits the cancellation message once to the configured emitter or normal output. The standalone design primitive is unchanged for other callers. Existing cancellation tests now explicitly reject calls to the extra primitive.
- TDD: 38 cases cover text, enum, boolean, selected-variant and selector prompts, legacy confirmation, and missing-value choices across default/captured rich, JSON, and Markdown output. The authoritative red result is 14 failures / 24 passing controls; all 38 pass after the deletion. The first draft incorrectly expected the raw-message `outputEmitter` contract to return formatted JSON; that assertion was corrected before recording the red result. This patch deliberately preserves raw emitter semantics rather than changing output contracts.
- Verification: three focused files / 267 tests, all 77 Toolcraft files / 2,349 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 18-workspace build closure pass. The maintained help screenshot route completes its 70 uncached builds and bundle. Built Node 18.18.2 and 22.23.2 each pass 12 actual Ctrl+C cases using custom TTY streams: plain/variant × default/captured output × rich/JSON/Markdown. No handler executes, the cancellation message appears once, configured emitters leak zero stdout bytes, and keypress/resize listeners and raw mode are released. Default JSON remains parseable. The ordinary CLI usage hint is retained; an initial runtime assertion that omitted that existing hint was corrected, not treated as a product failure.
- Visual QA: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-cancellation-output.png` and refreshed `screenshots/help.png`. Cancellation state, the single error message, and retained usage hint are readable without overlap. Full snapshot 35 predates this patch; no latest-source full or fresh packaging pass is inferred.
- Status: locally fixed; not committed or released.

### TC-067: interactive enforcement prompts inactive nested variants

- Surface: nested CLI variant selection with interactive input; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `collectFields` and `enforceVariantConstraints`.
- Schema: `payload` is a discriminated union with selected branch `{ value: String }` and unused branch `{ nested: OneOf("mode", { extra: Object({}) }) }`. Invoke with all selected-branch arguments supplied: `--payload.kind selected --payload.value ready`.
- Actual interactive reproduction: the CLI still prompts for `--payload.nested.mode`, even though that field belongs only to the unused branch. Accepting `extra` invokes the handler with `{ payload: { kind: "selected", value: "ready", nested: { mode: "extra" } } }`. The handler receives data disallowed by the chosen branch, not merely an unnecessary prompt.
- Required-child variant: make the unused nested `extra` branch require `count: Number`. The CLI prompts for both the unused selector and count, then rejects the count it just collected as an unknown parameter of the selected outer branch. No handler runs in this case.
- Active-nesting follow-up: select an outer `chosen` branch containing an inner `left`/`right` discriminated object, then supply both selectors and the required `leftValue`. SDK accepts the complete valid object. Noninteractive CLI rejects it because the outer branch's flattened required-field list also demands `rightValue`. Interactive CLI prompts for that inactive grandchild; answering `unwanted` invokes the handler with both `leftValue` and `rightValue`, and the exact payload fails canonical validation. This independently confirms that parent required-field enforcement must not include descendants owned by another variant, even when the nested variant itself is active.
- Controls: both schemas execute correctly with `--yes`, never prompt, and pass only `{ payload: { kind: "selected", value: "ready" } }`. The four-case probe uses the rebuilt public CLI, real prompt implementations, in-memory terminal streams, exact handler payload assertions, and actual recorded prompt markers.
- Cause: nested variants are collected and enforced independently of their enclosing branch's active selection. Interactive deferred selection runs before optional/ancestor eligibility is established; the synthetic selection written during enforcement is not consistently counted as a supplied field when the outer branch is checked.
- Impact: fully specified valid interactive commands can require unrelated answers, fail because of those answers, or execute with invalid extra branch data. The issue is active-branch enforcement, separate from the now-fixed prompt stream routing and own-property presence checks.
- Fix: variant descriptors now carry their immediate owning branch and are collected parent-first. Enforcement tracks selected ancestors, ignores inactive descendants, and limits defaults/requirements to fields directly owned by each selected variant. Prompted selectors participate in explicit branch validation; explicit inactive inputs remain errors. Union controller fields remain synthetic and are not demanded as handler parameters.
- Verification: the new 106-case activation/default matrix starts with 88 failures and 18 passing controls, then passes alongside 308 existing default/own-property/prompt-routing controls. All 2,555 Toolcraft tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 18-workspace build pass. Node 18.18.0 and 22.23.2 each pass 24 real CLI scenarios across OneOf/Union nesting, supplied/deferred selectors, cancellation, and explicit wrong-branch inputs. Successful handler payloads pass canonical validation; no inactive prompts, descriptor mutation, or terminal listener/raw-mode leaks occur. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-active-variants.png`.
- Status: locally fixed for the reproduced branch-ownership failures. Tests cover required/optional variant wrappers; this does not claim exhaustive conditional ordinary-object semantics or shared branch-name support.

### TC-068: CLI binds inactive variant defaults and rejects shared branch fields

- Surface: CLI construction and argument/default binding; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, flat `collectFields` output, CLI flag uniqueness checks, and the field/default loops in `resolveParams`.
- Default reproduction: `payload` has discriminator `kind`, a `first` branch with `firstValue: String({ default: "first" })`, and a `second` branch with `secondValue: String({ default: "second" })`. Invoke `--payload.kind first --yes`. CLI exits zero and calls the handler with both `firstValue` and the inactive branch's `secondValue`.
- The exact resulting payload fails the canonical shared validator because `secondValue` is not allowed in the chosen branch. SDK and MCP calls selecting `first` apply only `firstValue` and return valid data. This does not require descriptor mutation or interactive prompts.
- Confirmed default matrix: scalar and array builder defaults, plus a valid record descriptor default. All three default values are validated independently before the probe; CLI injects each inactive value, while SDK/MCP controls do not. The record case uses `{ ...S.Record(S.String()), default: ... }`: `S.Record` has no options argument, so the initial ignored-options attempt was corrected and is not counted as a default reproduction.
- Cause: scalar and dynamic binding process fields from every branch before active-branch enforcement. Defaulted values are not added to the supplied-field set, so the later inactive-branch rejection does not catch the extra values.
- Related construction limitation: two mutually exclusive branches that both declare `value: String` are rejected as a conflicting `--payload.value` CLI flag, naming the same parameter twice. The corresponding SDK and MCP calls work. The constructor guard prevents the originally suspected same-key default overwrite; no overwrite or handler execution is claimed for these rejected shared-name definitions. This separate symptom shows that branch-local field namespaces are flattened during construction as well as binding.
- Impact: the high severity is based on handler execution with invalid inactive-branch defaults. Shared-name rejection is a developer-experience/parity limitation, not a demonstrated invalid invocation. Noninteractive default leakage is distinct from TC-067's deferred nested prompt ordering, although a coherent active-branch model should address both.
- Partial fix: scalar/dynamic defaults are no longer applied globally to branch-owned fields. Enforcement clones and applies only defaults directly owned by the selected active branch. The activation matrix verifies string, array, JSON, record, and object-array defaults across both variant kinds and three prompt modes, with repeated invocation isolation. Actual nested CLI payloads also validate on exact Node 18.18.0 and Node 22.23.2.
- Status: partially fixed, still counted open. The reproduced high-impact inactive-default leakage is repaired. Shared canonical flag names are still rejected at construction; registration guards remain intact. Supporting those names requires branch-specific typing/default binding, not simply disabling collision checks.

### TC-069: deprecated CLI confirmation ignores injected interactive streams

- Surface: the deprecated `confirm: true` command setting with `runCLI({ promptInput, promptOutput })`; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, the final `Proceed?` gate in command invocation.
- Reproduction: inject a TTY-like input/output pair while real process stdin is not a TTY. Define a command with `confirm: true` and a required string. The ordinary parameter prompt appears on the injected output and consumes `ready` from the injected input, but the CLI then invokes the handler without ever presenting `Proceed?`. Supplying `--target ready` directly also invokes the handler without confirmation.
- Controls: repeat both missing/supplied parameter cases with `confirm: false`; ordinary input collection and invocation behave identically. The four-case rebuilt probe records actual prompt text and handler calls. The confirmed failure is skipped confirmation with a custom TTY, not an inference from mocked prompt calls.
- Cause: ordinary prompting uses the resolved injected input's `isTTY`, but this final gate checks `process.stdin.isTTY` and calls `confirm()` without the configured streams. If the process does have a TTY, the latter omission also suggests wrong-channel prompting, but that second runtime scenario has not yet been reproduced.
- Boundary: README explicitly marks this setting as deprecated, CLI-only TTY confirmation and recommends `humanInLoop`. Noninteractive execution bypassing this legacy setting is not itself a new bug, and this finding does not demonstrate a bypass of human-in-loop authorization. The inconsistency is that the same invocation is considered interactive for parameter collection but not for its requested legacy confirmation.
- Fix: the legacy gate now reuses `shouldPrompt` and passes the configured prompt streams to `confirm()`. `--yes`, noninteractive input, disabled confirmation, and the separate human-in-loop path retain their previous intent.
- Verification: 72 deterministic cases start with nine failures / 63 controls, then pass. Three focused files / 291 tests, all 2,627 Toolcraft tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the 18-workspace build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0 and Node 22.23.2 each pass 24 actual terminal cases across simulated process TTY/pipe, supplied/default-prompted values, accept/decline/Ctrl+C, `--yes`, custom non-TTY input, and disabled confirmation. No process stdout/stderr writes, leaked keypress/resize listeners, or retained raw mode occur. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-confirmation-streams.png`.
- Status: locally fixed. This is a legacy CLI consistency repair, not a change to authorization semantics.

### TC-070: parity adapters do not start from equivalent filesystem state

- Surface: `CommandTestHarness.parity()` for filesystem-dependent commands; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/harness.ts`, which passes its shared `memoryFs`, and `testing/parity.ts`, which invokes SDK, MCP, and CLI sequentially with the same options.
- Reproduction: define an explicitly SDK/MCP/CLI-scoped command that reads `/counter.txt`, increments its numeric contents, writes them back, and returns `{ count }`. Seed the harness's in-memory filesystem with `"0"`, then invoke parity once. The adapters return `{ count: 1 }`, `{ count: 2 }`, and `{ count: 3 }`; `agree` is false and the supplied filesystem ends at `"3"`.
- Controls: invoke the real SDK, initialized MCP server, and CLI separately with independent in-memory filesystems seeded to `"0"`. All three return `{ count: 1 }`. The disagreement is caused by the harness changing the starting state between adapters, not by adapter behavior or schema conversion. No disk files, external service, credentials, or model calls are involved.
- Probe correction: the initial command omitted explicit MCP scope, so its MCP outcome correctly reported `SurfaceScopeError` while SDK/CLI advanced the counter. The authoritative three-adapter reproduction explicitly declares all scopes; the default-scope rejection is not a product finding.
- Impact: valid stateful commands fail parity assertions, and later harness calls observe the accumulated mutations from the comparison. This is separate from TC-014's parameter rewriting: it occurs with an empty argument object and identical field casing.
- Expanded dependency reproduction: a stateful injected service counter produces SDK/MCP/CLI counts 1/2/3 and `agree: false`; a stateful injected fetch returning the counter in JSON does the same. Stateless controls for both dependencies return 1/1/1 and agree. The four-case public harness probe uses an explicit shared result schema, no external network, and no filesystem writes. This confirms the shared-baseline limitation beyond the original filesystem example; it does not establish a universal reset implementation for arbitrary services or external effects.
- Boundary: do not promise generic cloning of arbitrary service instances or rollback of real external side effects. Equivalent independent fixtures need an explicit construction/reset contract; merely copying the options object does not isolate mutable closure state.
- Remediation constraint from source review: the harness also accepts a general `HandlerFs`; its adapter supplies a placeholder `snapshot: () => ({})`, not a real file inventory. Blindly cloning every harness filesystem from that method would silently erase external-adapter state. Keep the confirmed in-memory case distinct from external/reset semantics, and preserve binary buffers rather than assuming text snapshots are a lossless cloning interface. Session 128 verifies both boundaries on all three exact runtimes: a generic adapter can read its seeded file while snapshot returns an empty object; cloning a text snapshot changes a stored byte from base64 /w== to 77+9. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session128-filesystem-fork-boundary-<version>.log`. These are repair constraints, not newly counted findings or a claim that snapshot is intended as a binary-cloning API.
- Session-128 current-state reproduction, after full snapshot 127: six filesystem scenarios on Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2 cover increment, append, rename, unlink, conditional creation and read-only access, plus repeated counter comparisons. There are 117 handler invocations: 54 independent actual SDK/MCP/CLI baseline calls and 63 calls inside 21 parity comparisons. Eighteen comparisons falsely disagree; three read-only comparisons agree. The counter progresses through 1/2/3 and then 4/5/6, while each fresh adapter independently returns 1; rename/unlink leave later adapters missing the source file. Original seed objects remain unchanged, but the harness filesystem accumulates all effects. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session128-parity-filesystem-<version>.log`.
- Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-parity-filesystem-current.png`, rendered from actual independent controls, parity outcomes and parent filesystem snapshots. Full transcript: `/tmp/toolcraft-session128-parity-filesystem-transcript.log`. No runtime repair or fresh passing-parity claim is made.
- Status: confirmed, open. Each surface needs an equivalent initial filesystem snapshot, with explicit semantics for effects retained by the harness. Stateful external dependencies need a deliberate fixture/reset contract rather than silent shallow cloning.

### TC-071: the memory filesystem silently ignores write flags

- Surface: exported `createMemoryFs()` and the command harness's default filesystem; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/memory-fs.ts`, whose `writeFile()` unconditionally replaces the map entry and consults only the encoding option. The public `HandlerFs.writeFile` contract accepts `flag`, and the production filesystem adapter forwards it to Node's filesystem API.
- Reproduction: seed `/file.txt` with `original`, then write `new` using `flag: "wx"`. The test filesystem succeeds and overwrites the contents instead of rejecting an existing destination. Through `createCommandTestHarness.run()`, a command that reserves that path with an exclusive write returns successful `reserved` and records a write.
- Independent in-memory control: the same SDK command with a `memfs` filesystem rejects with `EEXIST` and retains `original`. The control does not create any disk file. It verifies a false passing command test, not just a difference in helper internals.
- Ten-case flag matrix: compare missing/existing files under `w`, `wx`, `a`, `ax`, and `r+` against independent `memfs` volumes. Five controls agree. Five cases disagree: existing `wx`/`ax` should reject with `EEXIST`; existing `a` should append `originalnew`; existing `r+` should preserve the unwritten suffix as `newginal`; and missing `r+` should reject with `ENOENT`. Toolcraft instead succeeds with `new` in each case.
- Extended investigation: 108 in-memory cases span 18 flag spellings, existing/missing files, and empty/ASCII/Unicode writes; 66 differ from `memfs`. That is a model comparison, not 108 native-conformance checks. Inspecting the results revealed that the installed `memfs` also permits nonempty writes through read-only `r`/`rs`/`sr` descriptors, so it is not a complete native oracle.
- Native control: 48 actual `fs/promises.writeFile` calls across exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 use only `/dev/null`, creating no file and modifying no persistent user data. Each runtime rejects nonempty read-only writes with `EBADF`, accepts empty read-only writes, and accepts `as`/`as+`, which are absent from the inspected `memfs` flag mapping. A repair must preserve byte-level update/append behavior and validate flags against native semantics rather than blindly copying that model. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session56-memory-flags.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session56-readonly-flags-native.log`.
- Flag-input follow-up: eight source-only memory cases and 24 more native `/dev/null` calls show that `rw`, `toString`, `constructor`, `__proto__`, and whitespace-padded flags should reject with `TypeError` / `ERR_INVALID_ARG_VALUE`, but the helper overwrites successfully. Native empty-string flags retain writeFile's default behavior; `sa`/`sa+` are valid synchronous-append aliases. Do not reject those valid controls in a repair. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session56-flag-input-{native,memory}.log`.
- Encoded-input boundary: 24 additional native `/dev/null` controls establish that read-only failure depends on encoded byte length, not input string length. Nonempty base64/hex strings decoding to zero bytes succeed without a write; decoded nonempty buffers reject with `EBADF`. Update-mode controls succeed in each case. All three exact runtimes agree. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session56-encoded-readonly-native.log`.
- Repair validation in progress: 173 flag tests produce 104 failures and 69 passing controls before the patch. The implementation now recognizes all 22 native string flags plus empty/default behavior, rejects invalid strings, checks existence/exclusivity, and appends or updates encoded bytes without truncating retained data. Read-only empty writes preserve contents. The new matrix plus existing filesystem tests passes 185 cases; the complete Toolcraft package passes 87 files / 4,114 tests. Separately, 69 native `/dev/null` cases on each exact runtime confirm valid flag spellings, exclusivity, and read-only behavior. Package lint/build and public runtime integration still need terminal verification before counting this issue as fixed.
- Impact: harness tests can approve incorrect overwrite protection, append behavior, existing-file preconditions, and invalid flag spellings. These are synthetic test-filesystem effects; no real file was overwritten and no production data loss is claimed. Permission modes and exhaustive native regular-file behavior remain unverified.
- Status: locally fixed. All 87 Toolcraft files / 4,114 tests, package lint/types, and the selected 18-workspace build pass. Each exact runtime passes 144 public flag cases, 96 independent `memfs` controls, and six actual harness-command cases. The control excludes `memfs`'s separately verified empty-flag, read-only, and synchronous-append incompatibilities rather than weakening native expectations. Failed operations retain contents and produce no successful filesystem-change record; successful empty read-only calls still appear in the call-based change log. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-memory-fs-flags.png` renders the actual harness outcomes through a CLI fixture. No native permission/directory emulation is claimed.

### TC-072: CLI defaults retain mutable state between invocations

- Surface: repeated `runCLI()` calls using one command definition; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, scalar/dynamic default assignment and the blank-input default return in `promptForField`.
- Reproduction: give an array parameter the default `["seed"]`; run the same command twice with `--yes`, and let its handler append `mutated` to the resolved array. The second handler begins with `["seed", "mutated"]`, and the descriptor's original default ends with two mutations.
- Confirmed matrix: array defaults, nested JSON defaults, and record-of-array descriptor defaults all leak the first handler's mutation into the second CLI invocation. Independent SDK fixtures begin with the original `seed` on both calls and leave their descriptor defaults unchanged. All six rebuilt cases pass their explicit reproduction/control assertions.
- Cause: CLI assigns `field.defaultValue` directly to handler parameters instead of using the shared default-cloning helper already used by SDK/MCP. Dynamic record defaults take a separate loop with the same aliasing. Interactive blank acceptance also returns the descriptor reference in current source, but that runtime path has not yet been reproduced.
- Relationship to TC-006: that finding and its fix explicitly cover SDK/MCP; the CLI has a separate default-binding implementation. This is not a regression of those fixed controls. TC-068 concerns defaults from inactive branches, whereas this issue occurs with ordinary non-variant parameters. TC-039's externally mutated invalid defaults remain a separate validation gap.
- Impact: state from a previous command handler changes later invocations and the declared default metadata. These are local synthetic values; no remote session or real-user data leakage is asserted.
- Local fix: apply the existing shared `cloneDefaultValue` helper at the scalar/default, dynamic/default, and blank-prompt/default binding points. No new cloning algorithm or dependency is introduced. Active-branch selection and validation of externally mutated defaults remain separate open issues; copying data does not establish either property.
- TDD: 100 cases cover array, nested JSON, record, and array-of-object defaults in plain/OneOf/Union placements, optional/required fields, interactive/noninteractive calls, explicit overrides, and falsy controls. The authoritative red result is 48 failures / 52 passes; all 100 pass after three binding changes. An initial fixture used the wrong required-key fingerprint for a union containing a defaulted non-optional field; its unexpected prompt caused test timeouts. The fingerprint was corrected and unexpected selector prompts now fail immediately. Those fixture failures are not counted as product evidence.
- Verification: 127 focused cases and all 78 Toolcraft files / 2,449 tests pass, with package ESLint/TypeScript and the selected 18-workspace build closure. The maintained help screenshot route completes 70 uncached builds and the bundle. Exact Node 18.18.0 and Node 22.23.2 each pass 24 built-API scenarios / 48 invocations across all four default kinds and three placements, including actual blank prompt acceptance; original metadata and nested array identities remain isolated. Keypress/resize listeners and raw mode are cleaned up.
- Visual QA: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-default-isolation.png` and refreshed `screenshots/help.png`. Both invocations visibly start with `seed`; mutation appears only in that invocation's result. Full snapshot 38 predates this change; later normal rebuilds are recorded separately.
- Session-91 extension: re-auditing dynamic object entries finds one remaining reference assignment in `finalizeDynamicValue` when an omitted object child has a default. Two record entries or object-array elements receive the same array/JSON/record/object default. Mutating the first entry changes the second entry in the same call, the descriptor, and later calls. This is the same default-isolation cause, not a new finding or a regression of the three previously repaired binding sites. A fresh-root public baseline runs 24 scenarios / 48 CLI/SDK/MCP calls; CLI reproduces sharing while SDK/MCP controls remain isolated. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-dynamic-default-public-red.log`.
- Session-91 repair/TDD: the remaining dynamic child-default assignment now calls the existing `cloneDefaultValue` helper. `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-dynamic-default-isolation.test.ts` adds 208 cases: four mutable default kinds, record/array/nested-record/nested-array collections, plain/OneOf/Union placements, optional/required child fields, explicit override controls, and falsy defaults. The red run has 96 failures / 112 controls; all 208 pass after the one-line repair. Three focused files have 414 passes, all 96 Toolcraft files / 5,112 tests pass, the selected 19-workspace build succeeds, and package ESLint/TypeScript pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-{dynamic-default-red,default-focused-green,build,package,lint}.log`.
- Session-91 native verification: each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes 72 public scenarios / 144 handler calls across CLI, SDK, and initialized MCP sessions: 432 calls total. This checks all four mutable kinds, record/object-array entries, three variant placements, repeated calls, sibling identity separation, and unchanged descriptors. Nested collections and optional/falsy/explicit variants are covered by the unit matrix rather than claimed as part of that native matrix. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-dynamic-default-native-green.log`.
- Session-91 visual/consumer verification: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-dynamic-default-isolation.png`, captured from two real record and two real object-array CLI invocations. Each call starts with independent `seed` values, mutation affects only the first entry, and the schema default stays unchanged. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-dynamic-default-{visual,transcript}.log`. Normal workspace build, all 1,054 OpenAPI and 130 codemode consumer tests, all 17 package-lint rules, 70 forced uncached root-help tasks plus bundle, and root types pass; refreshed help is inspected. This is focused/consumer/build verification through session 91, not a new full repository unit-gate pass.
- Status: locally fixed; not committed or released.

### TC-073: MCP tool admission requires an API absent from Node 20.0.0

- Surfaces: ordinary stdio/message-session and HTTP MCP `tools/call`; source: `packages/tiny-stdio-mcp-server/src/server.ts`, tool-call admission's unconditional `AbortSignal.any(...)`.
