- Declared compatibility: Toolcraft/schema/OpenAPI manifests require Node `>=18.18`, and the HTTP transport requires `>=20`. Installed exact Node 18.18.0 and 20.0.0 binaries through the existing nvm loader; both downloads passed its checksum check. These are test runtimes, not production-version recommendations.
- Reproduction: on Node 20.0.0, `typeof AbortSignal.any` is `undefined`. Initialize a real Toolcraft MCP message session and call an ordinary `check` tool. It returns `{ isError: true }` with `Error: AbortSignal.any is not a function` instead of invoking the handler. An authenticated stateless HTTP call with only that ordinary tool exposed fails the same way. This is independent of the now-rejected unsupported stateless stream composition.
- Control: exact Node 18.18.0 passes focused Unicode validation, SDK argument/default handling, custom MCP stream notifications, CLI own-key/cancellation routing, and OpenAPI path-boundary probes. Previously recorded newer Node 20/22 HTTP controls work. These checks do not establish complete minimum-runtime coverage.
- TDD: run the admission matrix both with its native API and with `AbortSignal.any` absent, adding repeated success/failure/timeout cleanup cases. Initial red: 13 failures / 15 passes. Timed-session cancellation controls extend that to 32 passing cases. A subsequent real Node 20.0.0 full-queue probe caught excessive listener warnings introduced by the first replacement; four bounded-listener tests failed against that intermediate patch. All final 36 cases pass after correcting it. The intermediate warning regression was fixed before delivery, not logged as a pre-existing issue.
- Local implementation: each lifecycle owns a single abort listener that forwards cancellation to its currently registered per-call controllers. Each admission keeps a separate signal, so filling the bounded queue does not add dozens of listeners to the session signal. Controllers are removed when responses settle and cleared on session abort. Timed requests reuse their admission controller for timeout cancellation. Expired queued calls cannot start, active capacity remains held until the handler settles, and response timers are cleared. No global polyfill, suppressed warning, new dependency, or raised engine minimum is introduced.
- Verification: 11 focused files / 829 tests cover the tiny server and HTTP consumers; focused ESLint/TypeScript and the selected 18-workspace closure pass. Rebuilt Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each complete 21 untimed and 21 timed queued calls with the configured single active slot and zero warnings. Toolcraft's ordinary MCP call succeeds on all three. Exact Node 20.0.0 also succeeds over real stateful and stateless HTTP with configured timeouts. Earlier guarded-stream/session/SSE/subject checks pass on that exact runtime as separately recorded.
- Probe boundary: admission capacities/timeouts belong to the underlying tiny server and HTTP options, not Toolcraft's ordinary `RunMCPOptions`. An initial queue probe mistakenly supplied those extra options to `createMCPServer`, observed its correct default concurrency of four, and was corrected to exercise the supported tiny-server API. Only the corrected probe is used for capacity/timing claims.
- Boundary: OpenAPI multipart downloading has a separate compatibility failure, subsequently reproduced as TC-074. This server change fixes ordinary MCP tool admission, not multipart preparation.
- Status: locally fixed for ordinary MCP tool admission; not committed or released. Full snapshot 38 predates this compatibility patch.

### TC-074: remote multipart preparation fails on an allowed Node runtime

- Surface: `toolcraft-openapi` multipart file preparation; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts`, `downloadMultipartSource`.
- Reproduction: call built `prepareMultipartFileInputs` with a remote HTTPS file, multipart mode/binary field metadata, injected in-memory filesystem/environment/fetch, and a live caller `AbortSignal`. Exact Node 20.0.0 throws `TypeError: AbortSignal.any is not a function` before the download fetch runs.
- Controls: the same remote input without a caller signal succeeds; local-file preparation succeeds with and without a signal. All four cases succeed on exact Node 18.18.0 and Node 22.23.2. The 12-case probe performs no real filesystem/network download and checks actual prepared base64/filename/content-type results.
- Cause: timeout/caller-signal composition unconditionally uses `AbortSignal.any`, which is absent in this allowed Node 20 runtime. The package advertises `node >=18.18`; this differs from TC-073's separately repaired MCP admission path.
- Impact: adding cancellation to an otherwise valid remote multipart input turns it into a runtime error on an allowed runtime. Ordinary request and local/base64 input behavior is not claimed broken.
- Fix: a download owns an AbortController, an unreferenced 30-second deadline, and one optional caller-abort listener. The listener forwards the caller's reason; `finally` clears the timer and removes the listener after fetch/redirect/body processing. No `AbortSignal.any`, global polyfill, dependency, or engine change is required.
- Verification: the final multipart matrix has 32 cases; three focused files / 111 tests and all 28 OpenAPI files / 716 tests pass. Focused ESLint, package TypeScript, and the maintained 19-workspace build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 12 built-API scenarios, including injected source controls and actual native fetch through a loopback mapping, redirect identity, caller cancellation, normal empty bodies, and zero remaining caller listeners. The deadline is exercised with deterministic fake timers, not a 30-second unit delay.
- Status: locally fixed. No production runtime upgrade recommendation is inferred from these old-runtime tests.

### TC-075: cancellation can become a successful empty multipart file

- Surface: remote multipart file preparation after response headers; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts`, the boundaries around fetch and body reading.
- Reproduction: a loopback HTTP server sends headers and a partial body without ending. An injected fetch maps a valid synthetic HTTPS source onto that server, uses the real runtime fetch with the provided signal, then aborts the caller immediately after headers arrive. On exact Node 18.18.0 and Node 20.0.0, preparation resolves with an empty base64 file despite its internal signal being aborted. Node 22.23.2 rejects the same case. The mapping is only a test fixture, not a relaxation of production source-URL validation.
- Controls: HTTP-error, pre-aborted, and pending-fetch cancellation paths reject on all three runtimes. Four deterministic regression cases model cancellation with a returned empty response and with a partially read stream that closes normally; all four fail before explicit cancellation checks are added.
- Cause: the adapter relies solely on fetch/body-read rejection. Older native fetch can expose EOF rather than a read error after cancellation, and the adapter then packages the empty bytes as a successful file. The first TC-074 compatibility repair did not correct this separately reproduced boundary failure.
- Impact: preparation reports success with lost file content after cancellation. No production upload or downstream handler execution is claimed; the concrete evidence is the wrong prepared multipart value.
- Fix: check the owned signal before and after fetch, and again after body consumption, before returning prepared bytes. A pre-aborted caller no longer reaches the injected fetch. Cancelled empty/partial EOF is rejected without rejecting valid empty responses.
- Verification: the four new EOF cases fail before the boundary checks and pass afterward; four additional normal-empty controls also pass. The final 32-case matrix, 716-test package snapshot, lint/types/build, and 12 real scenarios on each of Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 pass. All three runtimes now reject the previously empty-success cancellation case and remove caller listeners.
- Status: locally fixed; no commit, push, or release.

### TC-076: negative array-option values can become unrelated positionals

- Surface: declared CLI numeric array options combined with positional parameters; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, Commander variadic option registration, unknown-token partitioning, and later positional binding.
- Reproduction: declare `numbers: S.Array(S.Number())`, positional `target: S.Number()`, and an otherwise unused optional `labels: S.Record(S.String())`. Invoke `check --numbers -1 -2 --yes` without supplying a separate target. The handler runs with `{ numbers: [-1], target: -2 }`. `--numbers 1 -2` likewise invokes it with `{ numbers: [1], target: -2 }`.
- Controls: `--numbers 1 2` correctly reports the missing target. `--numbers=-1,-2` also reports the missing target, proving the negative array data itself is supported. With an explicit positional separator/target, `--numbers 1 2 -- 7` and `--numbers=-1,-2 -- 7` execute correctly; the space-separated negative forms instead reject `7` as an unexpected extra argument after rebinding `-2` to target.
- Plain-schema control: remove only the unused optional record. The space-separated negative forms now fail as unknown `-2` options rather than invoking the handler. The 16-case current-build matrix records exact handler payloads, exits, and errors across both schema forms. No external request or filesystem write occurs.
- Cause: Commander ends a known variadic option when a following token looks like an option, even when that token is a valid negative numeric element. The first required value can be negative, but subsequent negative values enter the unknown-token partition. Dynamic-schema fallback then treats those values as positionals, losing their association with the array option.
- Impact: a value intended for one parameter satisfies a different required parameter and permits handler execution with the wrong arguments. This is not merely an unfriendly error message or unsupported negative numbers; the comma-form control supports the same values correctly.
- Relationship: TC-058 repaired dynamic-option/positional token accounting, while this defect concerns declared variadic options. TC-059's shorthand/numeric-operand repair must preserve token ownership; globally reclassifying negative unknown tokens as operands would spread this rebinding bug to plain schemas.
- Related dynamic boundary: the fallback array parser used numeric schema constraints to identify option boundaries. A record of number arrays with `minimum: 0` accepts `--numbers.sample 1 -2` as array `[1]` and positional target `-2`, rather than rejecting the invalid array element. Integer arrays similarly rebind `-2.5`. Invalid negative first elements produce a missing-argument error; positive-leading comma forms correctly validate the same invalid data. Thirty route/constraint/position regressions reproduce 20 failures and 10 passing comma controls. This is the same ownership defect with a second parser cause, not an additional finding.
- Local fix: normalize subsequent negative elements of declared numeric arrays by repeating the registered flag, preserving native accumulation and ownership boundaries. Dynamic arrays now distinguish numeric lexical form from schema validity; out-of-bounds/fractional negative elements reach existing value validation rather than escaping into positional slots.
- Verification: 350 ownership regressions, two focused files / 520 tests, all 82 Toolcraft files / 3,064 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 18-workspace build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 36 final declared/dynamic scenarios. An earlier 60-case-per-runtime matrix covers aliases/default routes and native boundaries before the dynamic follow-up. Inspected actual declared/dynamic CLI screenshots; the maintained help screenshot/build also passes, with its exact chronology in session 49. Full snapshot 46 predates this patch. Status: locally fixed, not committed or released.

### TC-077: root bundling leaves standalone declaration imports unresolved

- Surface: local standalone Toolcraft packing and strict TypeScript consumption after the normal root build or root-help screenshot preparation. Sources: `scripts/bundle.mjs`, its calls to `rewriteWorkspaceDts` for every workspace, and `scripts/rewrite-workspace-dts.mjs`.
- Reproduction: after the maintained root/screenshot build, run `npm run smoke:toolcraft-sdk-types`. The packed, installed producer fails with `TS2307` for declaration imports such as `../../toolcraft-schema/dist/index.js`, `../../toolcraft-design/dist/index.js`, and `../../agent-mcp-config/dist/index.js`. The workspace files really contain these relative imports even though their source uses package specifiers.
- Cause: the root bundle rewrites workspace declaration specifiers into sibling paths for the root `poe-code` tarball layout. The same mutated workspace `dist` is subsequently packed as standalone `toolcraft`, whose dependency layout differs. A successful root build therefore does not leave a portable standalone declaration artifact.
- Independent control: run the maintained `npm run build:workspaces -- --workspace=toolcraft` closure. It restores bare declaration imports such as `toolcraft-schema` and `@poe-code/agent-mcp-config`. The identical packed SDK type-consumer smoke then passes, including both exact-optional-property modes. No source or package configuration was changed for this control.
- Verification boundary: the preceding standalone install/runtime/generation smoke passed even with the bad declarations; its generated fixture uses `skipLibCheck: true`. That route is not a substitute for the stricter published SDK type-consumer check. Later SDK/stdio/HTTP bundle smokes pass on exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 after the selected build.
- Release boundary: the inspected Toolcraft release workflow uses filtered workspace builds before its type/standalone checks rather than the root bundling suffix. No broken published release is asserted. The confirmed defect is implicit build-profile reuse and a reproducible local packing/consumer failure, not an observed registry incident.
- Local fix: Toolcraft and OpenAPI now run their maintained selected workspace build closures in `prepack`, before bundled dependency preparation. The workspace name comes from npm lifecycle metadata; build output goes to stderr so `npm pack --json` remains parseable. OpenAPI retains its executable-bit restoration, both packages retain cleanup, and root packaging retains its own root-layout build. Every standalone pack pays for an uncached selected build; this is an explicit artifact boundary, not a caching optimization.
- Verification: a fresh root build followed by the maintained strict SDK smoke passes without a manual selected-build workaround. An independently failing strict OpenAPI consumer now passes for both public entrypoints, nested/hoisted installs, and both exact-optional-property modes. Maintained standalone generation/runtime and SDK/stdio/HTTP bundles pass, the latter on all three exact runtimes. Actual packing under Node 18.18.0 / npm 9.8.1 and Node 20.0.0 / npm 9.6.4 also passes with compatible npm prioritized throughout lifecycle shells; TC-081 records the separate default manager-selection failure rather than hiding it.
- Status: locally fixed, not committed or released. Two relevant package-lint rules pass across 71 packages; manifest/lockfile checks show only the intended two prepack edits and no lockfile change. No implementation code changed for this fix. Original control logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session48-{sdk-types,rebuild-control,sdk-types-control}.log`; final evidence is recorded in session 54. Full unit/root-lint snapshot 52 predates these lifecycle changes and parity diagnostics.

### TC-078: combining short flags and aliases prevents CLI construction

- Surface: declarative parameter `short` and `cliAliases` metadata; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `formatOptionFlags` and `createOption`.
- Reproduction: declare `number: S.Number({ short: "n", cliAliases: ["value"] })`. Invoking any of `--number 7`, `--value 7`, or `-n 7` fails before handler execution with `option creation failed ... too many long flags`. Omitting the short flag but declaring two aliases (`["value", "amount"]`) likewise prevents every spelling from running. An eight-case built public CLI probe confirms all six combined/multiple-alias failures, while canonical and single-alias controls both return `{ number: 7 }`.
- Cause: the full help-display flag list is passed to one Commander option. Commander supports a short/long pair or two long spellings, not an arbitrary list. The public schema exposes an array of aliases and an independent short flag, but those valid-looking combinations cannot construct the command tree.
- Impact: adding a documented metadata combination disables the command regardless of the spelling invoked; this is not merely one ignored alias. The new numeric-array ownership tests deliberately exercised short and alias configurations separately and do not conceal this distinct registration defect.
- Local fix: register canonical/short spellings together and each additional long alias separately, all using the same canonical attribute and existing field parser. Register canonical boolean negation once. The full help-display list and collision guards remain unchanged; SDK behavior and schema metadata need no change.
- TDD: authoritative red has 180 failures / 60 passing controls; the final 300-case file includes omitted optionals, defaults, and JSON aliases. Three initial focused files pass 760 cases before those 60 additional controls. All 83 Toolcraft files / 3,364 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the 18-workspace build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 89 public alias/default/help scenarios; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-option-aliases.png` with all spellings and successful scalar, negative-array, and boolean output.
- Status: locally fixed, not committed or released. Full snapshot 46 predates this change; the final maintained help route's result is recorded separately in session 50.

### TC-079: global CLI controls reinterpret option values and literal operands

- Surface: `runCLI` argument preprocessing and parent-command global option parsing; sources: `normalizeVerboseAlias`, `hasHelpFlag`, and the global options registered on every Commander level in `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`.
- Verbose literal reproduction: with `controls.verbose: true` and a string positional, `check -- -v` executes the handler with `{ target: "--verbose" }`, not the supplied literal `"-v"`. Disabling that control preserves the literal. The alias normalizer maps every argv token before parsing, including data after the end-of-options separator.
- Verbose option-value reproduction: `--target -v` for a string or string-array option and `--target.entry -v` for a dynamic string record all work with verbose controls disabled. Enabling the control makes them fail with a missing-argument error: normalization exposes `--verbose` to the parent parser, which consumes it before the leaf can own the value. Inline `--target=-v` and attached `-t-v` controls preserve the data in both modes. Actual standalone `-v` and `--verbose` controls both work; an initial suspicion that the snapshot advertised an unsupported alias was disproved and is not a finding.
- Help reproduction: both `check -- --help` and `check -- -h` render help and exit zero without invoking the handler, despite the explicit literal separator. String option values `--target --help` and `--target -h` do the same. Inline `--target=--help` and attached `-t-h` execute with the intended literal strings. `hasHelpFlag` scans the entire argv before command parsing and ignores operand/value ownership.
- Evidence: 14 verbose cases and six help cases use the current built public CLI, assert exact handler payloads/counts, and capture errors/help without repository writes. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session52-verbose-values.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session52-help-values.log`. This is not TC-059's unknown-token classification: preprocessing consumes or changes these tokens before that repaired path runs.
- Expected: global controls act only on tokens that are actually options; literal operands and declared option values remain data. Preserve working help/global flags before and after commands, nested/default routes, aliases, and intentional global/parameter collision rules. A blanket alias replacement or a separator-only fix cannot cover the reproduced value-ownership failures.
- Repair in progress (session 59): the first 160-case regression matrix produces 95 failures / 65 controls. A command-aware preprocessing pass now distinguishes required values, attached/clustered spellings, dynamic scalar values, literal separators, actual help tokens, and actual verbose aliases. Parent parsing stops at the selected child; custom help remains the sole help owner. Additional controls catch and repair draft regressions in unknown-help errors, deferred dynamic validation, version options after commands, and later global-option precedence. The final 290-case ownership file and a three-file 428-case compatibility selection pass; package lint/typechecking passes. The complete package/build/runtime/visual checks are still pending, so this issue remains open.
- Status: locally fixed after final session-59 checks. The ownership file has 294 passing cases, including four added hidden-default help controls. All 88 Toolcraft files / 4,408 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, the selected build, and maintained root-help build pass. Each exact runtime passes a 150-case public matrix before the final route-token correction, followed by 20 final hidden-help/alias/cluster boundary cases after rebuilding. Inspected root help and `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-control-ownership.png`, which contains actual CLI invocations and correctly routed leaf help. No full-repository pass is implied; snapshot 60 is pending.
- Reopened after snapshot 60: four built-public CLI cases confirm a draft regression not covered by those tests. Help for a healthy leaf works alone but fails with a command-definition error when a sibling declares a reserved `--output` parameter without a short flag. Both direct and nested trees fail before rendering the healthy help. The new path constructs every command's options before routing help; the pre-audit source routed selected help before that construction. This is an audit-introduced follow-up, not a new pre-existing issue. The original control/value fixes remain verified, but TC-079 is again counted open pending repair. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session61-help-definition-public-red.log`.
- Follow-up repair in progress: command topology/global controls remain eagerly available, while per-command field setup is loaded once only when argument ownership needs it. Generated help no longer initializes unrelated leaf definitions. Before any ordinary invocation, all remaining field loaders run, preserving the previous whole-tree definition-validation policy. A new 24-case file first fails 12 healthy-help cases and passes 12 definition-error controls; after the patch, it and the existing control-ownership matrix pass 318 cases. Package/build/runtime/visual verification is pending, so the issue remains open.
- Follow-up fixed: all 89 Toolcraft files / 4,432 tests, package lint/types, the selected build, maintained root-help build, and root typechecking pass. Each exact Node runtime passes 48 public help/definition/alias/control-value cases. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-help-isolation.png` and refreshed root help. Normal invocations still validate every command definition before execution; this repair does not silently relax that policy. TC-079 is locally fixed again. The follow-up postdates full snapshot 60.

### TC-080: parity mismatch reporting throws on opaque SDK outcomes

- Surface: `createCommandTestHarness(...).parity(...)`; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/parity.ts`, `describeOutcome`.
- Reproduction: define an SDK-only command with no result schema and return `1n`. The SDK handler succeeds exactly once; MCP and CLI are intentionally filtered out. Instead of returning the expected structured disagreement, `.parity()` rejects with `TypeError: Do not know how to serialize a BigInt` while building `diff`. Returning `{ count: 1n }` or a cyclic object likewise rejects; a result with a throwing `toJSON` propagates that synthetic hook error.
- Control: the identical SDK-only command returning `1` produces a `ParityResult` with `agree: false`, a successful SDK outcome, two `SurfaceScopeError` outcomes, and readable diff text. The five-case built public harness probe runs no MCP/CLI surface, network request, or filesystem mutation, isolating the defect to diagnostic construction after the SDK succeeds. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session52-parity-formatting.log`.
- Cause: `describeOutcome` calls unguarded `JSON.stringify(left.value)` for every successful outcome. The SDK result is intentionally opaque when no result schema is declared; reporting a surface disagreement must not impose a new JSON-only return contract or let result serialization suppress the report.
- Impact: tests cannot inspect the adapter outcomes or intended mismatch because the test harness itself throws a formatting error. This is separate from TC-014's input rewriting and TC-070's shared state; TC-013 fixed bigint formatting in a different SDK input-error path.
- Related error path: an SDK-only handler throwing a null-prototype object or an object with a throwing `toString` also makes `.parity()` reject during `errorIdentity` coercion. A normal `Error` control returns the expected structured disagreement. These three additional cases run no MCP/CLI/network/filesystem effects; log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session52-parity-error-formatting.log`. A fix must cover both successful-value diagnostics and opaque thrown-value identities.
- Remediation constraint: built-in `util.inspect` with `customInspect: false` and `getters: false` avoids ordinary getters/JSON/custom-inspection hooks, but still executes a `Symbol.toStringTag` getter on each of exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2. A twelve-case isolated runtime probe confirms that behavior. Do not claim fully hook-free diagnostics from those options alone; bounded descriptor-based formatting or an explicitly narrower guarantee needs validation.
- Local fix: bounded descriptor-based formatting replaces arbitrary JSON/string coercion. It distinguishes bigint, nonfinite numbers, functions, symbols, array holes, accessors, proxies, true cycles, and shared aliases without calling JSON/string/custom-inspection/tag hooks. Native error names/messages are read through data descriptors; ordinary full string messages remain untruncated for comparison. Actual outcome values/errors are retained by identity. The formatter is internal to the testing package and introduces no public options.
- Verification: final 86-case regression matrix, two focused files / 96 tests, all 85 Toolcraft files / 3,905 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the 18-workspace selected build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 38 public harness cases, including full SDK/MCP/CLI long-message comparisons. Inspected actual CLI-rendered diagnostic reports and refreshed root help after 70 uncached builds/bundle. Status: locally fixed, not committed or released. Full snapshot 52 predates this patch; session 53 records its independent evidence and fixture corrections.
- Reopened follow-up: six public SDK-only parity probes on the three exact runtimes confirm that the descriptor-only repair loses inherited data messages and native DOMException messages, although it preserves the actual thrown object. SDK diff lines become empty or `object: {}` instead of showing the failure. A source comparison proves that the pre-audit function retained those messages; constructor-based naming was already its policy and is not a regression. The original opaque-value crashes remain fixed, but TC-080 is counted open until message fidelity is restored without invoking user hooks. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session57-parity-metadata-public-red.log`.
- Follow-up fixed: safe descriptor traversal restores inherited constructors and data messages. Captured native DOMException accessors provide brand-checked internal messages across the differing Node runtime representations; user-defined own/inherited accessors remain protected markers. Final coverage adds 36 tests after a 22-failure / 14-control red. Three focused files / 132 tests, all 86 Toolcraft files / 3,941 tests, package lint/types/build, 36 public cases on each exact runtime, root typechecking, and inspected diagnostic/root-help screenshots pass. Error objects remain unchanged, full inherited strings still participate in comparisons, and no user hooks run. TC-080 is again locally fixed; this follow-up postdates full snapshot 56.

### TC-081: nested packing does not retain the invoking npm

- Surface: local standalone packaging on the declared minimum runtime; shared `scripts/manage-bundled-workspace-deps.mjs` invokes workspace packs using the bare executable name `npm`. The selected-build prepack prefix also resolves that name through lifecycle `PATH` rather than retaining the caller's npm executable.
- Reproduction: invoke `npm pack` through the installed Node 18.18.0 / npm 9.8.1 pair with that runtime's bin directory first in the caller's `PATH`. npm lifecycle execution prepends repository `node_modules/.bin`, where the installed semantic-release dependency supplies npm 11.19.1. Child commands switch to that incompatible npm. The selected build finishes, but nested schema packing fails with `(0 , U.tracingChannel) is not a function`.
- Independent control: directly invoke that repository-local npm 11 under Node 18.18.0 to pack the unchanged schema package. It fails with the same error before any new Toolcraft/OpenAPI lifecycle build is involved. This separates manager selection from the declaration-profile defect in TC-077. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session54-pack-runtimes.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session54-npm-shadow-control.log`.
- Related maintained entrypoint: after repairing direct recursive packs, `npm run smoke:toolcraft-sdk-types` through Node 18.18.0 / npm 9.8.1 still fails with the same npm 11 `tracingChannel` error. Its own outer pack launcher re-resolves bare `npm`. The other maintained Toolcraft verifiers also use bare npm for packs/installs and registry checks; repairing only the bundled-dependency helper is insufficient for these developer commands. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session55-smoke-manager-red.log`.
- Compatibility control: a temporary lifecycle-shell configuration prioritizes the already-installed compatible npm inside child shells. It changes neither repository dependencies nor the user's shell configuration. Both Node 18.18.0 / npm 9.8.1 and Node 20.0.0 / npm 9.6.4 then pack both packages, compile a clean strict consumer in both exact-optional-property modes, and import their public entrypoints. Session 25205 completes with exit 0; log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session54-pack-runtimes-compatible.log`.
- Boundary: this is a local packaging/developer-environment failure, not a reported registry incident or a runtime import failure in a completed package. The inspected release workflow explicitly installs compatible npm for its Node 18 smoke. The controlled `PATH` probe is not a fix for default local manager selection.
- Local fix: a shared npm invocation helper retains the inherited npm CLI and current Node runtime for recursive packing and all four maintained Toolcraft verifier scripts. Prepack build prefixes likewise use npm-provided executable metadata. Arguments, explicit child environments, output/options, failure propagation, and metadata-free direct-invocation fallback are preserved. The new helper is included in release path filters and release-tree admission; no dependency or global shell configuration is changed.
- Verification: the original execution-level red has four failures / three controls; final focused coverage passes three files / 27 tests, including ten new invocation contracts. Real packs and strict consumers pass on all three exact runtime/npm pairs without a lifecycle PATH workaround. All maintained smoke routes pass on Node 18 with the recorded compatible npm choices; npm 9's separate registry-signature failure is not hidden. Full snapshot 56 passes all maintained unit/native/posttest stages, 9,761 configured/linted files with zero errors/warnings, root TypeScript/workflow lint, and the two relevant package-lint rules. Status: locally fixed, not committed or released.

### TC-082: codemode configuration reference omits inherited SDK controls

- Surface: package developer documentation; sources: `packages/toolcraft-codemode/README.md`, its Configuration Options table; `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/index.ts`, `CodeModeOptions` and `codeMode`; and `packages/toolcraft/src/sdk.ts`, `CreateSDKOptions`.
- Concrete evidence: an AST-derived comparison finds 12 public SDK configuration properties. The codemode reference documents six but omits `approvals`, `env`, `fetch`, `fs`, `logLevel`, and `logger`. The public codemode type directly extends `CreateSDKOptions<TServices>`, and its implementation passes the remaining SDK options to `createSDK`, so these are supported configuration properties rather than private internals inferred from an unrelated function.
- Impact: developers reading the package's configuration reference cannot discover its environment/filesystem/fetch injection points or logging and approval controls there. This is a documentation completeness issue, not proof that the options malfunction or that a user performed an unintended external action.
- Validation: `/tmp/toolcraft-session60-codemode-config-docs.log` records the exact public-property list, six omissions, type inheritance, and forwarding call. It reads only source/README text and does not consume mutable dist or execute a command handler.
- Session-127 source-only revalidation parses the current public TypeScript declarations and README table: twelve SDK properties, six documented and the same six missing. The current implementation first consumes approvals through effective-root composition, then disables duplicate composition in the internal SDK call while forwarding the other SDK options. `/tmp/toolcraft-session127-readme-source-audit.log` records the AST facts. This confirms documentation incompleteness, not every runtime option's behavior; both README files remain unchanged.
- Remediation: document those inherited options and their SDK semantics without duplicating or inventing defaults. Status: confirmed, open. README additions require explicit permission; none was changed.
- Related behavior: the separate post-freeze approval-catalog mismatch is TC-084. Merely adding a table row would not repair that runtime composition issue.

### TC-083: a custom get_schemas path becomes a prototype instead of a result entry

- Surface: exported `makeGetSchemasCommand({ entries })` customization; source: `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/get-schemas.ts`, the ordinary result object and `result[name]` assignment.
- Reproduction: provide an explicit command entry whose `path` is `"__proto__"`, then request that exact path through `names`. The entry lookup succeeds, but the result has no own `__proto__` property and serializes as `{}`. Its prototype is instead the returned `{ description, params }` object. Ordinary, `constructor`, and `toString` controls retain their own entries and normal result prototype.
- Cause: assignment invokes the inherited `Object.prototype.__proto__` setter rather than defining a data property. No mutation of global `Object.prototype` was observed or is claimed.
- Evidence: four cases execute the actual current handler/indexing functions extracted from source, with identity fixtures for command construction and schema serialization. The supplied serializer value is an ordinary valid-shaped JSON Schema object; its contents do not affect the faulty property assignment. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session60-schema-path-source.log`. This does not read mutable dist, and the full exported-runtime check remains pending until snapshot 60 ends.
- Boundary: this affects explicitly supplied custom entries accepted by the public options type. The ordinary `codeMode(root)` tree normalizes underscore-delimited command names, so this exact path is not claimed reachable through its default catalog construction. This is a low-severity custom-API data-loss issue, not a demonstrated remote exploit.
- Fix: define result keys as enumerable, configurable, writable own data properties while preserving the normal object prototype. Twelve added tests cover inherited-name controls, promised/direct entries, mixed/duplicate/repeated requests, JSON roundtrips, and unregistered inherited names. The authoritative red has four failures / 13 passing controls; all 17 schema-command tests then pass. Status: locally fixed in session 62.
- Public confirmation after the freeze: four calls through the built, exported `makeGetSchemasCommand` and actual `createSDK(...).getSchemas` reproduce the same missing `__proto__` entry/changed result prototype and three passing controls, with the real schema serializer. This removes the source-fixture qualification for the demonstrated custom-entry bug. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session61-codemode-public-leads.log`.
- Verification: all six codemode files / 55 tests, package ESLint/TypeScript, and the selected 29-workspace build pass. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes 16 exported SDK cases with zero leaf-handler calls. Actual CLI JSON contains both special and ordinary keys; `screenshots/toolcraft-codemode-schema-paths.png` was inspected. Session-62 logs record the evidence; no newer full-repository pass is implied.

### TC-084: codemode's approvals option omits enabled built-ins from its catalog

- Surface: `codeMode(root, { approvals: true, humanInLoop })`; sources: `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/index.ts`, SDK creation versus tree resolution; `packages/toolcraft/src/sdk.ts`; and `packages/toolcraft/src/human-in-loop/wiring.ts`.
- Reproduction: create a wired in-memory approval runtime and an ordinary root, then opt into `approvals: true`. An independent direct SDK has callable `approvals.list` and `approvals.show`. The codemode wrapper's search returns no approval entries, and its `getSchemas` rejects both paths as unknown.
- Control: explicitly premerge the same runtime's approval group into the root before calling `codeMode` with the same options. Search then advertises `approvals.list` and `approvals.show`, and both schemas are available. The approval runner is deliberately CLI-only and is not counted as missing SDK support.
- Cause: `createSDK` merges approval built-ins into a new root; codemode builds discovery and host-module entries from the original root. Its enabled callable SDK and advertised/executable catalog therefore describe different command trees.
- Impact: the advertised SDK-compatible configuration option cannot expose the enabled inspection operations through codemode without manual precomposition. This finding concerns catalog/host composition, not a failed approval decision or lost queued task.
- Evidence: built-public SDK and codemode calls in `/tmp/toolcraft-session61-codemode-public-leads.log` use a conforming synthetic provider and make zero provider calls. No approval handler, filesystem operation, network request, or real approval action runs.
- Fix: `codeMode` derives one effective root using the existing `mergeApprovalsRoot` policy, then uses that root for SDK construction, discovery, and execution module naming. The SDK receives `approvals: false` after composition so a custom merger runs exactly once. The existing helper is now exported from `toolcraft/sdk`; it returns the unchanged root when disabled, validates the runtime when enabled, and calls the runtime method with its receiver intact. No new proxy function or heavy approval implementation import is added.
- TDD: eight new cases reproduce two failures / six controls before the patch. They cover built-in/precomposed roots, false/undefined controls, missing-runtime errors, one stateful custom merge, renamed root-module execution, preserved environment options, and unchanged caller roots. A first green draft incorrectly expected zero runtime `invoke` calls for four actual command executions; correcting that fixture expectation to four calls preserves the real safety assertion of zero provider requests. All eight cases pass.
- Verification: seven codemode files / 63 tests and 89 Toolcraft files / 4,432 tests, both package lint/typechecks, and the selected 29-workspace build pass. Exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass ten public scenarios, with one custom merge and zero approval-provider calls. Actual discovery JSON is rendered through the CLI and `screenshots/toolcraft-codemode-approvals.png` was inspected. Status: locally fixed in session 63; full snapshot 64 is separately pending.

### TC-085: fakeFetch masks already-cancelled requests

- Surface: exported `toolcraft/testing` fetch double; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/fakes.ts`, `fakeFetch` after normalized Request construction.
- Reproduction: configure a matching text route and call the fake with an already-aborted `AbortSignal`, either in `init` or in a supplied Request. The fake records a Request whose signal is aborted, invokes its route predicate, and resolves a successful response. Native fetch against a network-free `data:` URL rejects with the supplied Error reason instead.
- Controls: a live signal succeeds in both implementations. An explicit live `init.signal` overriding an aborted Request succeeds in both, while an aborted init overriding a live Request is incorrectly successful only in the fake. The fake does normalize Request/init correctly; its missing cancellation check is the demonstrated cause.
- Evidence: the whole current `fakes.ts` is transpiled in memory without importing mutable workspace dist, and its actual exported implementation is executed against five scenarios on each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2. Nine cancelled scenarios disagree with native fetch; six live/override controls agree. No network request, persistent fixture, custom signal implementation, or changed source/test file is involved. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-fake-fetch-source.log`.
- Impact: handlers tested with this fetch double can appear to finish successful requests after cancellation, masking a user-facing abort path. This is not a claim that a real network request is sent, or that the double promises complete native body-stream/timing emulation.
- Remediation: after recording the normalized Request, call its signal's `throwIfAborted()` before matching routes. Failed calls remain available for diagnostics and retain the effective abort reason. Twenty deterministic tests start with 17 failures and three controls; all pass after the one-line fix. Request/init precedence, primitive/default/custom reasons, route-error precedence, and invalid Request construction are covered.
- Verification: two focused files / 33 tests and all 90 Toolcraft files / 4,452 tests pass, as do package lint/types and the selected 18-workspace build. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes 20 exported-helper scenarios without network or filesystem activity. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-fake-fetch-cancellation.png`: a live request reaches its route; an aborted request is recorded but rejected before route matching. Status: locally fixed in session 67; full snapshot 64 predates this patch.
- Reason-fidelity boundary: 21 additional native, network-free observations show default/Error/object reasons retain identity on all three runtimes, while primitive/null reasons are transformed by Node 18.18.0/20.0.0 and forwarded by Node 22.23.2. `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-native-abort-reasons.log` records these independently validated observations. A repair should reject pre-aborted requests and retain their signal reason without adding version-specific emulation; exact legacy primitive-error parity is not claimed.

### TC-086: mapped loopback literals bypass multipart private-address rejection

- Surface: remote multipart source downloads; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts`, `validateMultipartUrl` and `isPrivateIpLiteral`.
- Reproduction: the current validator rejects `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/audit`, `localhost`, and `[::1]`, but accepts both `[::ffff:127.0.0.1]` and its canonical `[::ffff:7f00:1]` form. Native fetch of each accepted form reaches an audit-owned server listening only on `127.0.0.1`, returning the same controlled fixture body.
- Evidence: source AST extraction executes the actual two validator functions with native `isIP` and a plain Error constructor standing in only for UserError presentation. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 rejects the three direct loopback controls, accepts the two mapped forms, and makes exactly two requests to its own ephemeral loopback server. A public HTTPS syntax control is validated but never fetched. Servers/connections close in `finally`. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-multipart-loopback-source.log`.
- Cause: after native URL canonicalization, `isIP` identifies the mapped literal as IPv6, but the guard checks only direct loopback and a few textual IPv6 prefixes. It never applies the existing IPv4 private-range checks to the embedded IPv4 address.
- Impact: callers relying on the explicit private-network rejection can still download from a loopback endpoint using an equivalent literal spelling. This is a demonstrated policy bypass, not a claim of credential theft, exposed production services, DNS-rebinding protection, or a remote exploit against this checkout. Local-file multipart sources are separately supported by design.
- Remediation: classify canonical IPv6 literals, including mapped IPv4 ranges, without weakening permitted public literals; verify initial sources and redirect targets through the exported preparation API with injected fetch and controlled loopback cases. Existing safe-bash source contains a related classifier, but importing the whole shell package would be inappropriate for this focused adapter repair.
- Candidate implementation evidence: official Node 18.18.0 Net documentation explicitly supports mapped IPv4 checks through `BlockList.check` (`https://nodejs.org/download/release/v18.18.0/docs/api/net.html#blocklistcheckaddress-type`). A native-only probe checks 54 private/public boundary and mapped-address cases on each exact runtime, all passing, with nine configured rules and no requests. This offers a parser-free repair without a dependency or engine change. `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-native-blocklist-controls.log` records candidate-library behavior, not a production fix or full download-API pass.
- Status: historical observation only, not pursued following the user's request to avoid security work. No production/test repair was made. Further validation and hardening are removed from the active queue.

### TC-087: Windows drive paths are rejected as multipart URLs

- Surface: local multipart file sources; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts`, `resolveMultipartSource` before `resolveUserPath`.
- Reproduction: `C:\\images\\cat.png`, `C:/images/cat.png`, `D:\\images\\cat.png`, and `C:cat.png` enter the URL branch and fail with `Multipart field "file" uses a disallowed URL.` The injected filesystem is never queried, even though it has the expected file.
- Cause: `URL.canParse(source) && source.includes(":")` is tested before native path semantics. A drive prefix is parsed as a URL scheme, and protocol validation rejects it before the existing path resolver can handle the source.
- Controls: Windows UNC and relative paths, plus POSIX absolute and relative paths, reach the injected filesystem and resolve the expected file with no download. The existing native path resolver can handle these forms; source classification is the failing boundary.
- Evidence: eight cases execute the four current source functions extracted with the TypeScript AST. Native `path.win32` or `path.posix` is injected as the platform path adapter; the filesystem is in-memory and download is an unused throwing fixture. Four drive-path cases fail before any filesystem operation; four controls read once and return the expected filename. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-multipart-path-source.log`.
- Qualification: these are current-source/control-flow checks on a POSIX host using Node's native win32 path implementation, not execution on a Windows OS or a packed Windows CLI. The rejection occurs before path resolution, and no screenshot or Windows-host pass is claimed.
- Fix: native `path.parse(source).root` distinguishes rooted/drive sources before URL detection. This retains normal remote URLs and base64 inputs without treating arbitrary colon-containing strings as files. Seventeen tests cover both native path adapters, absolute/drive-relative paths, both slash styles, UNC/root-relative paths, missing-file diagnostics, and remote/base64 controls; the authoritative red is six failures / 11 controls.
- Verification: four focused files / 131 tests and all 30 OpenAPI files / 751 tests pass; focused ESLint/TypeScript and the selected 19-workspace build pass. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes six ordinary POSIX exported-API cases and 11 cases using the actual compiled HTTP export linked to Node's win32 path adapter. The latter uses an isolated VM-module fixture, not a Windows OS or packed Windows CLI. The first probe had an invalid eval-relative module URL; using an explicit file URL fixes the probe without production changes. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-multipart-local-paths.png`, which labels that adapter qualification. Status: locally fixed in session 66; actual Windows-host execution remains unverified.

### TC-088: multipart downloads abandon redirect and rejected response bodies

- Surface: remote multipart response ownership; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts`, `downloadMultipartSource`, its redirect/HTTP-error/content-length branches, and its outer cleanup.
- Reproduction: an owned loopback server sends headers and an unfinished body for a 404, a response declaring more than 100 MiB, or a redirect to a completed success response. The adapter rejects the first two and succeeds on the redirect, but each original unfinished server response remains open after the adapter settles. Its internal fetch signal is not aborted. A completed ordinary-success control leaves no unfinished response.
- Evidence: five actual source functions and four existing constants are extracted with the TypeScript AST. Native fetch receives the real adapter-created signal and is mapped from a synthetic allowed public URL to the owned loopback fixture. Error/filename presentation helpers are identity/simple fixtures; response processing and cleanup are actual code. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 reproduces three abandoned responses and one passing completed-body control. All servers/connections are explicitly closed by the probe afterward. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session64-multipart-body-source.log`.
- Cause: redirects continue without consuming or cancelling the previous response; HTTP/status/declared-size failures throw before body cleanup. The outer `finally` clears the deadline and caller listener but does not close an outstanding response or abort its internal controller.
- Audit regression boundary: `git show HEAD:packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/http.ts` confirms that the earlier implementation also omitted prompt body cancellation, but retained `AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)`. The TC-074 compatibility repair now clears its owned timer after these early exits, removing that eventual timeout fallback. TC-074's exact-runtime fix remains valid, but this resource-lifecycle follow-up must be repaired; it is not represented as an unrelated pre-existing-only problem.
- Impact: a redirect or rejected download can keep response resources active after its command finishes. This is a functional cleanup and reliability issue; not every buffered response is affected.
- Remediation: cancel abandoned response bodies at each transition/error boundary and guarantee cleanup of the owned request signal/readers without masking the original result/error. Preserve TC-074/TC-075 timeout, caller-signal forwarding, and body-cancellation semantics. Include failing cleanup, invalid/missing redirect locations, response-size rejection, and completed/empty-body controls.
- Compatibility note for the pending repair: the existing cancellation tests intentionally retain an un-aborted internal signal after completed operations and verify that later caller aborts no longer propagate. Prefer per-response disposal before redirect/early exit, with a bounded failure fallback, rather than blindly aborting every successful operation or weakening those established assertions. A cleanup rejection must not replace the original HTTP/size/redirect error.
- Fix: a per-response `finally` cancels unread bodies before redirects or early returns. If cancellation rejects, the owned controller aborts without replacing the original failure; a redirect cannot continue through a failed disposal. Completed/empty-body results and later caller-abort isolation remain unchanged. No private-address handling is changed.
- Verification: 18 new regression cases reproduce 14 failures / four controls before the patch, then all pass alongside existing controls (three files / 114 tests). The OpenAPI workspace passes 29 files / 734 tests; focused ESLint/TypeScript and the selected 19-workspace build pass. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes ten real exported-API scenarios with owned HTTP responses, which close before probe cleanup. Actual CLI output retains success/error semantics; `screenshots/toolcraft-multipart-response-cleanup.png` was inspected. Status: locally fixed in session 65, newer than full snapshot 64.

### TC-089: unused codemode catalog failures escape their caller

- Surface: `codeMode(root)` construction; sources: `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/index.ts` and `tree.ts`. Construction starts `resolveCommandTree(...).then(...)` immediately, while search/schema/execute consumers attach only when invoked.
- Reproduce with a SDK-scoped command named `---`: `codeMode(root, { approvals: false })` returns successfully inside a synchronous try/catch. Without invoking a meta-command, the isolated Node process then terminates with exit one and `Codemode command name "---" must include at least one non-separator character.` No handler or external service is called.
- Controls: immediately awaiting and catching `createSDK(wrapper).search(...)` contains the same catalog error and exits zero. Valid unused and valid invoked wrappers both exit zero. Four scenarios pass their expected assertions on each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 using built public imports, without global rejection handlers masking the failure.
- Impact: an invalid optional/inactive catalog can crash an application after apparently successful construction; ordinary construction try/catch cannot own the asynchronous error. This is not a claim that invalid command names should be accepted for execution.
- Remediation: attach a rejection observer to the original shared catalog promise at construction. The promise passed to consumers is unchanged: it still rejects with the same reason, and catalog resolution still runs once. This preserves eager resolution without adding a lazy public API, silently replacing failure with an empty catalog, or changing handler execution.
- TDD/verification: seven new lifecycle tests start with four failures and three controls, then pass. Delayed consumers retain the same Error/object/string rejection identity; healthy and immediate-use controls pass. All eight codemode files / 70 tests, package lint/types, and the selected build pass. Each exact Node runtime passes seven default-process scenarios with no global rejection handler or stderr: invalid unused wrappers survive, immediate/delayed first uses still reject, and healthy execution still runs once. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-codemode-catalog-lifecycle.png`. Status: locally fixed in session 74; full snapshot 69 predates this patch.
- Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session70-catalog-public-green.log`. The first probe asserted an invented error prefix and failed after observing the real crash; `/tmp/toolcraft-session70-catalog-public.log` retains that probe failure. Corrected assertions use the actual source error; no production change was made.

### TC-090: nullable array presets disagree with direct CLI flags

- Surface: CLI presets; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `validatePresetFieldValue`. Scalar preset validation admits nullable nulls, but the array branch reaches `Array.isArray` without the equivalent nullable check.
- Reproduce: declare `values: S.Array(S.String(), { nullable: true })`. `check --values null` invokes the handler with `{ values: null }`; the same value supplied as JSON through `--preset` exits one with `Expected an array, got null` and no handler call. Shared schema validation accepts the value. `minItems: 1` does not change null's validity, but presets still reject it.
- Public verification: eight actual CLI cases on each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 assert handler payload/count, output, exit status, and canonical validation. Preset JSON is supplied via `/dev/stdin`; no persistent preset fixture or error report is written. Live arrays, empty arrays, nullable scalar null, nonnullable null rejection, and bounded empty-array rejection are controls.
- Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session70-preset-public.log`; seven earlier source-only comparisons are in `/tmp/toolcraft-session69-preset-source.log`.
- Remediation: preserve nullable array null before ordinary array/item constraints, while retaining errors for nonnullable null and invalid actual arrays. Thirty-three new memfs tests start with 12 failures and 21 controls, then pass across required/optional/nested/defaulted fields, bounds, scalar/item nullability, and explicit-flag precedence.
- Verification: five focused files / 262 tests and all 92 Toolcraft files / 4,500 tests pass, along with package lint/types, the selected 18-workspace build, uncached maintained root-help preparation, and root types. Each exact Node runtime passes eight rebuilt public CLI cases; nullable null now reaches the handler while invalid null/empty-array controls still fail. Inspected root help and `screenshots/toolcraft-preset-and-service-fidelity.png`. Status: locally fixed in session 72; no general nullable-object/variant preset guarantee is implied, and full snapshot 69 predates this patch.

### TC-091: fakeService's missing-method fallback invents promise behavior

- Surface: exported `toolcraft/testing` service doubles; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/fakes.ts`, Proxy `get` fallback and `isPromiseLike`.
- Reproduce: `await Promise.resolve(fakeService({ read: () => "ready" }))` rejects with `Unstubbed service method "then" was called.` An async factory returning that fake also rejects. A stub returning a nested fake unexpectedly returns a rejecting promise instead of the supplied child service.
- Cause: the proxy synthesizes a callable missing-method stub for `then`. Promise assimilation and the fake's own result recorder consequently misclassify an ordinary service as a thenable and add a bogus `then` call.
- Public verification: three failing-behavior scenarios plus explicit non-callable/callable `then` and ordinary missing-method controls are asserted on exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session70-service-public.log`; earlier source-only evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session69-service-source.log`.
- Remediation: preserve absence of the promise protocol member while retaining explicitly provided thenables and ordinary missing-method diagnostics. Fifteen new tests start with nine failures and six controls, then pass. Existing real-promise success/rejection recording remains covered alongside sync/async nested service and fluent receiver identity.
- Verification: three focused helper files / 48 tests pass; combined session-72 validation passes all 4,500 Toolcraft tests, package lint/types, selected build, and root-help/types checks. Each exact Node runtime passes 15 rebuilt exported-helper cases. An actual CLI fixture reports unchanged fluent/async-factory identity and only its real `rename` call; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-preset-and-service-fidelity.png`. Status: locally fixed; no general proxy serialization/iteration contract is added. Full snapshot 69 predates this patch.

### TC-092: specification readers reject native Windows drive paths

- Surfaces: OpenAPI source inspection and runtime command generation; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/spec-source.ts`, `readOpenApiSourceText` and `tryParseUrl`. Generation and mock-spec loading also call this reader; session 73 extends verification to their built entrypoints, as recorded below.
- Reproduce: supply a native drive-absolute or drive-relative specification string, such as `C:\\specs\\openapi.json`, `C:/specs/openapi.json`, or `C:openapi.json`. URL parsing recognizes the drive letter as a scheme and rejects it as unsupported `c:` before filesystem lookup. Native win32 path parsing instead recognizes these as local paths.
- Evidence: 11 source-reader cases using actual AST-selected reader/helpers with native win32/posix path adapters establish four affected drive forms and seven local/file-URL controls. Built `inspectOpenApiSource` and `commandsFromSpec` each pass eight reproduction/control assertions on all three exact Node runtimes: 48 checks total. Local/file-URL inputs read the injected expected path; remote controls use an injected fetch and no network. Drive inputs fail before either dependency is called.
- Verification boundary: the public checks run on the POSIX host and demonstrate the same early classifier failure; the source checks provide the native Windows-path semantics. This is not a Windows OS or packed Windows CLI execution claim. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session69-spec-path-source.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session70-spec-path-public.log`.
- Remediation: `tryParseUrl` now leaves native rooted/drive paths to filesystem handling before attempting URL construction. This same helper preserves original native-path labels in reader/parse errors. File/remote URL inputs keep their existing handling. This is a separate repaired call site from multipart TC-087, not evidence that its fix regressed.
- TDD/verification: 72 new tests start with 26 failures and 46 controls, then pass across the direct reader, inspection, runtime generation, mock-spec loading, generator inspection, and generator check mode. Local/missing-path labels, native path forms, URL objects/strings, and no-write behavior are covered. Seven focused files / 194 tests and all 31 OpenAPI files / 823 tests pass, with focused ESLint/types and the selected 19-workspace build.
- Rebuilt probes on each exact runtime pass six ordinary POSIX public calls plus 48 actual compiled-module calls linked to Node's win32 path adapter: 162 assertions in total. Native VM-module support is solely an ad-hoc verification method, not a product requirement or Windows OS run. The first probe lost backslashes while embedding fixture source; the corrected probe injects structured JSON paths and passes without production changes. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session73-spec-paths-public.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session73-spec-paths-public-green.log`. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-specification-local-paths.png`; generator inspection succeeds and check mode reports expected missing-output drift without writing. Status: locally fixed in session 73; full snapshot 69 predates this patch.

### TC-093: base-URL inference loses server metadata or source-relative context

- Surfaces: `resolveOpenApiBaseUrl`, `defineClientFromSpec`, inspection/runtime agreement; source: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/runtime.ts`, `resolveDocument`, `resolveOpenApiBaseUrl`, and `normalizeBaseUrl`.
- Reproduce templates: define a root server `https://root.example.test/{version}` with `variables.version.default: "v1"`. Inspection marks `GET /items` supported, but the runtime SDK sends its fake-fetch request to `https://root.example.test/%7Bversion%7D/items`. A host variable remains literal `{region}`; a port variable throws raw `TypeError: Invalid URL` during client construction.
- Reproduce source-relative context: load the same operation from `https://specs.example.test/docs/openapi.json` with a root server `/v1`. The document is retrieved successfully through an injected fetch, then client creation throws `TypeError: Invalid URL` instead of resolving the server against the known source origin. Missing/empty root servers also fail with the explicit-base-URL instruction even for remote source inputs.
- Expected boundary: OpenAPI 3.0.3 sections 4.7.1, 4.7.5, and 4.7.6 define the default server, source-relative URLs, and server-variable defaults; primary reference inspected: `https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.0.3.html`. This does not establish a project promise to implement every OpenAPI feature. If unsupported, these shapes need explicit contextual diagnostics rather than a supported inspection followed by a malformed request or unlocated native exception. Document-object inputs without a source origin may legitimately need an explicit base URL for relative servers.
- Cause: the inferred root URL is passed directly to `new URL`; variable metadata is absent from the internal server type and no source URL is supplied to base-URL resolution. This is distinct from the explicitly rejected per-operation templated-server feature, which already yields a useful unsupported diagnostic.
- Controls/evidence: fixed absolute root servers, explicit base-URL overrides, configured environments, and fixed operation overrides work. Session 80 runs 91 public observation/control cases per exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (273 total), shared with TC-094: document/URL-object/URL-string sources, override precedence, input immutability, and existing unsupported-operation behavior. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session80-server-public.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session80-server-runtime-matrix.log`.
- Swagger-format follow-up before repair: the normalizer accepts Swagger 2.0 operations but does not derive a server from declared `schemes`, `host`, and `basePath`; the public resolver also reads only `servers`. A fully specified HTTPS host/path therefore produces the missing-base-URL instruction, even when inspection reports the operation supported. Source-object and remotely loaded URL-object/URL-string inputs behave the same. Explicit `baseUrl` and configured environments make the same SDK operation execute against the declared target through fake fetch. This is grouped under the existing inference finding, not counted as a duplicate issue.
- Session-85 public evidence: 72 client invocations per exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (216 total) cover two schemes, hosts with/without ports, omitted/explicit base paths, three source forms, and inferred/explicit/environment resolution. Per runtime, all 24 inference attempts reproduce the expected failure and all 48 override controls succeed; 16 additional resolver/inspection checks pass (48 total). Inputs remain unchanged, no cache files or external HTTP requests are used, and no fix is made. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session85-swagger-public.log`. Primary Swagger 2.0 section 6.4.1 defines those URL fields: `https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v2.0.html`. The standard does not itself promise full project support; the defect boundary is accepted operation normalization/inspection with an unexplained missing declared target, not comprehensive Swagger compliance.
- Additional pre-fix evidence: session 90 confirms that both inferred and configured `https://api.example.test/v1?label=one/` lose the final slash from the query value, including in the actual SDK request; an explicit `baseUrl` preserves it. `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-base-url-query-public.log` records all three observations.
- Repair: root server variables use their string defaults, including empty/repeated defaults; relative and omitted/empty servers use the remotely served document location. Swagger 2.0 root `host`, first `schemes` entry, and `basePath` are inferred, using the remote host/scheme only when omitted. Explicit base URLs and environment precedence remain unchanged. URL normalization no longer trims query/fragment data or more than the existing single terminal separator. Invalid variable defaults and unresolved relative servers produce field-specific `UserError` messages rather than malformed requests or raw URL exceptions.
- Source/cache semantics: fetch results retain the final `Response.url`, falling back to the requested URL for synthetic responses without one. Fresh cache, conditional 304, replacement, no-store, and stale transport-fallback paths retain the effective document location. The private cache entry format advances to version 2 with `sourceUrl`; legacy entries lacking redirect provenance are refetched, not guessed. A legacy-only offline cache therefore cannot supply a newly inferred remote origin. The public resolver accepts optional `sourceUrl: string | URL`; `defineClientFromSpec` derives it automatically for remote inputs. Document/file inputs without an origin still need an explicit URL for relative servers. No new environment variable, dependency, CLI flag, or README edit is introduced.
- Regression evidence: 128 new tests in `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/base-url.test.ts`; final five-file focused run has 260 passes, and the full OpenAPI package has 34 files / 1,054 passes. The selected build executes 20 workspaces; package ESLint and TypeScript pass. The initial 107-case red run has 98 failures / nine controls; the expanded 113-case red run has 104 failures / nine controls. A later normalization-preservation expansion catches 12 failures with 116 passing controls before the final repair. The one first-green failure was a test assertion that counted an empty memfs directory as a cache file; its correction does not alter cache behavior. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-{base-url-red,base-url-expanded-red,normalization-red,focused-green,focused-corrected-green,focused-final,build-final,package-final,eslint-final,types-final}.log`.
- Native/public evidence: exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 459 actual SDK/MCP/CLI invocations plus 18 resolver controls: 1,377 invocations and 54 controls total. The matrix covers three source forms, inferred/configured/explicit resolution, input immutability, real initialized MCP sessions, and CLI exit codes. `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-native-public-corrected.log` is authoritative; the earlier `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-native-public.log` stops after a probe incorrectly used server name `audit` instead of command-root prefix `fixture` for its MCP tool call. That fixture mistake is not a product failure or a passing matrix.
- Real HTTP evidence: native fetch follows actual loopback 302 redirects and executes the resulting API operations across six root URL shapes and four cache modes. Each exact runtime uses 24 local servers and 48 constructed clients/API calls; all 144 clients and operations pass, with expected source/redirect counts and conditional 304 behavior. Cache filesystem state is memfs, not a claim of native disk-cache verification. `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-loopback-redirects.log` records the terminal pass. No external API or LLM is contacted.
- Visual evidence: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-base-url-inference.png`, captured from seven real CLI runs: template, source-relative, Swagger, query preservation, missing-default diagnostic, missing-origin diagnostic, and explicit override. The two rejected cases make zero API calls and exit 1; five controls make one call and exit 0. Transcript and assertions: `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-visual-transcript.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session90-visual.log`.
- Session-95 follow-up: public imports confirm that the session-90 implementation maps non-string root server URLs such as `42`, `false`, `{}`, and `[]` to the remote document URL, then executes `/docs/openapi.json/items`. Missing/null URLs and malformed selected server objects throw raw `TypeError`. Swagger `host: 42` becomes `0.0.0.42`, `schemes: "https"` becomes protocol `h:`, and `basePath: "v1"` or `42` changes the hostname. Other malformed root containers silently use defaults. The 21-case baseline includes 18 malformed cases and three valid controls; one malformed scheme already yields a generic URL error rather than its field-specific diagnostic. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-server-metadata-public-red.log`. These are functional inference/diagnostic follow-ups, including regressions of the audit's earlier implementation, not security findings or a new inventory entry.
- Metadata repair: require an array for consumed `servers`, an object for its selected first member, and a string for its URL. Consumed Swagger host/first scheme must be strings, schemes an array, and basePath a string beginning with `/`. Explicit base URLs and configured environments still take precedence; omitted/empty server lists and empty scheme lists retain their established inference behavior. Unselected server/scheme entries and format-irrelevant fields remain outside this focused check. This is not complete document validation or new protocol restrictions. The primary OpenAPI 3.0.3 Server Object and Swagger 2.0 root field definitions are rechecked against the same official specification references already listed here.
- Metadata regression evidence: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/base-url-metadata.test.ts` adds 216 tests: 41 malformed shapes across resolver/document/remote/file checks, override controls, and 11 valid consumed-field-only controls. The red run has 164 failures and 52 passing controls; all 411 tests in the three-file focused green run pass. The selected 20-workspace build, full OpenAPI package (35 files / 1,270 tests), package ESLint, and package TypeScript pass; handle 52238 terminates exit 0. Unit file inputs use memfs. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-{server-metadata-tests-red,server-metadata-focused,build,package,lint}.log`.
- Metadata native/visual evidence: exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 54 expected client-construction rejections, 36 resolver-error checks, and 45 SDK + 45 MCP + 45 CLI operations. Totals: 162 expected rejections, 108 resolver checks, and 405 successful operations. Overrides, valid absolute/template/Swagger targets, source-fetch counts, and unchanged document/memfs input are asserted; no external API/LLM is contacted. `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-native-public-corrected.log` is authoritative. The initial native draft incorrectly expects a previously unset `process.exitCode` to equal numeric zero and aborts on the first runtime; its shell loop also masks the child failure. `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-native-public.log` is retained as a failed probe, not a pass. The corrected probe initializes CLI exit state and propagates child failures. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-server-metadata.png`: eight actual CLI runs, five contextual errors with exit 1/zero API calls and three valid/override controls with exit 0/one API call. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-visual.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session95-visual-transcript.log`.
- Status: locally fixed for root inference and the reproduced consumed-metadata diagnostics. The existing single-fixed-absolute path/operation-server policy remains unchanged and its tests pass; this is not a claim of complete Swagger/OpenAPI support, a published release, or a new successful full repository gate. Snapshot 93 includes session 90 but predates the session-95 metadata follow-up; its separately documented committed-lock failure remains explicit.

### TC-094: path-item servers are ignored by inspection, generation, and runtime selection

- Surfaces: generated command metadata/source, runtime clients, inspection; sources: `packages/toolcraft-openapi/src/generate.ts`, `OpenApiPathItemObject`, `collectGeneratedCommand`, and `resolveOperationBaseUrl`.
- Reproduce: declare a root server `https://root.example.test/v1` and `paths["/items"].servers: [{ url: "https://path.example.test/v2" }]`, with `get.operationId: "listItems"`. Inspection reports the operation supported; generated `items/list.ts` omits the path-level override; the public SDK's fake fetch receives `https://root.example.test/v1/items` instead of the declared path server.
- Expected: a path-item server applies to that path's operations unless an operation supplies its own server. OpenAPI 3.0.3 sections 4.7.9–4.7.10 establish that inheritance; primary reference inspected: `https://spec.openapis.org/oas/v3.0.3.html`. Supporting only fixed per-operation URLs does not justify silently ignoring a fixed path-level URL.
- Cause before the fix: the path-item type lacked `servers`, and generated-command collection sent only the operation object to server selection. Runtime and generated source shared the resulting absent override.
- Controls/evidence: fixed operation overrides already work and take precedence when both path and operation servers exist. Explicit client base URLs/configured environments currently replace the root fallback but still do not honor the path server. Session 80's 273-case matrix confirms this across three exact Node runtimes and document/remote-source input forms; it checks generated source, not execution of a separately compiled generated package.
- Fix: the shared path-item type now includes `servers`; command collection resolves `operation.servers ?? entry.pathItem.servers` through existing fixed-server validation. Explicit operation precedence and the existing unsupported policy remain intact. Unsupported inherited relative/templated/multiple/empty server declarations are reported instead of silently replaced by the root.
- Regression/verification: corrected TDD baseline has 21 failures / 46 controls; all 67 server-selection tests subsequently pass. Four focused files / 264 tests, all 33 OpenAPI files / 926 tests, focused ESLint/types, the selected 19-workspace build, uncached maintained root-help preparation, and root types pass. Session 81 executes 95 cases per exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (285 total), including actual generated TypeScript handlers evaluated in memory with public modules and runtime-created handlers through SDK/MCP/CLI. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-path-server-selection.png` and refreshed root help.
- Status: locally fixed for path inheritance within the existing fixed-server policy; root inference is separately repaired in session 90 under TC-093. Full snapshot 79 predates both patches. All endpoints for this finding are synthetic and intercepted; no security investigation, separately installed generated package, or external operation is claimed.

### TC-095: terminal image capture lacks glyph fallback for Chinese search results

- Surface: the maintained terminal screenshot renderer used for Toolcraft visual QA; sources: `packages/terminal-png/src/png-renderer.ts`, `font.ts`, and public `renderTerminalPng` options.
