- Reproduce: render the actual session-82 codemode CLI transcript containing query `用户` and result path `people.用户`. The captured text and SDK/MCP/CLI assertions contain the correct characters, but `screenshots/toolcraft-search-identifiers.png` displays missing-glyph boxes for those characters. Latin text and the tested accented Latin spellings render normally.
- Cause: rasterization loads only the bundled JetBrains Mono font files and disables system fonts. Those fonts do not cover the demonstrated Chinese glyphs, and the public renderer options do not expose a fallback font selection. The render succeeds without telling the caller that text became unreadable.
- Control: the same parsed transcript/SVG rendered through the same rasterizer with an already-installed local Hiragino Sans GB font added displays the Chinese characters. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-search-identifiers-cjk-reference.png`; it explicitly labels the extra local font and states that production configuration is unchanged. No font is downloaded, bundled, redistributed, or committed for this control. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session82-cjk-reference.log`.
- Expected/remediation: make unsupported glyphs detectable or provide a deterministic, appropriately licensed fallback/configuration route. Keep reproducible default rendering and existing Latin/box-drawing behavior; a machine-specific QA override alone is not a portable product fix. Broader script/emoji coverage has not been established by this two-character reproduction.
- Status: confirmed tooling usability limitation, open. This does not invalidate the functional Unicode search results or claim that the user's interactive terminal lacks these glyphs.

### TC-096: CLI object flattening loses container requirements and defaults

- Surfaces: CLI nested-object argument collection, default application, and SDK/MCP agreement; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `collectFields` and `resolveParams`. This occurs without scope metadata, variants, descriptor mutation, or malformed builder defaults, so it is distinct from TC-060, TC-068, and TC-039.
- Missing-child reproduction: declare `settings: S.Optional(S.Object({ required: S.String(), note: S.Optional(S.String()) }))`. Actual `check --yes --settings.note memo` exits zero and invokes the handler with `{ settings: { note: "memo" } }`. Shared validation rejects that payload; SDK and initialized MCP calls with the same object reject missing `settings.required` without invoking their handlers. The same failure occurs with two optional object levels and missing `settings.inner.required`.
- Default activation reproduction: give `note` a valid scalar default while retaining the required sibling and optional parent. With no input, CLI `--yes` invokes the handler with `{ settings: { note: "seed" } }`, which fails the shared schema. SDK/MCP correctly omit the absent optional object and invoke with `{}`.
- Object-default reproduction: give the optional `settings` object its own valid `{ required: "seed" }` default. CLI `--yes` invokes with `{}`, omitting the container default; SDK/MCP supply it. With the parent object required instead of optional, CLI rejects missing `settings.required` despite the valid object default, while SDK/MCP still supply it successfully. These are declared valid defaults, not the mutated-default constraint gap.
- Cause: object containers are flattened into leaf fields without retaining their default/presence semantics. Inherited optionality makes required children optional even when the parent becomes active. `requiredWhenActive` is applied through variant bookkeeping, not ordinary object-container activation; nonvariant leaf defaults are injected independently. There is no final assembled-object validation for these ordinary CLI values.
- Controls/evidence: seven fresh-root scenarios across actual CLI, SDK, and initialized in-memory MCP (21 invocations) preserve schema descriptors. An absent optional object without child defaults and a complete supplied object agree across all three surfaces. The three malformed CLI payloads fail shared validation. All CLI comparisons explicitly enable/use `--yes`; no default-acceptance or interactive assumption is hidden. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session88-object-containers-public.log`.
- Preset/runtime extension: 15 scenarios per exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 compare seven CLI flag calls, 15 real JSON-preset calls, 15 SDK calls, and 15 initialized MCP calls (52 per runtime; 156 total). The earlier failures also occur through presets. Additionally, an explicit empty optional object is erased instead of activating required-child validation; a nullable object preset containing `null` is rejected; and an empty-shape object cannot be supplied in a preset because it has no flattened leaf field. Nonnullable-null rejection, valid nullable objects, absent/complete objects, and explicit overrides of whole-parent defaults remain healthy controls. All schemas and preset bytes remain unchanged; owned temporary JSON files are removed. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session89-container-runtime-matrix.log`.
- Actual interactive extension: session 20341 runs with real stdin/stdout TTYs and no `--yes`. The optional partial object and inactive-parent child-default cases invoke handlers with invalid payloads without asking for the required sibling. A required-parent control displays the real `--settings.required` prompt; entering `ready` through the PTY produces a valid handler payload. An absent optional-object control remains absent. All four observations pass their assertions and the process exits zero; that confirms faulty behavior, not a feature pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session89-interactive.{log,json}`. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-object-container-prompts.png`, rendered from the actual captured terminal transcript with cursor/erase handling; no prompt implementation is mocked.
- Session-91 variant/default extension: 15 additional scenarios produce 58 actual flag/preset/SDK/MCP observations. Selecting a OneOf branch incorrectly requires the child of an absent optional object, while SDK/MCP accept the branch without that object. Conversely, supplying an optional outer object's ordinary sibling lets CLI omit that now-required object's nested variant entirely and still invoke with invalid parameters. This confirms that a repair must combine ordinary-container and variant activation, not globally toggle leaf optionality. Partial/complete selected-branch inputs, inactive branches, absent outer variants, and absent outer objects with nested object defaults provide rejection/success controls. Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-container-variants-public.log`.
- Additional session-91 boundaries: nested leaf defaults can activate absent parents; nullable object defaults are replaced with child defaults or rejected as missing; empty object defaults and whole-object defaults containing a variant are omitted. A required empty object produces an invalid empty root through flags and an unknown-parameter error through a preset. Preset `null` is rejected before a supplied child flag can replace the parent; an explicit empty object overriding a whole-parent default is erased rather than validated as partial input. SDK/MCP agree on the intended final payload in these comparisons. The required-empty-object flag case documents a representation gap, not a claim that absent SDK input is equivalent to explicit `{ settings: {} }`.
- Remediation/boundary: retain ordinary object-container metadata and explicit preset presence, resolve explicit inputs before deciding parent activation, then apply defaults/requiredness only within both active container and active variant ancestry. Preserve absent optional objects, explicit null/empty containers, and whole-parent default replacement rather than merging defaults into supplied partial objects. Add regressions for the expanded observations before a structural resolver change; preserve existing variant diagnostics, dynamic fields, missing-value callbacks, positional precedence, and prompts. The initial in-memory reproduction performs no disk writes; later native preset controls use only owned temporary JSON fixtures with byte-preservation assertions and cleanup. No container-resolution source fix is made in these investigations. Status: confirmed, open.

### TC-097: JSON presets reject known dynamic parameters as unknown

- Surfaces: `runCLI` with `presets: true`, record parameters, and arrays of objects; sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `collectFields`, `loadPresetValues`, and `resolveParams`. The existing README describes presets as loading parameter defaults from JSON files, without excluding dynamic fields; it is read for context, not edited.
- Reproduce: declare `entries: S.Record(S.String())`, then supply `{ "entries": { "alpha": "ready" } }` through `--preset`. CLI exits 1 with `contains unknown parameter "entries"` and never invokes the handler. The same command accepts `--entries.alpha ready`; SDK and initialized MCP accept the same structured object. `S.Array(S.Object({ label: S.String() }))` behaves the same: a valid JSON array preset is rejected while `--entries.0.label ready` succeeds.
- Cause: collection separates dynamic fields from ordinary leaf fields, but `loadPresetValues` receives only the ordinary list and builds its known-parameter lookup from it. Dynamic resolution subsequently reads only parsed argv values, with no preset binding path. Adding a known-key exception alone would therefore not make the preset value reach the handler.
- Confirmed matrix: 13 scenarios / 48 observations per exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (144 total) cover records of strings/objects/arrays/records, object arrays and optional-object items, optional records, empty records/arrays, and nullable records/arrays. Eleven valid preset shapes fail as unknown on every runtime. Flat scalar-array presets and an absent optional record are healthy controls; nine direct flag controls and all 13 SDK plus 13 MCP calls pass per runtime. There is no ordinary parent container, scope projection, default mutation, or variant involved in the primary reproduction, distinguishing this from TC-096, TC-060, and TC-072.
- Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-dynamic-presets-public.log`. Every fixture uses a fresh root and explicit MCP scope, disables error-report writes, checks schema/preset-byte immutability, and removes its owned temporary preset directory. Success of the reproduction process confirms the stated defect and controls; it does not mean dynamic preset support passes. No external service or LLM is used.
- Visual evidence: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-dynamic-preset-gap.png`, captured from actual record and object-array commands. Both flag controls invoke once and exit 0; both valid preset inputs exit 1 without a handler call and identify the known parameter as unknown. Preset contents and actual temporary paths are shown without substituting a hand-authored diagnostic. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session91-dynamic-preset-{visual,transcript}.log`.
- Session-92 repair: the preset loader recognizes both ordinary and dynamic paths, validates supplied dynamic JSON values, and binds them into parameter resolution. Empty/null collections remain supplied values, valid nested containers are reconstructed, required child defaults are cloned per entry, and supplied dynamic members participate in inactive-variant rejection. Preset bytes and schema descriptors remain unchanged. Actual JSON arrays go through shared typed validation, not the argv-only indexed-object finalizer; string/number coercion and invalid bounds/children are rejected with preset and member paths.
- Precedence: explicit dynamic flags replace the entire preset value of that dynamic parameter, matching whole-field scalar-array overrides. They do not deep-merge partial entries or retain omitted preset entries. A preset must still be valid even when flags override its value, consistent with the ordinary preset path. Tests cover complete replacement, incomplete flag rejection, nullable-preset replacement, absent collection defaults, explicit empty collections, mixed ordinary/dynamic parameters, and distinct selected/inactive OneOf/Union members.
- Shared validation API: `toolcraft-schema` exports `ValidationOptions` and accepts `validate(schema, value, { defaults: "all" })`. The default remains `"optional"`, preserving existing two-argument behavior. `"all"` also applies defaults to missing required object members, clones and validates the resulting values, and propagates through nested arrays/records/variant branches. It does not invent a missing parent without its own default, activate an absent optional parent, replace explicit undefined in a required member, or merge a whole-parent default into an explicitly supplied partial object. In this mode union candidates are all checked because defaults may make an otherwise missing-key branch valid. No existing SDK/MCP default policy, configuration file, environment variable, or dependency is changed; README edits remain permission-gated. TC-039's broader adapter default-validation gap is not claimed fixed.
- TDD/package verification: two new files add 43 schema-option tests and 124 CLI-preset tests. The initial 140-case red run has 112 failures / 28 controls; final expansion has 167 new cases. Four focused files pass 408 tests; the complete schema package passes 12 files / 2,455 tests and Toolcraft passes 97 files / 5,236 tests. The selected 19-workspace build and both package lint/type routes pass. Strengthened invalid-default diagnostic assertions separately pass all 43 schema tests. One fixture's integer metadata key is corrected to the actual `jsonType` option before adding a rejecting fractional-value control. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session92-{presets-red,presets-focused-green,presets-focused-expanded,build,packages,lint,default-diagnostics}.log`.
- Native verification: each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 completes 191 passing CLI/SDK/MCP invocations and six passing public-validator checks. The matrix includes required/optional/nested/OneOf placements, records, object arrays, nested defaults, empty/null values, child variants, punctuation/Unicode record keys, malformed input, and expected zero-handler rejection. Each runtime also explicitly records one still-failing TC-096 observation: a preset containing only an empty ordinary parent around an omitted optional dynamic field is erased by CLI. Those three known faults are not passes or skipped cases. Totals: 573 passing invocations, 18 passing validator checks, and three classified TC-096 observations. `/tmp/toolcraft-session92-native-presets-classified.log` is authoritative. The earlier `/tmp/toolcraft-session92-native-presets.log` aborts when it reaches this existing container boundary; it is not a passing matrix or a new regression.
- Public type/visual checks: an in-memory TypeScript 5.9.3 program imports the rebuilt public `ValidationOptions` and `validate`, verifies inferred result types with `skipLibCheck: false`, and confirms one expected TS2322 diagnostic for an invalid option; no source fixture is written to disk. `/tmp/toolcraft-session92-public-types.log`. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-dynamic-presets.png`, captured from six actual CLI runs covering record, defaulted object array, nested empty collection, nullable value, invalid numeric child, and explicit override. Five valid cases invoke once/exit 0; invalid input invokes zero times/exits 1. `/tmp/toolcraft-session92-presets-{visual,transcript}.log`.
- Session-94 type-surface follow-up: the initial public compiler check verifies `toolcraft-schema` but reproduces TS2305 for the new type through `toolcraft/schema` and `toolcraft`, which already re-export the neighboring validation types. Added the two missing type-only re-exports. All three import paths now compile with inferred numeric validation results, and each rejects an invalid mode with exactly one TS2322 diagnostic. The maintained selected build, Toolcraft lint/types, Node 18.18.0/npm 9.8.1 published SDK-type smoke, and root types pass. These erased type exports do not change the runtime behavior covered by snapshot 93. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session94-{schema-export-red,schema-export-green,build,lint,sdk-types,root-types}.log`.
- Status: locally fixed for dynamic preset admission/binding and its stated validation/precedence semantics. Ordinary empty/nullable-parent behavior (TC-096), shared variant registration (TC-068), and CLI scope projection (TC-060) remain separate open boundaries. Snapshot 93 verifies the broader runtime/test/build/lint state but exits 1 on the separately documented committed-lock qualification; no new successful full gate, commit, push, or release is claimed.

### TC-098: returned schema defaults retain live command-definition state

- Surfaces: public `toJsonSchema`, `toJsonSchemaDocument`, and SDK-backed codemode `getSchemas`/detailed/full search. Source: `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/index.ts`, shared `withMetadata` default assignment. Codemode serializes parameter schemas through that function; no command handler needs to mutate its own input to trigger this issue.
- Reproduction: an optional array default starts as `["seed"]`. Get the schema through any of those five surfaces and append `"changed-by-consumer"` to the returned `properties.tags.default`. The descriptor's original array changes, and a subsequent real codemode `execute` host call with `{}` returns both entries instead of the original default. An earlier call returns only `seed`. The public baseline confirms reference identity and changed before/after execution on all five surfaces: `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-codemode-schema-public.log`.
- Impact: a consumer that edits an independently returned schema document silently changes later command behavior and other discovery responses. This is local mutable-data ownership, not a security investigation. TC-006/TC-072 repair invocation binding; their cloned handler inputs do not prevent a schema renderer from exposing the original descriptor. TC-039's deliberately mutated invalid defaults and TC-025's schema/input projection remain separate.
- Root repair: shared metadata rendering uses the existing `cloneDefaultValue` helper instead of assigning `schema.default` by reference. Nested object/array/record/JSON defaults and injected discriminator branch defaults now have independent plain mutable graphs. Default values, scalar/null/missing metadata, discriminator tags, and repeated references within one copied graph remain intact. There is no new cloning implementation, dependency, configuration, or public option. Opaque non-JSON resources retain the established helper semantics; this is not a new universal serialization promise.
- TDD: two new files add 80 schema-renderer checks and 30 codemode discovery/execution checks. The authoritative corrected red run has 100 failures and 10 passing scalar/null/missing controls; all 162 tests in the six-file focused green run pass. Repeated renders, reused sibling descriptors, deep branch defaults, five mutable default shapes, six placements, three discovery surfaces, optional/required fields, and actual before/after host execution are covered. Both schema and codemode lint/type routes pass. Tests use no disk fixtures or LLMs. Files: `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/schema-default-isolation.test.ts`, `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/schema-default-isolation.test.ts`. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-default-ownership-corrected-{red,green}.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-focused-lint.log`.
- Failed fixture qualification: the first draft incorrectly supplied an options object to `S.Json()`, which accepts no arguments, so its JSON case did not have a default. The first green attempt retains 18 fixture failures. The fixture is corrected to the existing descriptor metadata pattern, only this session's production assignment is temporarily restored to reproduce a fresh corrected red run, and the cloning fix is reapplied. The initial red and first green logs remain evidence of that failed test draft, not extra defect cases or passing coverage.
- Maintained package evidence: normal build completes 70 builds / 71 workspaces / 210 edges / 11 layers plus root suffix stages. Schema passes 13 files / 2,535 tests; codemode 10 files / 160 tests; Toolcraft 97 files / 5,236 tests; OpenAPI 35 files / 1,270 tests. Toolcraft lint/types also pass. Handle 30602 terminates exit 0; logs `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-{build,packages,toolcraft-lint}.log`.
- Native public evidence: exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 70 ownership scenarios and 140 real codemode host calls, covering five mutable default shapes, optional/required fields, three catalog surfaces, and four renderer entrypoints (`toolcraft-schema` schema/document exports, `toolcraft`, and `toolcraft/schema`). All 210 scenarios / 420 host calls assert unchanged descriptor/peer/fresh metadata and before/after execution while the consumer's own copy changes. No external request or disk fixture is used. Handle 60275 terminates exit 0; `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-native-default-ownership.log`.
- Visual evidence: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-schema-default-ownership.png`, captured from six actual CLI runs across array and nested-JSON defaults. For each case, a before call returns `seed`, an edit command changes only its returned schema copy, and an after call still returns `seed`. All six CLI runs exit 0 and the four actual codemode host calls preserve their original defaults. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-visual.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session96-visual-transcript.log`.
- Status: locally repaired with focused/package/native/visual evidence. Full snapshot 97 includes the session-95 root metadata diagnostics and this patch, but terminates exit 1 on the independently recorded committed-lock qualification. Its other stages pass; no successful full-gate or release claim is made.

### TC-099: additional-property aliases populate unchecked declared members

- Surfaces: SDK and MCP calls with either snake or camel casing. Sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/sdk.ts` and `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, `validateObjectSchema` unknown-property copying followed by optional-field resolution.
- Reproduce: declare `S.Object({ "user-id": S.Optional(S.String()) }, { additionalProperties: true })`. Invoke the SDK with `{ "user-id": 42 }`; its declared caller key is `userId`. The input is treated as an arbitrary additional property, copied under its literal name, and left in the handler payload when the missing optional caller key is skipped. The handler executes with numeric `params["user-id"]`, which fails the declared canonical schema. MCP repeats this with both expected caller keys, `user_id` and `userId`, and reports successful tool results.
- Current public matrix: 168 observations on Node 22.23.2. All 18 alias-only cases execute an invalid canonical payload across SDK/two MCP casing modes and top-level/object/array/record/OneOf/Union placements. All 150 controls behave as asserted: correctly cased invalid values are rejected, correctly cased valid values work, absent optionals stay absent, required canonical-only inputs are rejected, closed objects reject unknown aliases, explicit/defaulted known values remain authoritative, unrelated extras survive, and literal record/JSON keys remain unchanged. Inputs are unchanged; no disk fixtures, network API, or LLM are used. `/tmp/toolcraft-session97-additional-alias-public.log` records the counts and all 18 handler payloads. This is a real functional contract defect, not merely two schema names colliding in a definition.
- Relationship: TC-013 guards declared member-name collisions during construction; it does not validate arbitrary additional input keys. TC-002/TC-005 keep selected discriminators authoritative. Their controls do not fix this ordinary optional-member path. This finding concerns incorrect typed handler data, not a security-hardening investigation.
- Remediation boundary: disambiguate additional keys that become declared canonical members, with matching caller-schema documentation/metadata and contextual errors where appropriate. Do not globally rename or reject literal record/JSON keys, reject valid alternate union branches before selection, or silently change the established explicit/defaulted-member precedence. Validate those decisions with failing tests before implementation; do not label a runtime-only rejection as complete discovery/execution parity.
- Exact-runtime pre-fix evidence: session 98 independently reproduces the top-level invalid handler value through SDK and both MCP casing modes on exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 (nine confirmed calls). Log: `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-alias-native-red.log`.
- Local repair: when an optional caller member has neither a supplied value nor a default, SDK/MCP resolution rejects a copied extra whose name would populate that declared canonical member. The error identifies both names and tells the caller which spelling to use. This intentionally rejects alias-only calls even when the alias value happens to have the correct type; they are not treated as undocumented alternative parameter spellings. Explicit values, including nullable values, and present defaults remain authoritative over same-named extras. Unrelated extra properties, literal record/JSON keys, filtered-out members, alternate union branches, and injected-discriminator behavior retain their existing contracts. SDK explicit optional `undefined` is not treated as a supplied value.
- Matching metadata: MCP ordinary-tool and stream input schemas apply the same ownership conditions recursively through objects, arrays, records, discriminators, and unions. Each relevant canonical extra requires its distinct caller field unless a default supplies that field; multiple conditions must all hold. Conditions use `allOf`/`anyOf`/`not`/`required`, and `JsonSchema` gains type declarations for those already supported combination keywords. Result/event schemas do not acquire input-only restrictions. Primary combination/conditional guidance is inspected at `https://json-schema.org/understanding-json-schema/reference/combining` and `https://json-schema.org/understanding-json-schema/reference/conditionals`; actual admission is additionally compared with independent compiler modes rather than inferred solely from the reference.
- TDD: `packages/toolcraft/src/cased-additional-input.test.ts` starts with 420 cases, reproducing 79 failures with 341 passing controls. The five-file first green run passes 812 tests. An expansion to 489 new cases covers additional canonical naming forms, Unicode, multiple aliases, falsey/container defaults, caller `undefined`, valid alternate branches, and output/event metadata controls. All 489 pass. The selected 19-workspace build passes; Toolcraft passes 98 files / 5,725 tests, schema 13 / 2,535, codemode 10 / 160, and OpenAPI 35 / 1,270. Toolcraft/schema/codemode lint and types pass after rebuilding the changed schema dependency. The first pre-build typecheck encounters stale declarations lacking the new combination fields; that failed attempt is retained, not called a pass or patched around. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-{alias-red,alias-green,alias-expanded,build,packages,lint,lint-after-build}.log`.
- Rebuilt native evidence: each exact runtime passes 396 SDK/MCP ordinary/stream attempts: 216 accepted handler/producer calls and 180 expected zero-handler rejections. Across three runtimes this is 1,188 attempts, 648 accepted calls, and 540 expected rejections. All 2,376 metadata-admission decisions agree across Toolcraft's compiler and installed Ajv 8.20.0 in draft-7 and 2020-12 modes. Ajv uses `strict: false` without enabling coercion or default insertion; inputs remain unchanged. The two Ajv modes are an independent implementation from Toolcraft, not two separately implemented libraries. `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-alias-native-corrected-green.log` is authoritative; handle 85691 terminates exit 0. The initial native draft uses unavailable `import.meta.url` in Node 18.18.0 stdin evaluation and fails before assertions; an import-free control reproduces that environment behavior. The corrected probe supplies `createRequire` the absolute existing package-manifest path. `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-alias-native-green.log` is a failed fixture attempt, not product evidence or a passing matrix.
- Visual evidence: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cased-additional-input.png`, captured from eight real CLI fixture commands exercising SDK/MCP calls. Three alias-only cases exit 1 with zero underlying handler calls and a useful caller-spelling hint; five caller/explicit/default controls exit 0 with the expected canonical values. This is actual CLI presentation of API diagnostics, not a claim that the CLI argument parser implements these aliases. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-visual.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session98-visual-transcript.log`.
- Status: locally fixed for the reproduced SDK/MCP input ownership and associated MCP discovery rules. Codemode's raw input projection (TC-025), general applied-default validation (TC-039), and ordinary CLI container semantics (TC-096) remain separate. Full snapshot 99 includes this patch and all other current executable changes, but terminates exit 1 on the recorded committed-lock qualification. No successful full-gate, commit, push, or release is claimed.

### TC-100: additional result aliases replace declared wire values

- Surfaces: MCP structured command results and stream events, in snake and camel casing. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, `serializeResultObject`, which writes converted declared members before copying additional fields under their literal names.
- Reproduce value replacement: declare `S.Object({ "display-name": S.String() }, { additionalProperties: true })` and return `{ "display-name": "known", display_name: "extra" }` in snake mode, or use `displayName` for the extra in camel mode. The canonical value passes shared schema validation, but MCP emits the extra value under the declared wire member and loses `known`. A declared optional member's default is likewise overwritten by the additional wire-name field.
- Reproduce false failure: make the additional wire-name field numeric while the declared canonical value remains a valid string. The additional field overwrites the converted string, and final wire-schema validation rejects the result/event after the handler has run. A missing optional canonical string plus a numeric extra named like its wire field also passes canonical validation but fails wire serialization. These are rejected valid canonical values, not successful emissions of schema-invalid data; the existing final schema checks remain effective.
- Public matrix: 152 observations on rebuilt Node 22.23.2 across both casing modes, ordinary/stream execution, six placements (root/object/array/record/OneOf/Union), and six value/control cases. It records 48 replaced declared/defaulted values, 48 rejected valid canonical values, and 56 healthy controls. Every operation invokes once, source values remain unchanged, unrelated extras survive, and standalone record/JSON payloads preserve both literal names. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session99-result-alias-public.log`. An additional eight normal/stream SDK calls preserve both canonical and extra fields unchanged: `/tmp/toolcraft-session99-result-alias-sdk-controls.log`. No external API, LLM, or disk fixture is used.
- Relationship: TC-099 addresses input binding and now preserves explicit/defaulted member precedence there. This is the separate reverse conversion path. TC-046 repairs event casing and validates emitted schemas; those guards still work, but they cannot detect a same-type replacement or make a canonical-valid value survive a conflicting conversion. This is functional data fidelity and error handling, not a security investigation.
- Local policy and repair, session 100: declared object members reserve their converted wire names, including when an optional member is absent. `serializeResultObject` copies additional properties only when their literal names are neither canonical declared names nor declared wire names. Canonical values and applied defaults therefore win; absent optionals remain absent instead of acquiring an extra's value. This is deliberately a lossy projection for colliding additional fields, not preservation of both differently named canonical fields in one wire slot. Use `Record`/JSON for opaque keys that must retain both names; those payloads and non-colliding extras remain unchanged. Unselected union branches do not reserve keys in the selected branch. Final result/event validation remains enabled, and invalid/missing canonical required members cannot be repaired by an extra alias. SDK results retain their canonical, unprojected representation.
- TDD: `packages/toolcraft/src/cased-additional-output.test.ts` reproduces 256 failures with 160 passing controls, then all 416 cases pass. The matrix covers both casing modes, ordinary/stream execution, six placements, same/different-type aliases, optional absence/undefined, defaults, nulls, unrelated extras, invalid canonical values, missing required values, closed objects, literal record/JSON keys, unselected union branches, falsey/container defaults, input/output/event default metadata, and unchanged SDK results. Six focused files pass 1,375 tests; the selected 19-workspace build, Toolcraft's 99 files / 6,141 tests, and package lint/types pass. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session100-{result-alias-red,result-alias-green,build,package,lint}.log`.
- Rebuilt public verification: exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each complete 368 result/event attempts (296 accepted, 72 expected output rejections), 144 default-metadata invocation attempts, four SDK controls, and 1,456 schema decisions using Toolcraft's compiler and independent Ajv draft-7 validation. Across runtimes: 1,104 result/event attempts, 432 default-metadata invocations, 12 SDK controls, 1,536 MCP handler calls, and 4,368 validator decisions. Nested default metadata is checked across all six placements, both omission and explicit advertised-default invocation, ordinary/stream paths, and both casings. Canonical source/default values remain unchanged. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session100-result-alias-native.log`.
- Visual: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cased-additional-output.png`, generated from eight actual CLI fixture commands wrapping MCP calls. Seven valid scenarios return exit 0 with one underlying handler invocation; an invalid canonical result returns exit 1 with one invocation and the canonical-field diagnostic. Both casing modes display `known`, absent optionals remain absent, defaults/nulls survive, literal record entries are both retained, and stream output agrees. This is CLI presentation of MCP results, not a change to CLI parameter parsing. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session100-{visual,visual-transcript}.log`.
- Status: locally fixed and package/native/visually verified. Snapshot 102 covers this patch but ends exit 1 on the committed-lock qualification; no newer full-repository success is claimed. Broader schema-projection/variant issues remain separately tracked.

### TC-101: MCP discovery requires defaulted parameters that callers can omit

- Surfaces: MCP ordinary-tool and stream discovery, parameter summaries, and execution. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, `applySchemaCasing`, `collectParamSummaries`, and `validateObjectSchema`. This is independent of TC-025's codemode projection and TC-042's already repaired scope selection.
- Reproduce: declare `S.Object({ "display-name": S.String({ default: "known" }) })` and invoke with `{}`. Both normal calls and streams execute once with canonical `{ "display-name": "known" }`, but discovery lists the converted member in `required`; the command description also marks it required. A client validating against discovery rejects input that Toolcraft accepts. Optional fields promoted by MCP `requiredScopes` exhibit the same disagreement when they have defaults.
- Public baseline: rebuilt Node 22.23.2 records 168 discovery/execution observations across two casings, ordinary/stream paths, six placements (root/object/array/record/OneOf/Union), and seven scenarios. There are 96 mismatches and 72 agreeing controls. Required string/zero/null defaults and requiredScopes defaults execute despite advertised rejection; optional defaults and genuinely missing required members agree. Actual handler payloads and counts are checked. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session100-required-default-public.log`.
- Local repair: only input projection removes a canonical declared member from its required list when the scoped descriptor has a defined default. Injected discriminators remain required, because they are not defaultable canonical branch members. Output/event schemas and shared `toJsonSchema` requiredness are unchanged. Parameter summaries no longer label a defaulted field, or children of an omittable defaulted parent, as unconditionally required. Supplied values still override defaults; invalid supplied values do not fall back, and partially supplied objects do not deep-merge a whole-object default. This aligns discovery with existing binding rather than changing runtime default semantics or making every optional field required.
- TDD: new `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp-default-requiredness.test.ts` starts with 284 failures / 192 passing controls; after repair all 476 cases pass. Coverage includes ten scalar/container/default forms, optional and scoped requiredness, supplied valid/invalid values, missing required members, partial and complete supplied objects, nested placements, descriptions, unchanged result/shared requiredness, required injected discriminators, and default-induced union ambiguity. Six focused files pass 1,507 tests. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session101-required-default-{red,green}.log`.
- Maintained checks: normal `npm run build` completes with all root suffix stages; Toolcraft passes 100 files / 6,617 tests, codemode 10 / 160, OpenAPI 35 / 1,270, and Toolcraft lint/types pass. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session101-{build,packages,lint}.log`.
- Rebuilt verification: exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass 476 actual invocation attempts, including 372 accepted calls and 104 expected zero-handler rejections, with 1,712 metadata/output decisions checked against Toolcraft's compiler and independent Ajv draft-7 validation. Totals: 1,428 attempts, 1,116 accepted calls, 312 expected rejections, and 5,136 validator decisions. Both casing modes, normal/stream execution, all six placements, required/default/description/discriminator/union controls, and caller input immutability are verified. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session101-required-default-native.log`.
- Visual: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-mcp-default-requiredness.png` from six actual CLI fixture commands wrapping MCP discovery and calls. Five defaulted scenarios show empty required lists, descriptions without the false required marker, and the expected result; a genuinely required member without a default still reports the missing-parameter error with exit 1 and zero handler calls. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session101-{visual,visual-transcript}.log`. These are actual displayed discovery/call results, not a change to CLI parameter-binding behavior.
- Status: locally fixed, with package/native/visual verification. Full snapshot 102 covers this source state but ends exit 1 on the committed-lock qualification; no successful full result is claimed. TC-039's applied-default constraints are independently fixed in session 116. TC-025's codemode projection and CLI container/requiredness findings remain open.

### TC-102: rich result labels collapse distinct sibling keys

- Surface: default CLI rich object cards, nested detail rows, list labels, object/array section titles, and the detail-table renderer. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/renderer.ts`, `humanizeKey` and its result-label call sites.
- Reproduce: return `{ "display-name": "dash", display_name: "underscore" }` from an ordinary command. Both rows appear as `Display name`, so users cannot determine which key owns each value. The same ambiguity occurs in nested objects, section titles, scalar/list mixtures, and Unicode keys. All values still exist; this is presentation ambiguity, distinct from TC-100's data replacement.
- Public baseline: 30 actual CLI calls on rebuilt Node 22.23.2 cover ten fixtures in rich, JSON, and Markdown modes. Seven rich scenarios have ambiguous sibling labels; 23 format/ordinary/separate-parent/top-level-array controls remain healthy. JSON preserves the complete original value, Markdown retains literal keys, and top-level rich array tables already retain raw column names. Every call exits 0, invokes once, and leaves its value unchanged. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session102-labels-public-corrected.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-result-labels-before.png` and transcript `/tmp/toolcraft-session102-labels-transcript.log`.
- Probe correction: the first draft incorrectly passes an unsupported top-level `runCLI` output option and then tries to parse rich output as JSON. Its failure is retained in `/tmp/toolcraft-session102-labels-public.log`, not counted as a Toolcraft issue. The corrected fixture enables output controls and passes the actual `--output` flag.
- Local repair: compute humanized-label frequencies within each object and use literal keys only for colliding sibling labels. Reuse that sibling map across scalar rows, lists, and object/array section titles, and compute fresh maps for nested objects. Ordinary readable labels and matching keys in different parents remain unchanged; no data keys, values, custom renderers, JSON output, Markdown output, or array-table column names are rewritten. This is a label-correctness fix, not a design-system/theme change.
- TDD: 37 new cases in `packages/toolcraft/src/renderer.test.ts` reproduce 17 failures with 40 passing old/new controls, then the complete file passes 57 tests. Renderer plus CLI tests pass two files / 238 tests. Cases include all seven baseline shapes, already-humanized sibling keys, detail-table labels, local rather than global collision scope, unchanged data, machine formats, and custom renderer ownership. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session103-labels-{red,green}.log`.
- Verification: selected 19-workspace build, Toolcraft 100 files / 6,654 tests, and package lint/types pass. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes 33 actual CLI attempts across 11 fixtures and three formats: 99 attempts total, all exit 0, one handler each, immutable results. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session103-{build,package,lint,labels-native}.log`. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-result-labels-after.png`: colliding keys are distinct, ordinary labels remain readable, and the array/separate-parent controls retain their prior presentation.
- Status: locally fixed and package/native/visually verified. Session 104's later renderer checks retain this fix. Full snapshot 102 predates these renderer edits; it is not claimed as full verification of this patch.

### TC-103: MCP result-envelope rendering discards sibling fields

- Surface: CLI rich, Markdown, JSON, custom renderers, and MCP error-result presentation. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/renderer.ts`, `extractMcpPayload`, which previously unwrapped `structuredContent.result` whenever a `result` key existed, regardless of other fields.
- Reproduce: an actual MCP call returns `structuredContent: { result: "visible", next_cursor: "page-2" }`. Return that MCP envelope from a CLI command. The CLI prints only `visible`, dropping the cursor even in JSON output. Payloads such as `{ result: [], total: 0 }`, `{ result: null, total: 0 }`, or false/zero results also lose `total`; the null case becomes `Done.` or `{ "ok": true }` instead of the received payload. The loss occurs after the complete structured payload has been received and validated.
- Public baseline: 24 actual MCP-to-CLI attempts on rebuilt Node 22.23.2 cover six affected result shapes plus single-result-wrapper and no-result-key controls, in all three formats. There are 18 losses and six healthy controls. Each MCP handler runs once; its complete structured payload and source immutability are checked before rendering. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session103-envelope-public.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-mcp-envelope-before.png` and transcript `/tmp/toolcraft-session103-envelope-transcript.log`.
- Local repair: unwrap only when the structured object has exactly one enumerable key and that key is `result`. Otherwise retain the whole structured payload for default/custom rendering and error output. Existing single-result wrapper behavior, text-content fallback, non-envelope objects, and MCP error status/stream routing remain intact. This does not change the separate MCP proxy declared-output handling or resolve every possible ambiguity in envelope-shaped ordinary domain objects.
- TDD: 42 new renderer cases reproduce 24 failures with 75 passing controls. They cover six result value forms in three output modes, sibling fields, source immutability, retained single-wrapper behavior, error payload context, stderr/status preservation, and complete values passed to custom rich/Markdown/JSON renderers. Renderer, CLI, and MCP-proxy focused tests pass three files / 314 tests. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session104-envelope-{red,green}.log`.
- Verification: selected 19-workspace build, Toolcraft 100 files / 6,696 tests, package lint/types, forced root-help screenshot with 70 uncached tasks, maintained root types, and all 17 package-lint rules pass. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session104-{build,package,lint,root-help,root-types,package-lint}.log`. Refreshed `screenshots/help.png` is inspected.
- Exact-runtime/visual verification: each Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 passes 27 actual MCP-to-CLI calls across nine fixtures and three output modes. Totals: 81 attempts, 72 successful CLI calls and nine expected error-result exits; every upstream handler runs once. JSON equals the complete payload, sibling metadata survives rich/Markdown output, successful output stays on stdout, and an actual in-process upstream `isError` envelope retains actionable detail on stderr with exit 1. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session104-envelope-native.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-mcp-envelope-after.png` and transcript `/tmp/toolcraft-session104-envelope-transcript.log`.
- Status: locally fixed and package/native/visually verified. Full snapshot 102 predates this renderer patch; no fresh full-repository pass is claimed.

### TC-104: MCP proxies lose upstream failure semantics

- Surfaces: MCP-proxied SDK calls, CLI commands, and re-exposed MCP tools. Sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp-proxy.ts`, `createProxyCommand`; `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp.ts`, handler-result adaptation. This is distinct from TC-103's already repaired sibling-field rendering: the proxy can lose the error flag or original failure before rendering sees it.
- Typed error without structured content: an upstream tool declares an object `outputSchema` and returns `{ content: [{ type: "text", text: "upstream failure typederror" }], isError: true }`. SDK/CLI/downstream MCP replace the actual failure with `declared outputSchema but returned no structuredContent`. The real upstream accepts error results without requiring a success-schema payload; this is not the malformed-success control.
- Typed error with structured content: return `isError: true` plus `structuredContent: { id: "diagnostic" }`. The proxy returns only the structured object, so SDK resolves normally, CLI exits 0, and re-exposed MCP returns a successful structured result. If the error payload does not match the success schema, SDK/CLI still expose it as successful data, while downstream MCP reports unrelated result-schema errors instead of the upstream failure.
- Untyped error re-exposure: SDK preserves the raw error envelope, and CLI correctly exits 1 and renders its error context. Re-exposed MCP instead embeds that envelope as JSON text inside a successful result and loses the outer `isError` flag. A client cannot rely on normal MCP failure classification, even though the text may still contain the original message.
- Public evidence: a real ephemeral loopback HTTP upstream exposes eight tools; actual SDK, CLI, and downstream MCP calls produce 24 observations and 24 upstream invocations. Of 15 upstream-error observations, 11 lose or misreport failure semantics and four preserve them. Nine successful/malformed-success controls behave as expected. The temporary proxy-cache directory is created for the probe and removed in `finally`; upstream connections/listener are closed. No external API or LLM is used. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-proxy-errors-public-final.log`.
- Exact-runtime extension: Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each reproduce seven known defects with five healthy controls over 12 real loopback calls: 36 attempts, 21 known-defect observations, 15 healthy controls, and 36 upstream invocations total. The narrower matrix covers missing error structured content, structured error false-success, untyped downstream flag loss, and typed success. These expected-defect observations are not passing functionality. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-proxy-errors-native.log`.
- Visual: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-errors-before.png`, rendered from the eight actual CLI captures and their exit codes. Typed structured upstream errors visibly exit 0; typed text-only failures show the unrelated missing-structured-content diagnostic; untyped failures retain exit 1 and context. Transcript: `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-proxy-errors-transcript.log`.
- Fixture corrections: the first draft forgets that `createMCPServer` is asynchronous when proxy discovery is needed. The next draft expects the proxy's malformed-success message, but the real upstream correctly rejects that malformed success first. Both failed drafts are retained in `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-proxy-errors-public.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-proxy-errors-public-corrected.log`. The final probe awaits server creation, separately verifies control rejection without assigning it to the wrong layer, and records observations as they occur. Neither fixture mistake is promoted as a product defect.
- Remediation boundary: define end-to-end error ownership before changing return shapes. Preserve upstream failure classification and useful text/structured context without applying a success output schema to an error or returning an error payload as a normal typed value. Retain the existing healthy untyped SDK envelope and CLI controls, and distinguish downstream MCP protocol errors from MCP `isError` tool results. A local `structuredContent` fallback or renderer-only patch is not sufficient.
- Status: locally fixed in session 107 with TDD, package checks, exact Node 18/20/22 calls, and inspected CLI screenshots. Typed SDK failures reject with the upstream message and original envelope as cause; untyped SDK envelopes retain their existing contract. Proxy errors bypass success-only transforms/validation and preserve MCP failure classification. Successful typed results retain their existing domain-payload behavior. Full snapshot 108 covers this repair but retains the separately recorded committed-lock qualification failure.

### TC-105: ordinary domain results are mistaken for MCP envelopes

- Surface: ordinary CLI command results, with or without a declared `result` schema, in default/custom rich, Markdown, and JSON rendering. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/renderer.ts`, `isMcpCallToolResult` and unconditional envelope unwrapping before custom renderers. These fixtures make no MCP calls and use no proxies.
- Reproduce: return `{ content: [] }` from a command, including one whose result schema explicitly declares `content`. CLI prints `Done.` or `{ "ok": true }` instead of the object. A domain `{ content: [{ type: "text", text: "domain-text" }] }` becomes the text alone. `{ structuredContent: { name: "domain" } }` loses its outer field, and an accompanying `_meta` domain field disappears too.
- Custom renderer impact: the callback receives the extracted text or inner object rather than the actual schema-valid handler result. A callback written against the declared result shape cannot safely access its documented fields. This happens before custom presentation, so selecting a custom renderer does not avoid it. SDK calls preserve the original values; a string-valued `content` or an extra ordinary key prevents the heuristic and serves as a control, not a recommended workaround.
- Public evidence: 84 actual calls on rebuilt Node 22.23.2 cover six fixtures with/without declared result schemas, default/custom rendering, and rich/Markdown/JSON formats. There are 48 affected CLI calls, 24 healthy CLI controls, and 12 healthy SDK controls. Every schema-valid handler value is checked, every invocation occurs once, source values remain unchanged, custom callback inputs are compared to the original values, and JSON is compared structurally. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-domain-envelope-public.log`.
- Visual: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-domain-envelope-before.png`, showing six ordinary schema-declared command outputs, not MCP output. The affected content/structured-content fields are replaced or removed, while string-content and extra-key controls retain their fields. Transcript: `/tmp/toolcraft-session105-domain-envelope-transcript.log`.
- Relationship/remediation: TC-103 now preserves siblings inside a genuine structured MCP payload, but it does not establish whether a domain object is an MCP envelope at all. Preserve ordinary handler values and declared/custom-renderer contracts while retaining genuine MCP envelope/error presentation and existing single-result wrapper compatibility. Resolve provenance/explicit adapter ownership rather than adding more field-name heuristics or silently fixing only schema-declared cases while leaving the untyped ambiguity unreported.
- Status: locally fixed in session 107. Shape inference is removed; `asMCPResult` explicitly marks direct protocol values and proxies mark them automatically. Ordinary data stays intact with or without a schema, and custom renderers receive the actual handler return value. Direct integrations must opt in; custom MCP renderers now access structured content explicitly. This compatibility boundary is documented in `docs/toolcraft-results.md`. TDD/package/native/visual checks pass; snapshot 108 covers the repair with its separately recorded committed-lock qualification failure.

### TC-106: typed MCP successes lose envelope metadata

- Surface: `tiny-stdio-mcp-server` result normalization, also affecting Toolcraft and HTTP servers built on it. `normalizeToolResult` reconstructs successful typed envelopes from content, `isError`, and `structuredContent`, discarding `_meta`. Untyped successes and protocol errors retain metadata, so adding a valid output schema changes unrelated result data.
- Concrete reproduction: register a tool returning `{ content: [{ type: "text", text: "upstream text" }], structuredContent: { temperature: 22 }, isError: false, _meta: { trace: "request-one", pagination: { cursor: "next" } } }`. Without an output schema metadata survives; with a matching object output schema it disappears. Empty-content typed successes lose it too. The server still validates the structured payload successfully.
- TDD: eight new direct-server cases cover typed/untyped, success/error, and empty/nonempty content; two failures and 35 passing controls reproduce the defect. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session107-result-metadata-red.log`. The two Toolcraft typed-success adapter regressions independently observe the same boundary.
- Local repair spreads the recognized protocol envelope before supplying content fallback and structured content. Existing validation, typed JSON-text fallback, error semantics, and source immutability remain intact. Direct-server regressions and the complete server package pass; 36 exact-runtime calls independently verify typed/untyped metadata, success/error envelopes, empty/nonempty content, and Toolcraft output casing/transforms. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session107-metadata-native.log`. Status: locally fixed; snapshot 108 covers the repair, but its committed-lock qualification failure prevents claiming a fresh full pass.
- This is a functional data-preservation issue, not security investigation.

### TC-107: proxy discovery repeats pagination cycles instead of terminating

- Surface: MCP proxy discovery and CLI startup/help. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp-proxy.ts`, `fetchCache`. The loop ends only when `nextCursor` is undefined; it does not recognize a previously returned cursor.
- Public reproduction: a real loopback HTTP upstream returns a self-repeating cursor, a two-cursor cycle, or a repeated empty-string cursor. Both `resolveMcpProxies` and actual CLI group help keep requesting pages until the fixture deliberately returns an error. The self/empty cases make four requests each, and the longer cycle makes five, rather than terminating on the repeated cursor. No elapsed timeout or unbounded test is needed to establish the repeated requests.
- Controls: a three-page listing with an opaque cursor followed by an empty-string cursor completes and caches all tools; subsequent CLI help uses the cache without new upstream listings. The eight-attempt baseline has six known-defect observations and two healthy controls, totaling 29 upstream listings. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-pagination-public-before-corrected.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-pagination-before.png`. The error text mentioning a fixture bound is deliberately supplied by the test upstream, not a product diagnostic.
- Fixture correction: the first manual HTTP fixture forces a protocol version that the client did not negotiate, so initialization fails before pagination. The corrected fixture echoes the client's proposed supported version. The failed draft remains `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-pagination-public-before.log` and is not product evidence.
- TDD: six cold/warm cyclic-pagination cases fail before the fix, while one-page, opaque/empty-cursor, and 35-distinct-page controls pass. Tests check exact request counts, discovery-client closure, preserved command identities, and unchanged/absent cache files. The repair tracks returned cursors and rejects only repetition; no arbitrary page limit or new configuration is added. Broader verification is pending at this checkpoint.
- Verification/status: locally fixed. Complete Toolcraft scope passes 101 files / 6,807 tests; selected build, package lint/types, 17 package-lint rules, forced root help, and maintained root types pass. Each Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 verifies eight real resolver/CLI attempts: six expected cycle failures and two healthy controls, with 17 upstream listings per runtime. Self/empty cycles terminate after two pages and the longer cycle after three; no fixture cutoff is reached. Totals are 24 attempts / 51 listings. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-pagination-native.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-pagination-after.png` and transcript `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-pagination-after-transcript.log`. Full snapshot 108 predates this fix.
- Protocol boundary checked September 6, 2026: the current `tiny-mcp-client` requests version `2025-03-26`. Its versioned pagination guidance describes opaque position tokens and stable cursors without requiring a cursor encoding or fixed page size. The official Ruby SDK also documents a repeated-cursor guard. The current draft, however, contains stricter wording about making value-based cursor determinations. This repair is a current-client cycle policy, not a claim that every repeated token violates every MCP revision or that Toolcraft supports the draft protocol. Revisit the policy with a protocol-version migration. Primary references: `https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-03-26/server/utilities/pagination`, `https://ruby.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/client/pagination/`, and `https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/draft/server/utilities/pagination`.

### TC-108: failed proxy cache persistence leaves a replaced command tree

- Surface: cold discovery and refresh of an existing MCP proxy group. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/mcp-proxy.ts`, `resolveSingleProxy`. Child population has its own rollback, but successful population followed by `writeCache` failure lies outside that rollback.
- Public reproduction: a real HTTP upstream advertises `old` and `new`. Make the isolated temporary cache directory unwritable, then resolve the proxy. Discovery rejects with a real `EACCES` write error, but the cold group now contains both generated commands. On a warm refresh, the old command object is replaced and `new` appears despite rejection. Existing cache bytes remain unchanged, so the in-memory generation no longer matches the failed operation's prior state.
- Controls: the previously created warm SDK call remains callable, returning the old connection's result before and after failure. This is not evidence of an incorrect upstream tool invocation; it demonstrates partial state replacement despite failed persistence. The probe restores temporary-directory permissions, environment state, connections, and files in `finally`. No external system or persistent user file is involved. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-cache-public-before.log`.
- Visual: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-cache-before.png`. An actual CLI fixture command renders the observed cold/warm state, including `originalCommandsRetained: false`; its exit 0 belongs to the inspection command, not the failed discovery. The failure is independently asserted before that rendering. Transcript: `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-cache-before-transcript.log`.
- TDD: eight cold/warm × flat/nested-manual-group × write/rename cases fail before repair. They check child identity at every captured group, unchanged cache bytes, removed staging files, and continued use of the old hot connection. Rollback now surrounds both child population and cache persistence for that group, before connection replacement. This does not promise a transaction across unrelated proxy groups or arbitrary external effects. Broader verification is pending at this checkpoint.
- Verification/status: locally fixed. Package/build/lint/type checks pass with the TC-107 matrix. Each exact Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 reproduces two real filesystem failures and verifies that cold discovery leaves no generated commands and warm discovery retains the original command object/cache. The two existing-SDK calls and two CLI inspection-command outputs per runtime remain healthy. These permission-error probes run as the ordinary local macOS user; they are not claims about Windows filesystem behavior. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-cache-native.log`; inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-cache-after.png` and transcript `/tmp/toolcraft-session109-cache-after-transcript.log`. Full snapshot 108 predates this fix.

### TC-109: completed proxy CLI commands retain live upstream processes

- Surface: one-shot CLI calls through stdio MCP proxies. `runCLI` resolves and renders the result, but the hot proxy connection is not disposed. The unused global disposer set has no shutdown consumer. A live child and its pipes keep the completed CLI process alive; existing integration fixtures' explicit child cleanup or `process.exit` conceal this lifetime boundary.
- Public baseline: actual Node 22.23.2 CLI subprocesses invoke a synthetic stdio upstream without an explicit exit. Successful and protocol-error tool calls print their expected output and completion marker, but remain alive after a bounded 500 ms observation with one live upstream each. Plain commands, proxy help, local commands with a configured proxy, and invalid arguments exit naturally with expected statuses. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-lifecycle-public-before.log`, six cases: two known defects and four healthy controls. Only owned fixture children are terminated afterward.
- TDD: thirteen new cases cover CLI success, protocol/thrown errors, cleanup failures without output loss, repeated calls, independent SDK ownership, failed-resolution preservation, empty roots, explicit reuse/reconnection, pending connection establishment, concurrent disposal, and all-settled close errors. Five helper regressions fail before the new export. The initial CLI fixture incorrectly hyphenates the literal `create_issue` command, so those failures are not counted as lifecycle evidence. With the argv corrected and CLI cleanup assignment temporarily absent, seven CLI regressions fail at actual close/reconnection assertions and seven controls pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-lifecycle-red.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-lifecycle-green.log`, and `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-cli-red-corrected.log` preserve this distinction.
- Repair: export `disposeMcpProxies(root)` from `toolcraft/mcp-proxy`; visit only that root's proxy groups, share pending closes, wait for pending connection establishment, close all groups despite individual failures, and retain failures in `AggregateError`. Remove the unused global strong-reference set. `runCLI` acquires cleanup responsibility after successful proxy resolution and disposes in `finally`. Cleanup errors set exit status 1 and add a stderr diagnostic rather than replacing the earlier result or failure. Failed resolution leaves pre-existing working connections alone.
- Ownership boundary: SDK and MCP integrations remain reusable until their owner disposes the root. Separate root instances retain independent lifetimes; sharing one mutable root also shares connections. Disposal permits later reconnection and is not cancellation or request draining. The underlying stdio transport initiates termination rather than guaranteeing synchronous PID disappearance. The first SDK public fixture incorrectly assumes an immediate missing PID; the corrected fixture observes termination within a bounded 500 ms window. The failed draft is retained in `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-sdk-native-draft.log`, not counted as another product issue.
- Verification: focused five-file suite passes 301 tests; complete Toolcraft suite passes 101 files / 6,820 tests. Selected 19-workspace closure build and package lint/types pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-lifecycle-green-corrected.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-{build,package,lint}.log`. Snapshot 111 subsequently passes the expanded published-SDK smoke, verifying the new subpath export and `Promise<void>` result, plus the bundle, standalone, and entrypoint smokes. Its single native committed-lock qualification failure prevents claiming a full pass. Non-README lifetime guidance is in `docs/toolcraft-results.md`.
- Exact-runtime verification: Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 each pass all six real CLI subprocess cases: eighteen natural exits, expected success/failure statuses, and no remaining owned upstream processes. SDK controls additionally verify hot reuse, independent roots, concurrent disposal, reconnection, and repeated disposal: fifteen calls across nine hot connections, with all fifteen discovery/hot upstream processes terminated and all three parent processes exiting naturally. Evidence: `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-lifecycle-native.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session110-sdk-native.log`.
- Visual verification: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-lifecycle-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-proxy-lifecycle-after.png`, rendered from actual successful/error CLI transcripts. Completion markers and bracketed process observations are labeled fixture diagnostics, not product output. The result/error payloads are preserved while observations change from completed-but-alive to natural exit. Full snapshot 111 covers this repair and passes build, all four smokes, shared tests, repository/package lint, and root help/types, but exits 1 on the recorded committed-lock qualification. No new full pass is claimed.

### TC-110: CLI union selector fingerprints collide across distinct branches

- Surface: ordinary and streaming CLI commands with `S.Union` parameters. Source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `getRequiredBranchFingerprint`, union collection in `collectFields`, and branch selection/inactive-argument checks. Distinct required-key arrays are reduced to a plus-joined display string and then used as branch identity.
- Reproduce: define one object branch requiring the literal key `first+second` and another requiring separate `first` / `second` keys. These are valid, distinct schema branches with disjoint CLI option paths. Both receive selector ID `first+second`. An actual `audit read --payload-kind first+second --payload.first one --payload.second two --yes --output json` call exits one, reports missing `payload.first+second`, and never invokes the handler. The second branch is otherwise callable through SDK and MCP.
- Mixed-input reproduction: supply the literal-key flag as well as both separate-key flags with that selector. CLI exits zero and invokes the handler with all three properties. Shared validation rejects that payload against the declared union because neither branch allows the other's fields. This is not only ambiguous help text: the identity collision changes executable branch membership.
- Cause: selection uses the first matching branch ID; the inactive-argument loop skips every branch whose ID equals that selection. Different branches with the same display fingerprint therefore share identity. Required-field checking still follows the first branch, making the second unreachable while allowing mixed fields to bypass the inactive-branch check.
- Expected: every valid branch has an unambiguous usable selector; selecting a branch validates its own required fields and rejects fields from other branches. Preserve existing unambiguous selector values and the working first-branch path when introducing disambiguation. This is separate from TC-068's duplicate-option registration problem: these branches have disjoint option names and register successfully.
- Controls: the literal-key branch works; ordinary non-colliding left/right branches remain selectable; their mixed arguments reject before handlers. An inactive default on the second colliding branch is not injected into a first-branch call. Both colliding branches are valid and callable through SDK plus snake/camel MCP. Shared schema matching remains correct, consistent with session 115's source-only control.
- Exact-runtime evidence: Node 18.18.0, 20.0.0, and 22.23.2 run both supported CLI casing modes (kebab/snake), ordinary/streaming commands, and seven scenarios each. The 84 CLI invocations include twelve unreachable-second-branch failures, twelve invalid mixed-payload admissions and sixty healthy controls. Eighteen additional SDK/MCP calls validate both branches. Outputs, exit statuses, handler counts and shared-schema validity are asserted. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session118-cli-selector-<version>-before-supported.log`.
- Probe corrections: the first draft predicts an inactive-default leak, but its healthy runtime result disproves that prediction. The next draft incorrectly treats camel as a supported CLI casing; the public type only allows kebab/snake, and its camel-style flag is rejected as unknown. These are probe errors, not new product issues. Retained logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session118-cli-selector-22.23.2-before.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session118-cli-selector-<version>-before-corrected.log`. Only the corrected supported-mode matrix establishes the finding.
- Visual evidence: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-union-selector-before.png`, rendered from actual CLI calls. It shows the working first branch, unreachable second branch, admitted mixed payload and healthy inactive-default control. Bracketed handler/validity observations are fixture diagnostics, not product output.
- Repair (session 119): preserve the first existing fingerprint and add ` (branch N)` to later collisions, where N is the one-based declaration position. Each selector remains scoped to its union, and existing non-colliding selectors are unchanged. Adds `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-union-selectors.test.ts` with 76 cases across both CLI casings and normal/streaming commands, including three-way collisions, nested selectors, dynamic members, required fields, defaults, mixed-field rejection, interactive choices and human help. The non-README guide `docs/toolcraft-cli.md` documents quoting and compatibility.
- TDD evidence: the corrected before run has 44 failing cases and 32 healthy controls; all 76 pass after the patch. Combined focused variants/snapshot coverage passes 184 tests. The initial help fixture incorrectly expects `outputEmitter` to receive help and snapshot metadata to expose enum values; those assumptions are corrected before rerunning the genuine red/green pair. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session119-selectors-red-corrected.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session119-selectors-green-corrected.log`. Enum metadata omission remains a separate unclassified DX lead, not an extra claimed fix.
- After verification: all 102 supported-runtime CLI/SDK/MCP invocations pass repaired/control assertions. Six actual PTY flows select the second branch and naturally exit zero across Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2, normal and streaming. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session119-cli-selector-<version>-after.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session119-interactive-results.log`. Inspected actual after transcript and normal/streaming menus: `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-union-selector-after.png`, `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-union-selector-menu.png`, and `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-union-selector-stream-menu.png`.
- Maintained checks: selected Toolcraft closure passes 19 builds from the 71-workspace/210-edge graph; Toolcraft 104 files / 7,270 tests and OpenAPI 36 files / 1,280 tests pass. Terminal 79702 exits zero after the maintained uncached root-help rebuild, seventeen package-lint rules, root types and Toolcraft lint/types. Root help is inspected. Original combined handle 86253 becomes unavailable after truncated output; completed build/test logs remain, and lint is independently established by terminal 79702 rather than guessing a lost terminal status.
- Status: locally fixed with focused, native and visual evidence. Broader snapshot 122 includes this patch: build, all four smokes, shared tests, recorded native controls/tails, lint/package checks and root help/types pass, but its native committed-lock qualification fails. No new wholly successful full snapshot is claimed. No external service, LLM, security investigation, README edit, commit, push or release is involved.

### TC-111: structured CLI help omits accepted enum and selector values

- Surfaces: command `--help --output json` and the public `createCLICommandTreeSnapshot` API. Sources: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `formatJsonHelpOption`, `createFieldSnapshotOption`, and built-in snapshot controls. Before the fix, parameter metadata identifies an enum but gives no accepted values; only the output-format control exposes `choices`. Debug and log-level controls omit their accepted tokens too.
- Reproduce: define a required region enum with nine values, plus numeric/boolean enums and a union selector. Both metadata APIs return records such as `{ "name": "region", "type": "enum", "required": true }` with flags but no choices. Explicit valid argv succeeds, but a consumer of either discovery format cannot obtain an allowed region or selector value from its structured record. Long enums are especially inconvenient because human help intentionally omits their long value list. The selector case exposes the same metadata omission, not another TC-110 identity defect.
- Current-built evidence: Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2 each exercise kebab/snake and ordinary/streaming commands through both discovery formats. Twenty-four metadata observations confirm missing values, while twenty-four explicit invocations confirm the declared choices work. Help does not invoke handlers or option loaders; existing output-format choices are a healthy control. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-help-<version>-before.log`.
- Repair: emit owned string-valued `choices` arrays for enum fields in both formats, using the same formatting function. Numeric/boolean choices use accepted argv strings, nullable enums include the accepted `null` token without adding a duplicate, and labels do not replace actual values. Synthetic union/oneOf selectors inherit the same behavior. Snapshot debug/log-level controls now expose accepted modes, with log levels derived from the existing runtime declaration. No parsing or prompt behavior changes; dynamic option loaders remain uncalled.
- TDD: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-enum-metadata.test.ts` adds 156 cases: both formats, both casings, ordinary/streaming commands, sixteen enum shapes, isolation/default/positional/non-enum controls, and built-in enum controls. Corrected red: 140 failures / sixteen controls. Green: all 156 pass; combined selector/snapshot/help-isolation coverage passes 258. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-metadata-red-corrected.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-metadata-green.log`.
- Scope correction: the first red suite additionally expects CLI-excluded fields to disappear. Eight failures reconfirm existing TC-060 rather than this metadata omission; those unrelated assertions are removed before the authoritative red/green cycle. Original `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-metadata-red.log` remains. TC-060 is not claimed fixed. Before the after-probe, source inspection also corrects the expected log-level list to include supported `silent`; no runtime failure is hidden by that correction.
- After evidence: the same twenty-four metadata-driven invocations now obtain region and selector values from discovery and pass exact handler assertions. A separate matrix round-trips every advertised token from seven representative enums plus debug/log-level controls: 104 calls per exact runtime, 312 total, covering negative numbers, false, mixed values, nullable/literal-null behavior and spaced values. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-help-<version>-after.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-tokens-<version>.log`.
- Visual verification: inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-enum-help-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-enum-help-after.png`. They are explicitly labeled excerpts of the first three option records from actual captured CLI JSON help, not invented product output. Full transcripts remain under `/tmp/toolcraft-session120-enum-help-{before,after}-transcript.log`. Fresh maintained root help is also inspected.
- Maintained checks: terminal 78260 exits zero after the selected nineteen-workspace build closure, Toolcraft 105 files / 7,426 tests, OpenAPI 36 files / 1,280 tests, and Toolcraft lint/types. Terminal 32263 exits zero after seventy uncached maintained root-help build tasks, all seventeen package-lint rules, and root types. The non-README CLI guide documents discovery semantics and remaining limitations.
- Status: locally fixed with regression, native and visual evidence. Broader snapshot 122 includes this patch and all source changes through session 120; its sole native committed-lock qualification failure remains explicit. No new wholly successful full snapshot, security work, README edit, commit, push or release is claimed.

### TC-112: harness CLI results are not actual stdout and stderr
