- Surface: `createCommandTestHarness(...).cli()`, specifically `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/harness.ts`. The public result has stdout/stderr fields, and an existing custom-formatter test promises exact bytes, but the implementation concatenates result-emitter messages and hardcodes stderr to an empty string.
- Reproduce: compare a direct `runCLI` invocation with the same fresh definition through the harness. JSON help escapes to process stdout while returned stdout is empty; ordinary JSON/Markdown results lose their final newline; a JSON UserError becomes its raw message; NDJSON events become adjacent JSON objects without separators. Runtime diagnostics and handler console output escape capture, with returned stderr still empty. Exit codes agree in these sequential fixtures, and custom formatter bytes are a healthy control.
- Current-built evidence: twelve scenarios on exact Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2, each using a direct and a harness call: 72 invocations, 33 mismatching comparisons and three custom-formatter controls. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-harness-capture-<version>-before.log`. The same matrix after the independent TC-113 repair retains all 33 mismatches and three controls: `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-harness-capture-<version>-current.log`.
- Inspected visual evidence: `screenshots/toolcraft-harness-capture-before.png`, rendered from actual comparison records for custom output, JSON errors, diagnostics and streams; source excerpt `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-harness-capture-visual.log`. JSON string escaping makes missing line separators and empty capture channels visible rather than normalizing them away.
- Impact: tests can assert output that the real CLI never emits, miss diagnostic failures, or pollute their runner's streams. This is separate from TC-047's deliberate message-emitter contract. Repair needs owned byte-stream capture without changing emitter semantics or mixing unrelated/concurrent process writes. A global stream monkeypatch without ownership is not treated as a sufficient fix.
- Status: confirmed, open; no harness production patch. The non-README CLI guide describes the limitation and independent actual-CLI capture workaround. Concurrent process exit-code isolation remains unverified and is not inferred from sequential controls.

### TC-113: nested and overlapping CLI calls borrow the wrong output format

- Surfaces: normal and streaming `runCLI`, progress/error rendering, and early invocation errors. The CLI's local `withOutputFormat` helper temporarily rewrites global `process.env.OUTPUT_FORMAT`, while the design system already uses an asynchronous output-format context. An outer design scope therefore overrides an explicit CLI selection; overlapping invocations borrow each other's format and can restore the environment in the wrong order.
- Current-built reproduction on exact Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2: an outer Markdown context plus CLI JSON output renders Markdown progress and errors, unlike the same CLI invocation alone. In a gated JSON/Markdown overlap, the JSON command emits Markdown progress, the Markdown command emits rich progress, and the original undefined OUTPUT_FORMAT leaks as json after both complete. Same-format outer contexts are controls. Fourteen calls per runtime, 42 total; logs `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-output-context-<version>-before.log`.
- TDD: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-output-context.test.ts` adds 84 cases for nested normal/streaming success/errors, ordered overlaps, unchanged parent environments and early errors. Corrected red is 61 failures / 23 controls, before production changes; an initial table-shape mistake is corrected in the authoritative `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-output-context-red-corrected.log`. The four-file focused green passes 242 tests. Tests use observed standalone baselines, not hand-written output assumptions.
- Repair: import the existing public design-system `withOutputFormat` in `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, map CLI formats through the existing mapper at all five call sites, and remove the duplicate global-environment helper. No new wrapper, design-system production change or configuration option is added. The caller's environment and outer context remain unchanged during and after execution.
- Compatibility: handlers must use public `resolveOutputFormat()` for the active design format instead of observing a temporary process-environment mutation; child-process environments require explicit settings. This is documented in `docs/toolcraft-cli.md`. Formatting isolation does not claim independent process exit status or arbitrary command side effects.
- Existing-test adaptation: the first package run exposes five full design-module mocks missing the newly used export, plus three tests asserting the superseded global-environment implementation. Those mocks now extend the actual module; format observations use the public resolver, while unchanged-parent-environment assertions remain. The first run's 208 failures / 7,302 passes / one unhandled error are preserved in `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-toolcraft.log`, not dismissed as pre-existing failures. Corrected six-file coverage passes 307 tests.
- Native after-checks: all three runtimes pass the 42-call original matrix after correcting its expected overlap bytes to retain ordinary null-result acknowledgments (JSON ok and Markdown Done). The initial after-probe's missing-acknowledgment assumption is retained in its logs; actual before output and the independent baseline-based red tests already establish the defect. Corrected logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-output-context-<version>-after-corrected.log`. Another 216 native stream calls pass across nested scopes, overlaps, normal completion and UserErrors thrown after an emitted event: `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-output-context-stream-<version>.log`.
- Inspected screenshots: `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-output-context-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-cli-output-context-after.png`, rendered from actual before/after transcripts, show corrected format routing and unchanged environment. Maintained root help is freshly rebuilt and inspected too.
- Maintained verification: selected nineteen-workspace build succeeds; Toolcraft 106 files / 7,510 tests, design 81 files / 1,712 tests, OpenAPI 36 files / 1,280 tests, and Toolcraft lint/types pass. The attempted design test:unit command is unavailable, not a pass; its maintained npm test route is run successfully instead. Terminal 93494 finishes the corrected design/OpenAPI/lint chain with zero. Terminal 12886 exits zero after seventy uncached root-help builds, seventeen package-lint rules and root types; logs `/tmp/toolcraft-session123-final.dU8QvE`.
- Status: locally fixed with regression, native and visual evidence. Broader snapshot 124 includes this patch; its sole native committed-lock qualification fails while the other maintained checks pass. No new wholly successful full snapshot or remote delivery is claimed.

### TC-114: CLI test harness drops the configured API version

- Surface: `createCommandTestHarness(root, { apiVersion }).cli(...)`. `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/harness.ts` forwards the option for run, stream and parity, but omits it from the CLI invocation. A valid configured version therefore appears missing; older and malformed versions produce the wrong missing-version diagnostic too.
- Reproduce: declare `requires: { apiVersion: ">=1.2.3" }` on a command or its group, configure harness apiVersion 1.2.3, and invoke its CLI method. Direct CLI with the same option and harness run/stream succeed, but harness CLI exits one with no runner API version provided and never calls the handler. This is an option-forwarding defect, separate from TC-112's process-output capture limitation.
- Before evidence: thirty configurations per exact Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2, each through direct CLI, harness run/stream and harness CLI: 270 calls total. Ordinary/streaming commands, direct/inherited/no requirements, and missing/older/minimum/newer/malformed versions are covered. Across runtimes, 24 valid CLI calls are rejected and 24 invalid-version calls report the wrong reason. Missing-version and unversioned-command controls agree. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-harness-version-<version>-before.log`.
- TDD: `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/harness-cli-options.test.ts` adds thirty cases, each comparing the CLI result with the existing run/stream control and checking handler counts. Red is sixteen failures / fourteen controls; green with existing harness/runtime-option tests passes three files / 68 tests. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-harness-version-red.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-harness-version-green.log`.
- Repair: one existing-options forwarding line, `apiVersion: options.apiVersion`, in the CLI adapter call. No new option, wrapper, version parser, requirement policy or output-capture behavior is introduced. The non-README CLI guide documents parity with the other harness entrypoints.
- After evidence: the same 270 public calls pass, including exact handler admission counts and matching exit statuses. Separately comparing the 36 captured native error messages against direct CLI JSON messages yields zero mismatches; `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-native-diagnostic-comparisons.log`. Raw harness messages are still not complete process stdout (TC-112 remains open).
- Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-harness-api-version-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-harness-api-version-after.png`, rendered from actual harness results and labeled direct-versus-harness exit codes. The healthy unversioned control remains. Saved transcripts: `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-harness-version-{before,after}-transcript.log`. Fresh maintained root help is also inspected.
- Maintained checks: terminal 82130 exits zero after the selected nineteen-workspace build closure, Toolcraft 107 files / 7,540 tests, and package lint/types. Terminal 98813 exits zero after seventy uncached root-help builds, seventeen package-lint rules, root types and the tracked-source whitespace check. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-build.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-toolcraft.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-lint.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-help.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-packages.log`, and `/tmp/toolcraft-session125-types.log`.
- Status: locally fixed with regression, native and visual evidence. Broader snapshot 127 covers this source patch with its recorded sole native qualification failure; snapshot 79 remains the latest wholly successful full check. No remote delivery is claimed.

### TC-115: codemode runtime stacks repeat multiline error text

- Surface: `execute` runtime errors, first observed while reproducing TC-025's multi-parameter diagnostics. `error.message` is correct, but `error.stack` repeats every continuation line before the actual call frames. A host error with message one/newline/two becomes Error: one, two, two, then the call frame. Repeated validation bullets make the diagnostic harder to read without representing additional failures.
- Root cause: `packages/safe-js/src/interp/exceptions.ts` reconstructs a surfaced error with its full message while `readSandboxStackFrames` removes only the first line of the existing header. The remaining message lines are mistaken for frames. Codemode merely returns this shared runtime result; a Toolcraft-specific stack rewrite would mask the cause.
- Before public evidence: exact Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2 each run 96 actual codemode executions, covering Error/TypeError, synchronous/asynchronous handlers, direct/function/rethrow/nested-rethrow scripts, and six message shapes. Across 288 calls, 240 stacks differ from a healthy single-line frame baseline; 48 single-line controls agree. Blank/trailing lines, CRLF and message text resembling a frame are included. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session126-multiline-errors-<version>-before.log`.
- TDD: `packages/safe-js/src/error/multiline-stack.test.ts` adds 96 core cases; `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/multiline-errors.test.ts` adds 24 adapter cases. Both are written before the runtime patch. Red is 100 failures / twenty controls; green with existing runtime/error-format/execute checks passes six files / 219 tests. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session126-multiline-red.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session126-multiline-green.log`.
- Repair: recognize and remove the complete existing name/message header before reusing its frames. A header-only stack yields no frames; a different custom header retains the existing first-line fallback. The message, constructor name, call-frame order and caller-visible execution outcome are not rewritten. No filtering or runtime-policy change is introduced.
- After evidence: the same 288 native calls pass. Every repaired stack also equals the expected string derived from its stored pre-fix single-line baseline, not merely a newly computed baseline; message values and handler counts remain exact. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session126-multiline-errors-<version>-after.log`.
- Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-codemode-multiline-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-codemode-multiline-after.png`, rendered from actual returned stacks with labeled inputs. Single-line and nested-call controls keep the same frames. Saved transcripts: `/tmp/toolcraft-session126-multiline-{before,after}-transcript.log`.
- Maintained focused verification: selected thirty-workspace build passes; SafeJS 269 files / 10,283 tests pass with one file / 41 tests skipped, and codemode twelve files / 211 tests pass. Codemode lint/types and focused runtime-source/test ESLint pass (18492 and 72382 exit zero). The non-README codemode guide describes stack presentation and the independent open discovery limitation.
- Status: locally fixed with regression, public-runtime and visual evidence. Broader snapshot 127 covers this source patch; its sole committed-lock qualification fails while other maintained checks pass. No newer wholly successful full check or remote delivery is claimed. TC-025 remains open and is independently rechecked after the repair.

### TC-116: dynamic CLI diagnostics contradict their own supported-type list

- Surface: CLI error presentation; source: `packages/toolcraft/src/cli.ts`, `formatUnsupportedDynamicSchemaMessage`, shared by dynamic-leaf resolution and dynamic-value finalization.
- Reproduction: declare `entries: S.Record(S.Object({ name: S.String() }))` and pass `--entries.alpha '{}'`. The invocation correctly rejects a parent value where indexed/dotted leaf flags are required, but says `Unsupported parameter type "object" for "entries.alpha". Supported types: string, number, integer, boolean, array, object, enum, oneof.` A oneOf-valued record similarly rejects `oneof` while listing it as supported. The same fixed list omits supported JSON leaves and does not express the distinction between a schema type and its argument position.
- Expected: identify the unsupported CLI argument shape and parameter path without claiming that the very same usage is supported. Do not suggest new parsing support or change accepted inputs just to improve the message.
- Cause: both call sites use an unconditional generic list, although dynamic leaf arguments and recursively reconstructed objects have different accepted shapes.
- Local repair: replaces that list with `Unsupported CLI argument shape for "entries.alpha" (type "object").` The existing usage pointer, path, kind, user-error routing, exit status and handler suppression remain intact. No parser behavior, dynamic variant support, preset handling or parity serialization changes.
- TDD: new `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-dynamic-shape-diagnostics.test.ts` first produces 32 failed assertions and twenty passing controls. The corrected diagnostic and one existing expected-message assertion then pass all 357 cases in three focused suites, including existing CLI and dynamic-preset tests. New cases cover object/record/oneOf/union types, optional wrappers, nesting and normal/streaming commands; controls exercise nested object/record flags and all six supported leaf families, including JSON. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostic-red.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostic-green.log`.
- Independent native evidence before and after rebuilding: 156 actual CLI invocations per snapshot across Node 18.18.0 / 20.0.0 / 22.23.2, comprising 96 rejected-shape cases and sixty successful controls. Every rejection has exit one, no handler call and empty stderr; successful values reach the handler unchanged. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-18.18.0-before.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-20.0.0-before.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-22.23.2-before.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-18.18.0-after.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-20.0.0-after.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostics-22.23.2-after.log`.
- Maintained focused verification: selected Toolcraft build closure passes with nineteen builds, 71 declared workspaces, 210 edges and eleven layers; Toolcraft's 108 files / 7,592 tests and package lint/types pass. Logs: `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-build.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-toolcraft.log`, `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-lint.log`. Inspected `screenshots/toolcraft-dynamic-diagnostic-before.png` and `screenshots/toolcraft-dynamic-diagnostic-after.png`, rendered from `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostic-before-transcript.log` and `/tmp/toolcraft-session130-dynamic-diagnostic-after-transcript.log`, show actual process-output messages and successful controls. Broader snapshot 131 is in progress; no full-pass claim is made.

## Regression entry points

These current test files provide direct entry points for functional repairs. The finding narratives and work log retain the original red/green evidence; this index is not an additional pass count, an exhaustive list of tests, or a claim that still-open findings are covered by a repair. Shared files intentionally cover more than one issue.

| Findings | Current regression files | Checked behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TC-002, TC-003, TC-005 | `packages/toolcraft/src/discriminator-validation.test.ts` | Missing/unknown/non-string discriminators reject before handlers; explicitly declared branch names and caller-cased discriminators normalize correctly across normal/streaming SDK and MCP. |
| TC-004 | `packages/toolcraft/src/union-validation.test.ts` | Exact-one branch matching includes constraints, scope-collapsed ambiguity, caller casing and defaults from only the successful branch. |
| TC-006, TC-040 | `packages/toolcraft/src/default-isolation.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/default-cloning.test.ts` | Repeated calls receive independent mutable containers while canonical values, callable extras and supported opaque resources retain their contracts. |
| TC-007 | `packages/toolcraft/src/optional-validation.test.ts` | Absent/explicit-undefined optionals, falsy defaults, array items, nested wrappers, nullable controls and required-value rejection. |
| TC-008 | `packages/toolcraft/src/sparse-arrays.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/sparse-arrays.test.ts` | Indexed holes, optional/defaulted items, nested JSON arrays and handler/transport suppression for invalid values. |
| TC-012 | `packages/toolcraft/src/nonplain-values.test.ts` | Unsupported non-plain input objects reject before handlers across the tested schema/container placements. |
| TC-013 | `packages/toolcraft/src/sdk-validation.test.ts` | Bigint and other invalid primitive inputs produce useful diagnostics rather than failing during error formatting. |
| TC-024 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/host-modules.test.ts` | Root/subgroup module-export collisions reject in either declaration order before either handler executes. |
| TC-026 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/search-identifiers.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/search.test.ts` | Identifier components and tested Unicode terms remain searchable without running handlers; ranking, punctuation, limits and detail controls remain intact. |
| TC-027 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/stream-catalog.test.ts` | SDK-visible stream catalogs reject consistently across discovery/execution before producers run; SDK-excluded streams do not break ordinary commands. |
| TC-028 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/execute.test.ts` | Script console output uses diagnostics rather than process console methods, while an explicitly supplied sink retains ownership. |
| TC-039 | `packages/toolcraft/src/applied-default-validation.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/validation-no-defaults.test.ts` | Applied defaults are validated without inserting additional defaults or changing the original canonical payload. |
| TC-089 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/catalog-lifecycle.test.ts` | An unused failed catalog does not emit an unhandled rejection; delayed consumers receive the original failure without resolving again or starting handlers. |
| TC-098 | `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/schema-default-isolation.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-schema/src/schema-default-isolation.test.ts` | Mutating returned schema defaults does not change the definition, subsequent discovery or executed defaults. |
| TC-110, TC-111 | `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-union-selectors.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-enum-metadata.test.ts` | Colliding selector identities remain independently reachable, and structured discovery exposes owned accepted-token arrays. |
| TC-113 | `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-output-context.test.ts` | Nested/overlapping normal and streaming calls keep their selected format and leave the caller environment unchanged. |
| TC-114 | `packages/toolcraft/src/testing/harness-cli-options.test.ts` | Harness CLI forwards API versions consistently with run/stream, including inherited and unversioned controls. |
| TC-115 | `packages/safe-js/src/error/multiline-stack.test.ts`; `packages/toolcraft-codemode/src/multiline-errors.test.ts` | Multiline messages appear once in stacks while names, original messages and actual call frames are preserved. |
| TC-116 | `packages/toolcraft/src/cli-dynamic-shape-diagnostics.test.ts` | Rejected dynamic shapes have noncontradictory diagnostics; nested object/record flags and supported scalar/JSON/array leaves retain their behavior. |

## Working remediation order

This order is provisional while coverage is incomplete; it separates remaining work from the local fixes already listed above.

1. Preserve session 116's applied-default validation repair (TC-039), alongside fixed nested-member admission, dot-segment handling and exact-one/discriminator controls. These are regression-preservation checks, not newly open defects.
2. Restore execution/discovery consistency: codemode parameter schemas (TC-025), CLI scope/requiredness, shared variant fields, ordinary object containers (TC-060, TC-068, TC-096), and parity/harness input, filesystem and output fidelity (TC-014, TC-070, TC-112). Preserve the explicit unsupported-stream diagnostic (TC-027), isolated output contexts (TC-113), repaired dynamic presets (TC-097), exhausted MCP scope projection (TC-041), and retry-interruption controls. Implementing codemode streaming is not required to preserve its repaired rejection contract.
3. Preserve repaired base-URL inference and source/format metadata handling (TC-093), retaining fixed path-server precedence (TC-094). Preserve repaired supplied non-plain-value rejection (TC-012), sparse-array and optional-undefined controls. Focus on functional input fidelity and useful error presentation; further credential-format or security-hardening investigation is excluded by the user's scope clarification.
4. Improve onboarding and discoverability: screenshot glyph coverage (TC-095) and documented schema examples/configuration (TC-009, TC-010, TC-082). Preserve repaired command search (TC-026), maintained fixture dependency (TC-015), screenshot bootstrap (TC-016), parser context (TC-037), and scalar-error presentation (TC-057). README edits remain gated on permission.

Each follow-up should begin with the narrow failing reproduction, preserve existing supported behavior with controls, and use maintained checks appropriate to its actual cross-package impact. An issue marked locally fixed is not a release claim.

### Ruled-out leads and execution caveats

- An ordinary invalid numeric default is rejected at schema construction: `S.Number({ default: -1, minimum: 0 })` throws `default must satisfy schema: Expected number greater than or equal to 0 at value, got -1`. Therefore the broad claim that SDK builders admit invalid default constraints is not supported. Mutation of a previously valid shared default is a separate question.
- Do not probe built package imports concurrently with a build that deletes/recreates `dist`: one follow-up probe encountered a transient missing `tiny-mcp-client/dist/index.js` while the screenshot preparation was rebuilding dependencies. After the build completed, probes ran successfully. The transient observation is not counted as a product bug.
- The two local code fixes affect SDK input validation and diagnostics, not CLI rendering. The screenshot is additional audit coverage, not evidence for those SDK contracts.

- Implemented shared boundary: replace automatic envelope-shape detection with an explicit, non-enumerable MCP-result marker. Proxy handlers know their upstream origin and mark protocol envelopes automatically; ordinary domain objects remain untouched regardless of field names or declared schemas. Public `asMCPResult` lets direct integrations opt in without importing proxy runtime dependencies.
- Preserve upstream error envelopes before validating successful typed results. Typed SDK commands should reject with the upstream message and original envelope as the error cause; untyped proxy SDK callers retain their existing raw-envelope contract. Downstream MCP should forward the protocol error rather than serialize it as successful text. Successful typed results still undergo the existing schema/casing checks.
- Custom renderers should receive the actual handler return value, consistent with their public `Renderers<TResult>` type. Automatic CLI rendering alone extracts explicitly marked protocol payloads; error envelopes still route to stderr and exit 1. This avoids introducing a new callback type mismatch while removing the old ordinary-data ambiguity. Genuine direct MCP integrations must opt in explicitly, and custom renderers can access `structuredContent` themselves; this migration belongs in a non-README guide and regression tests.
- TDD covers unmarked domain values, explicit envelope tagging without source mutation, text and structured upstream errors, success-schema bypass only for protocol errors, successful output validation/casing, and custom-renderer identity. Subsequent public checks cover real proxy SDK/CLI/MCP behavior. No security work is included.
- Implementation now replaces shape detection with `asMCPResult`, auto-tags proxy envelopes, preserves upstream errors in MCP, rejects typed SDK protocol failures with the original envelope as cause, and gives custom renderers the actual handler value. A new non-README guide explains direct-integration migration and the untyped SDK contract. Empty marked CLI failures now say `Upstream tool failed.` rather than `Done.`.

## Completion gates

- Finish the requested duration with honest work accounting.
- Investigate every coverage area; state any inaccessible or unverified cases explicitly.
- Consolidate duplicate causes while preserving distinct user symptoms and reproductions.
- Separate confirmed open issues, locally fixed issues, and unconfirmed leads.
- Verify each fix with regression tests and relevant maintained checks; inspect screenshots for visual changes.
- Review this document against the final worktree and evidence, not earlier assumptions.
- Report local changes, commits, remote delivery, and releases separately. None of the latter three is implied by a local fix.
